#### Licenses Configuration

Licenses properties cannot be set by SET ENVIRONMENT within the program. They must appear in the external configuration.

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.compiler.license.2026 | This property specifies the license code for the isCOBOL Compiler. |
| iscobol.easydb.license.2026 | This property specifies the license code for isCOBOL DatabaseBridge. |
| iscobol.eis.license.2026 | This property specifies the license code for isCOBOL EIS. |
| iscobol.license.2026 | This property specifies the isCOBOL license code. This license activates runtime and debugging features and controls how many concurrent users can connect to the Application Server. |
| iscobol.udbc.license.2026 | This property specifies the license code for isCOBOL UDBC. |

#### Compiler Configuration

Unlike Framework properties, Compiler properties cannot be set by SET ENVIRONMENT within the program. Most of them can be set within the program using [SET Directive](../Compiler/Compiler-Directives/SET-Directive) if stripped of the "iscobol.compiler" prefix.

(\*) The asterisk after the property name means that the property can’t be set using [SET Directive](../Compiler/Compiler-Directives/SET-Directive), but can only appear in the external configuration.

| Property | Meaning |
| :--- | :--- |
| iscobol.compiler.command_line_linkage (boolean) | **True** = command line parameters are moved to the Linkage Section<br>**False** = command line parameters can’t be intercepted via Linkage Section. <br><br>When set to true, the command line is passed to the main program using the following parameter structure:           <br><br>LINKAGE SECTION.           <br>01 CmdLine.              <br>03 CmdLineLen   pic 9(4)comp.              <br>03 CmdLineData  pic x(256). <br><br>The maximum allowed command line length is 2048 characters. Longer command line values will be truncated. <br><br>The default vale is False. |
| iscobol.compiler.const.*ConstantName* | Sets the value for a constant that can be used by the compiler directives.<br>*ConstantName* is case insensitive but, since this is a configuration property, it’s good practice to use lower case text. <br><br>The characters in the value define the constant type. A value of characters between 0 and 9 (included) defines a numeric constant. A value that includes any other character defines an alphanumeric constant. <br><br>Refer to the [Compiler Directives](../Compiler/Compiler-Directives/Compiler-Directives) section for further details. |
| iscobol.compiler.custompreproc * | Specifies one or more preprocessor programs.<br>Program names must be separated by space.<br>See [Preprocessor programs](../Compiler/Source-code-preprocessing/Preprocessor-programs) for more information about preprocessors. |
| iscobol.compiler.debug.replaced_source (boolean) | **True** = Debugger shows the code changes applied by [iscobol.compiler.custompreproc](./Configuration-Properties#compiler-configuration) \* and [iscobol.compiler.regexp \*](#compiler_regexp), unless the source code is loaded externally.<br>**False** = Debugger always shows the original code. <br><br>Note - when this property is set to *True*, the error line numbers shown by the Compiler refer to the altered source, not to the original source. This can make it more difficult to spot the problematic code in your code editor. <br><br>If omitted, *True* is assumed. |
| iscobol.compiler.efd_field_name_num (boolean) <span id="compiler_efd_field_name_num"></span> | **True** = field names that begin with a number are registered as they are in the EFD/ISS dictionaries.<br>**False** = field names that begin with a number are registered with a leading underscore in the EFD/ISS dictionaries. <br><br>This option affects the generation of EFD/ISS dictionaries as well as the generation of EDBI routines performed when [iscobol.compiler.easydb (boolean)](#compiler_easydb) is set to true. <br><br>If omitted, *False* is assumed. |
| iscobol.compiler.esql.array.*TypeName* <span id="compiler_esql_array_TypeName"></span> | This property specifies the field type within a ARRAY SQL type.<br>It’s used in conjunction with [iscobol.compiler.esql.procedure.ProcedureName](#compiler_esql_procedure_ProcedureName) or the [HOSTVAR Directive](../../../Language-Reference/SQL-Directives/HOSTVAR-Directive) when they define parameters with *dbtype*=ARRAY. <br><br>*TypeName* must be written in lower case regardless of the case it has on the database. <br><br>For example, given the following procedure, that returns an array of varchar fields: <br><br>create or replace package MYPKG<br>IS<br>TYPE Tbl_TYPE<br>IS<br>TABLE OF VARCHAR(8)<br>INDEX BY BINARY_INTEGER; <br><br>PROCEDURE GET(P1 OUT Tbl_TYPE);<br>END MYPKG; <br><br>You would set: <br><br>iscobol.compiler.esql.procedure.mypkg.get=o,array,tbl_type<br>iscobol.compiler.esql.array.tbl_type=varchar |
| iscobol.compiler.esql.db2 (boolean) | This property is deprecated and supported only for backward compatibility.<br>Use the [-csdb2](../Compiler/Compiler-Options#compatibility-options) compiler option instead. <br><br>**True** = Treat ESQL in compatibility with IBM DB2.<br>**False** = Don’t treat ESQL in compatibility with IBM DB2. <br><br>If omitted, *False* is assumed. |
| iscobol.compiler.esql.db2.fun.*FunctionName* | When the [-csdb2](../Compiler/Compiler-Options#compatibility-options) compiler option is used, the following DB2 functions are intercepted and handled by the Compiler: ADD_DAYS, ADD_HOURS,<br>ADD_MINUTES, ADD_MONTHS, ADD_SECONDS, ADD_YEARS, AGE, DATE_PART,<br>DATE_TRUNC, DAYNAME, DAYOFMONTH, DAYOFWEEK, DAYOFWEEK_ISO, DAYOFYEAR,<br>DAYS, DAYS_BETWEEN, DAYS_TO_END_OF_MONTH, DATE, EXTRACT, FIRST_DAY,<br>ROM_UTC_TIMESTAMP, HOUR, HOURS_BETWEEN, JULIAN_DATE, MICROSECOND,<br>MIDNIGHT_SECONDS, MINUTE, MINUTES_BETWEEN, MONTH, MONTHNAME, MONTHS_BETWEEN, <br>NEXT_DAY, NEXT_MONTH, NEXT_QUARTER, NEXT_WEEK,<br>NEXT_YEAR, QUARTER, ROUND, ROUND_TIMESTAMP, SECOND, SECONDS_BETWEEN,<br>THIS_MONTH, THIS_QUARTER, THIS_WEEK, THIS_YEAR, TIME, TIMESTAMP,<br>TIMESTAMP_FORMAT, TIMESTAMP_ISO, TIMESTAMPDIFF, TIMEZONE, TO_CHAR,<br>VARCHAR_FORMAT, WEEK, WEEK_ISO, WEEKS_BETWEEN, YEAR, YEARS_BETWEEN,YMD_BETWEEN <br><br>If programs use other functions (e.g. user defined functions) that require DATE, TIME or TIMESTAMP parameters, you can inform the Compiler through this configuration property, that should be set for each function with DATE, TIME or TIMESTAMP parameters not listed above.<br>The value of this property is the list of database types for each parameter. Semicolon is used as separator between multiple database types.Any Java SQL type is allowed. Refer to the [java.sqlTypes](https://docs.oracle.com/javase/8/docs/api/java/sql/Types.html) javadoc for the list of possible values. <br>For example, assuming that the programs invoke an user defined function named MY_TS_BUILDER that takes a DATE parameter, a TIME parameter and a BOOLEAN parameter and returns a TIMESTAMP as result, you should set: <br><br>iscobol.compiler.esql.db2.fun.my_ts_builder=DATE;TIME;BOOLEAN |
| iscobol.compiler.esql.procedure.*ProcedureName* <span id="compiler_esql_procedure_ProcedureName"></span> | This property specifies the signature of a stored procedure. The compiler uses this information to bind the host variables used as parameters when the stored procedure is invoked through a Format 3 [EXECUTE](../../../Language-Reference/Embedded-SQL-Statements/EXECUTE) statement (PL/SQL block). The value of this property has the following format: <br><br>type[,dbtype[,typename]];type[,dbtype[,typename]];...;type[,dbtype[,typename]] <br><br>Where:<br>• *type* is the parameter type; use "i" for input, "o" for output and "u" for input-output.<br>• *dbtype* is the type of the corresponding database field. Any Java SQL type is allowed. Refer to the [java.sqlTypes javadoc](https://docs.oracle.com/javase/8/docs/api/java/sql/Types.html) for the list of possible values. If the *dbtype* is ARRAY, specify also the type name; the Compiler will look for the configuration property [iscobol.compiler.esql.array.TypeName](#compiler_esql_array_TypeName) in order to know which field type is used in the array. If *dbtype* is omitted, *dbtype*=OTHER is assumed. <br><br>*ProcedureName* must be written in lower case regardless of the case it has on the database. If the procedure is included in a package, the package name must be specified as well. <br><br>For example, given the following procedure: <br><br>create or replace package MYPKG<br>IS<br>PROCEDURE PROC1( P1 IN NUMBER, P2 OUT VARCHAR);<br>END MYPKG; <br><br>You would set: <br>iscobol.compiler.esql.procedure.mypkg.proc1=i,numeric;o,varchar |
| iscobol.compiler.generate.keep_structure (boolean) | **True** = Recreate the "bean", "easydb", and "servicebridge" subfolders in the output folder pointed by the [-od=DirName](../Compiler/Compiler-Options#output-options) option when compiling DatabaseBridge routines and ServiceBridge programs.<br>**False** = Put the classes in the output folder pointed by [-od=DirName](../Compiler/Compiler-Options#output-options) option when compiling DatabaseBridge routines and ServiceBridge programs. These classes will be mixed with the classes of other COBOL programs. <br><br>The default value is False. <br><br>**Note** - the "easylinkage" folder is not affected by this setting. |
| iscobol.compiler.generate.root_dir | Specifies the directory where the Compiler will create the subfolders to host the source code for DatabaseBridge routines, ServiceBridge programs and EasyLinkage programs. <br><br>If this property is not set, then the same directory as the source file is assumed as root directory for the subfolders. |
| iscobol.compiler.gui.<control-type\>.defaults | Specifies the default properties and styles for graphical controls. <br><br>When the Compiler finds a graphical control in the source, the code specified by this configuration entry is placed at the beginning of the control description in order to be overridden by the original COBOL code if a conflict occurs. <br><br>Both SCREEN SECTION and DISPLAY statement are affected. <br><br>Valid values for control-type are:<br>bar<br>bitmap<br>check_box<br>combo_box<br>date_entry<br>entry_field<br>frame<br>grid<br>java_bean<br>label<br>list_box<br>push_button<br>radio_button<br>ribbon<br>scroll_barslider<br>status_bar<br>tab_control<br>tree_view<br>web_browser<br>window<br>tool_bar <br><br>Example: <br><br>Let’s consider these two labels: <br>03 label    line 2 col 2 title "label 1".<br>03 label    line 4 col 2 title "label 2" font medium-font. <br><br>Setting *iscobol.compiler.gui.label.defaults=color 5 font small-font* makes the Compiler translate the above code as follows: <br><br>03 label    color 5 font small-font   line 2 col 2 title "label 1".<br>03 label    color 5 font small-font   line 4 col 2 title "label 2" font medium-font. |
| iscobol.compiler.indd <span id="compiler_indd"></span> | When this property is set, all format 1 ACCEPT statements which either have no FROM option or specify FROM SYSIN are transformed into READ statements, reading from the file specified by this property. <br><br>Example: <br><br>iscobol.compiler.indd=in.txt <br><br>If a relative path is provided, it’s resolved according to the runtime working directory.<br>In thin client environment, the file is searched client side.If the file is not found, the system input (SYSIN) is used. <br><br>The name of the indd file can be changed at runtime via configuration if [iscobol.file.env_naming (boolean)](#file_env_naming) is set to true. <br><br>The management of the file can be performed by a custom file handler specified by the configuration property [iscobol.file.indd](#file_indd). |
| iscobol.compiler.iss_julian_base | Specifies the julian base date in the format YYYYMMDD. This property affects only iss dictionaries generated by the [-efc](../Compiler/Compiler-Options#external-file-options) option.<br>If this property is not set, no base date is registered in the iss dictionary and c-treeSQL will use March 1st 1700 as base date for julian dates. |
| iscobol.compiler.javac | Specifies the name of the Java compiler to be used in place of the default. <br><br>This property is ignored by the IDE. When you work with the IDE, the Java compiler has to be configured in the Preferences. |
| iscobol.compiler.javac.options | List of options that should be passed to the Java compiler. Multiple options must be separated by space. A possible values for this property is: <br><br>-source 1.8 -target 1.8 <br><br>The above options create classes that are compatible with previous verions of Java (in this case 1.8 and higher) <br><br>Options can also be passed to the Java compiler through the -jo compiler option. <br><br>This property is ignored by the IDE. When you work with the IDE, the Java compiler options have to be configured in the Preferences. |
| iscobol.compiler.max_constants * <span id="compiler_max_constants"></span> | This property sets the maximum number of constants that can be generated into a class when the [-big](../Compiler/Compiler-Options#source-options) option is used. When the number is exceeded, the program is split into multiple class files. <br><br>The default value is 1000 |
| iscobol.compiler.max_hostvars * <span id="compiler_max_hostvars"></span> | This property sets the maximum number of host variables that can be used in an ESQL statement. When the number is exceeded, the program is split into multiple class files. <br><br>The default value is 700 |
| iscobol.compiler.max_paragraphs * <span id="compiler_max_paragraphs"></span> | This property sets the maximum number of paragraphs that can be generated into a class when the [-big](../Compiler/Compiler-Options#source-options) option is used. When the number is exceeded, the program is split into multiple class files. <br><br>The default value is 300 |
| iscobol.compiler.messagelevel.(error-number)=(action) <span id="compiler_messagelevel"></span> | This property allows you to choose which errors should be traced when compiling. The (error-number) is the number that appears at the beginning of the error message. (action) can be one of the following values: <br>**0** = Do not show<br>**1** = Show as Informational<br>**2** = Show as Warning<br>**3** = Show as Error<br>**4** = Show as Severe Error <br><br>For example, to map the following Error (--E: 154 End statement required END-EVALUATE;) as Informational, this setting must be used: <br><br>iscobol.compiler.messagelevel.154=1 <br><br>Refer to [Error numbers list](../../../Appendices/Compiler-Errors) for the list of error messages and their number. <br><br>**Note** - if error-number refers to a Severe error, then the property will have no effect, as it’s not possible to downgrade the severity of a Severe error. |
| iscobol.compiler.oop.trim_parameters | **0** = Alphanumeric data items mapped to java.lang.String parameters in object oriented programming are not automatically trimmed, both leading and trailing spaces are preserved.<br>**1** = Alphanumeric data items mapped to java.lang.String parameters in object oriented programming are automatically trimmed, both leading and trailing spaces are removed.<br>**2** = Alphanumeric data items mapped to java.lang.String parameters in object oriented programming are automatically right trimmed, only trailing spaces are removed. <br><br>The default value is 1. |
| iscobol.compiler.options * | Lists compiler options. Refer to the [Compiler Options](../Compiler/Compiler-Options) section for further details. These options are used along with the ones specified on the command-line. Multiple options must be separated by space. <br><br>Example:<br>iscobol.compiler.options=-b -cudc |
| iscobol.compiler.outdd <span id="compiler_outdd"></span> | When this property is set, all format 1 DISPLAY statements which either have no UPON option or specify UPON SYSOUT are transformed into WRITE statements, writing to the file specified by this property. Along with the file name it’s possible to specify two optional parameters: the record size and the file type. The file type can be either "L" for Line Sequential (default) or "R" for Record Sequential. Parameters must be separated by space. <br><br>Example of setting with just the file name: <br><br>iscobol.compiler.outdd=out.txt <br><br>Example of setting with all parameters: <br><br>iscobol.compiler.outdd=out.txt 10 L <br><br>If a relative path is provided, it’s resolved according to the runtime working directory.<br>In thin client environment, the file is searched client side.<br>If the file can’t be created, the system output (SYSOUT) is used. <br><br>The file is initialized when the JVM starts and remains locked until the JVM terminates. If multiple COBOL programs refer to the same outdd file, the information that they display is appended to the existing content in the file. <br><br>The name of the outdd file can be changed at runtime via configuration if [iscobol.file.env_naming (boolean)](#file_env_naming) is set to true. <br><br>The management of the file can be performed by a custom file handler specified by the configuration property [iscobol.file.outdd](#file_outdd). |
| iscobol.compiler.regexp * <span id="compiler_regexp"></span> | This property allows you to modify the source code on the fly at compile time through regular expressions. <br><br>See [Source code preprocessing](../Compiler/Source-code-preprocessing/Source-code-preprocessing) for details. |
| iscobol.compiler.rundebug | This property specifies how the Compiler interacts with the remote debuggers.It allows you to debug preoprocessor programs written in COBOL.<br>See [Preprocessor programs](../Compiler/Source-code-preprocessing/Preprocessor-programs) for more information. <br><br>Possible values are: <br>*0* = Remote debugging is not possible.<br>*1* = The first preprocessor program will start and run immediately. The remote debug session waits and listens for a program that is compiled for debug to launch, connecting to the program when it detects it.<br>*2* = The Compiler will start in debug mode, pausing to connect to a remote debugger before running the first preprocessor program. <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.compiler.sqlj * | This property specifies the pathname of the sqlj utility.<br>Relative paths are resolved from the compiler’s working directory. <br><br>The default value is "sqlj". |
| iscobol.compiler.sqlj.options * | This property specifies optional command line options for the sqlj utility. |

##### Properties for the EasyLinkage feature

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.compiler.easylinkage (boolean) | **0** = The EasyLinkage feature is turned off. No bridge classes are generated.<br>**1** = The EasyLinkage feature is turned on and it generates bridge classes for each COBOL program with Linkage Section.<br>**2** = The EasyLinkage feature is turned on and it generates stub classes for each CALL statement.<br>**3** = The EasyLinkage feature is turned on and it generates both bridge classes and stub classes. <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.compiler.easylinkage.cut | This property specifies the substrings to be removed from the Linkage Section data items name before creating the corresponding Java object in the bridge program. Multiple values must be separated by space. <br><br>For example: if a data item is named "lnk-p1" and this property is set to "lnk-", then the item will be considered as "p1". |
| iscobol.compiler.easylinkage.decoration (boolean) | **True** = Linkage Section data items name is capitalized according to Java rules in order to build the name of the corresponding Java object in the bridge program.<br>**False** = Linkage Section data items name is normalized according to COBOL rules (it’s made uppercase replacing hyphens with underscores) in order to build the name of the corresponding Java object in the bridge program. <br><br>For example: if a data item is named "lnk-p1", the corresponding Java object will be named "lnkP1" if this property is set to true, "LNK_P1" otherwise. <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.compiler.easylinkage.package | This property specifies the package that the generated classes must belong to. <br>By default, no package is used. |
| iscobol.compiler.easylinkage.prefix | This property specifies a prefix to be put before the name of the generated bridge class. <br><br>For example: if the compiled program is named "PROG1" and this property is set to "bridgeTo", then the generated class will be named "bridgeToPROG1". <br><br>The default value is "link". |

#### DatabaseBridge (EasyDB) Configuration

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb (boolean) <span id="compiler_easydb"></span> | **True** = The Compiler generates bridge classes that allow the COBOL programs to access RDBMS in the same way as they do with ISAM indexed files.<br>**False** = No bridge class is generated. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.db2 (boolean) | **True** = Generate EDBI routines suitable for IBM DB2.<br>**False** = Don’t generate EDBI routines suitable for IBM DB2. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.db2_as400 (boolean) | **True** = Generate EDBI routines suitable for IBM DB2 on AS/400.<br>**False** = Don’t generate EDBI routines suitable for IBM DB2 on AS/400. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.db2_as400.prefix | This property specifies the prefix that will be put before each indexed file name to build the bridge program name when *easydb.db2_as400*=true.<br>The default value is "d24". |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.db2.prefix | This property specifies the prefix that will be put before each indexed file name to build the bridge program name when *easydb.db2*=true.<br>The default value is "db2". |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.dbmaker (boolean) | **True** = Generate EDBI routines suitable for DBMaker.<br>**False** = Don’t generate EDBI routines suitable for DBMaker. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.dbmaker.prefix | This property specifies the prefix that will be put before each indexed file name to build the bridge program name when *easydb.dbmaker*=true.<br>The default value is "dbm". |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.defchar (boolean) | **True** = Map alphanumeric COBOL fields to CHAR on the database.<br>**False** = Map alphanumeric COBOL fields to VARCHAR on the database. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.dirlevel_to_column (boolean) | **True** = Enables the *dirlevel-to-column* feature.<br>**False** = Disables the *dirlevel-to-column* feature. <br><br>When the *dirlevel-to-column* feature is enabled, the EDBI routines add a varchar column named DTC when they create a table. This column will host portions of the file path depending on the [iscobol.easydb.dirlevel_to_column](#easydb_dirlevel_to_column) runtime setting.<br>The DTC column is used as first field of the primary key and every index.This feature allows you to store in the same table data that was intended to be stored in multiple files with the same name in different paths.This feature is currently supported only on the Oracle database. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.duplicates_in_order (boolean) | **True** = Return records with duplicate key values in the primary key order.<br>**False** = Return records with duplicate key values as you read them from the database. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.entry_points (boolean) | **True** = Generate entry-points in the EDBI routine where the user can inject customized code.<br>**False** = Don’t generate entry-points. <br><br>See [Extending EDBI routines through entry points](../../../../is-cobol-db-bridge/Extending-EDBI-routines-through-entry-points) for more information. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.generic (boolean) | **True** = Generate generic EDBI routines.<br>**False** = Don’t generate generic EDBI routines. <br><br>The default value is False, however generic EDBI routines are generated by default if *compiler.easydb* is true and all the *easydb.<rdbms\>* properties are false. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.generic.prefix | This property specifies the prefix that will be put before each indexed file name to build the bridge program name when *easydb.generic*=true.<br>The default value is "gen". |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.high_values_as_max_val (boolean) | **True** = Replace high-values with the maximum value allowed by the field.<br>**False** = Don’t replace high-values in the fields. <br><br>This option affects numeric items that cannot be set to HIGH-VALUE. It doesn’t affect COMP, BINARY, COMP-X, COMP-5 and COMP-2 as well as numeric items for which either the [ALPHA Directive](../../../Language-Reference/EFD-Directives/ALPHA-Directive) or the [DATE Directive](../../../Language-Reference/EFD-Directives/DATE-Directive) were used. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.index_only (boolean) | **True** = Generate EDBI routines only for indexed files.<br>**False** = Generate EDBI routines for indexed, line sequential, relative and sequential files. <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.informix (boolean) | **True** = Generate EDBI routines suitable for Informix. <br>**False** = Don’t generate EDBI routines suitable for Informix. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.informix.dates_as_strings (boolean) | **True** = Use the string representation to deal with dates.<br>**False** = Use conversion functions to deal with dates. <br><br>The default value is False. <br><br>This property is considered only for Informix. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.informix.prefix | This property specifies the prefix that will be put before each indexed file name to build the bridge program name when *easydb.informix*=true.<br>The default value is "ifx". |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.isam_eof (boolean) | **True** = Use ISAM positioning rules on end of file.<br>**False** = Don’t use ISAM positioning rules on end of file. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.julian_routines=<cbdb\>;<dbcb\>; <span id="compiler_easydb_julian_routines"></span> | This property specifies the name of custom routines for julian dates management. Two routines must be provided: the first is for conversions between COBOL and database while the second is for conversions between database and COBOL. The two names must be separated by semicolon. <br><br>In order to create these routines, you can edit and customize EDBI-DTJUCBDB.cbl and EDBI-DTJUDBCB.cbl installed under $ISCOBOL/easydb/edbisource. <br><br>If the property is not set, then the standard EDBI routines EDBI-DTJUCBDB and EDBI-DTJUDBCB stored in the isCOBOL runtime library are used. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.light_cursors <span id="#compiler_easydb_light_cursors"></span> | This property is considered only for Microsoft SQL Server, MySQL and PostgreSQL.<br>When set to "1" or "2", the EDBI routines perform a pagination of the read records.<br>Possible values are: <br><br>**0** = No pagination<br>**1** = Pagination only when using a UNIQUE index<br>**2** = Pagination for every index <br><br>The default value is 0. <br><br>**Note** - When the value 2 is used, an additional column is generated for FDs that have at least one key with duplicates. For this reason, routines generated with *light_cursors*=2 may not work on tables that were created by routines generated with a different value of *light_cursors* and vice versa. The name of the additional column is "OID" but it can be changed at compile time through the configuration properties [iscobol.compiler.easydb.sqlserver.oid_name](#compiler_easydb_sqlserver_oidname), [iscobol.compiler.easydb.mysql.oid_name](#compiler_easydb_mysql_oidname) and [iscobol.compiler.easydb.postgres.oid_name](#compiler_easydb_postgres_oidname). <br><br>The number of records per page is controlled at runtime by the configuration properties [iscobol.easydb.sqlserver_row_limit](#easydb_sqlserver_row_limit), [iscobol.easydb.mysql_row_limit](#easydb_mysql_row_limit) and [iscobol.easydb.postgres_row_limit](#easydb_postgres_row_limit). |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.max_char_len | This property sets the size limit used by EDBI routines to choose if the an alphanumeric COBOL field must be mapped to CHAR or VARCHAR. If the size of the COBOL field is not greater than the value of the property, then the field is mapped to CHAR, otherwise it is mapped to VARCHAR. <br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.mysql (boolean) | **True** = Generate EDBI routines suitable for MySQL.<br>**False** = Don’t generate EDBI routines suitable for MySQL. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.mysql.hints (boolean) <span id="compiler_easydb_mysql_hints"></span> | This property is considered only for MySQL. It forces the use of hints within queries used to simulate the COBOL Start statement. This should speed up the reading of records.<br>Possible values are: <br><br>**True** = Use hints<br>**False** = Don’t use hints <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.mysql.oid_name <span id="compiler_easydb_mysql_oidname"></span> | This property specifies the name of the OID column generated when [iscobol.compiler.easydb.light_cursors](##compiler_easydb_light_cursors) is set to 2. It’s considered only for MySQL. <br><br>The default value is "OID". |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.mysql.prefix | This property specifies the prefix that will be put before each indexed file name to build the bridge program name when *easydb.mysql*=true.<br>The default value is "mys". |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.names_with_leading_zeros (boolean) | **True** = Use leading zeroes in OCCURS item names.<br>The number of leading zeroes depends by the occurs size. EasyDB puts before as many zeroes as it takes to reach the number of digits of the occurs size.<br>**False** = Don’t use leading zeroes in OCCURS item names. <br><br>Example:<br>Consider the following COBOL items:   <br><br>03 my_item_a pic x(10) occurs 3.  <br>03 my_item_b pic x(10) occurs 30.  <br>03 my_item_c pic x(10) occurs 300. <br><br>If the property is false, then the columns are named:   <br><br>my_item_a_1, my_item_a_2, my_item_a_3  <br>my_item_b_1, my_item_b_2, my_item_b_3, ... my_item_b_30  <br>my_item_c_1, my_item_c_2, my_item_c_3, ... my_item_c_300 <br><br>If the property is true, then the columns are named:   <br><br>my_item_a_1, my_item_a_2, my_item_a_3  <br>my_item_b_01, my_item_b_02, my_item_b_03, ... my_item_b_30  <br>my_item_c_001, my_item_c_002, my_item_c_003, ... my_item_c_300  <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.no_check (boolean) <span id="compiler_easydb_no_check"></span> | **True** = Don’t check for table existence during OPEN.<br>**False** = Check for table existence during OPEN. <br><br>The default value is False. <br><br>**Note** - disabling the check of table existence may improve performance, especially if your programs use the OPEN statement a lot. However, there are also few side effects, for example, since the OPEN will never fail with ‘file not found’ you will not be able to create with I$IO having io_creates=1 in the configuration. Also, if the table doesn’t exist but the OPEN doesn’t fail, you might have odd errors in the next operations. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.not_null_columns (boolean) | **True** = Apply the NOT NULL clause to every field when the table is created.<br>**False** = When the table is created, apply the NOT NULL clause only in these cases:<br><br>• SQLServer RAW columns<br>• MySQL and generic CHAR columns<br>• DB2 numeric columns <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.oracle (boolean) | **True** = Generate EDBI routines suitable for Oracle.<br>**False** = Don’t generate EDBI routines suitable for Oracle. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.oracle.hints <span id="compiler_easydb_oracle_hints"></span> | This property is considered only for Oracle. It forces the use of hints within queries used to simulate the COBOL Start statement. This should speed up the reading of records.<br>Possible values are: <br><br>**0** = Don’t use hints.<br>**1** = Use hints but keep the ORDER BY in they query.<br>**2** = Use hints and discard the ORDER BY in the query, assuming that records will be sorted according to the index set by the hint. <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.oracle.index_storage_initial_value | This property is considered only for Oracle. It specifies the initial storage value for indexes. <br><br>If not set, then the default storage value is used. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.oracle.index_storage_next_value | This property is considered only for Oracle. It specifies the next storage value for indexes. <br><br>If not set, then the default storage value is used. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.oracle.index_storage_pctincrease_value | This property is considered only for Oracle. It specifies the initial pctincrease for indexes. <br><br>If not set, then the default pctincrease value is used. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.oracle.prefix | This property specifies the prefix that will be put before each indexed file name to build the bridge program name when *easydb.oracle*=true.<br>The default value is "ora". |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.oracle.table_storage_initial_value | This property is considered only for Oracle. It specifies the initial storage value for tables. <br><br>If not set, then the default storage value is used. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.oracle.table_storage_next_value | This property is considered only for Oracle. It specifies the next storage value for tables. <br><br>If not set, then the default storage value is used. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.oracle.table_storage_pctincrease_value | This property is considered only for Oracle. It specifies the initial pctincrease for tables. <br><br>If not set, then the default pctincrease value is used. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.oracle.tablespace_index_name | This property is considered only for Oracle. It specifies the name of the tablespace where indexes must be created and searched. <br><br>If not set, then the default tablespace is used. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.oracle.tablespace_name | This property is considered only for Oracle. It specifies the name of the tablespace where tables must be created and searched. <br><br>If not set, then the default tablespace is used. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.oracle.wait_for_lock | This property is considered only for Oracle. It specifies how the EDBI routine should behave when a record locked condition occurs. You can choose between waiting for the lock to be released or returning an error to the COBOL program.<br>Possible values are: <br><br>**0** = Never wait for locks and return an error instead<br>**1** = Always wait for locks<br>**2** = Wait for locks or return an error depending on the [iscobol.easydb.wait_for_lock (boolean)](#easydb_wait_for_lock) runtime setting <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.output | This property specifies the output directory where the EDBI routine must be generated. If not set, then the routine is generated in *easydb* subdirectory, created in the same directory of the COBOL source file. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.postgres (boolean) | **True** = Generate EDBI routines suitable for PostgreSQL.<br>**False** = Don’t generate EDBI routines suitable for PostgreSQL. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.postgres.oid_name <span id="compiler_easydb_postgres_oidname"></span> | This property specifies the name of the OID column generated when [iscobol.compiler.easydb.light_cursors](./Configuration-Properties#compiler_easydb_light_cursors) is set to 2. It’s considered only for PostgreSQL. <br><br>The default value is "OID". |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.postgres.prefix | This property specifies the prefix that will be put before each indexed file name to build the bridge program name when *easydb.postgres*=true.<br>The default value is "pgs". |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.sql (boolean) <span id="compiler_easydb_sql"></span> | **True** = Generate a script file with .sql extension that includes the CREATE TABLE statement.<br>**False** = Don’t generate script files. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.sql.output | This property specifies the output directory where the script files must be generated. If not set, then the script files are generated in the same directory as the COBOL source file. This property is considered only if [iscobol.compiler.easydb.sql (boolean)](#compiler_easydb_sql) is set to true. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.sqlserver (boolean) | **True** = Generate EDBI routines suitable for Microsoft SQL Server.<br>**False** = Don’t generate EDBI routines suitable for Microsoft SQL Server. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.sqlserver.datetime_always (boolean) | **True** = Every field with EFD DATE directive becomes a DATETIME, regardless of the date format string.<br>**False** = Fields with EFD DATE directive become DATE, TIME or DATETIME according to the date format string. <br><br>The default value is False. <br><br>This property is considered only for Microsoft SQL Server. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.sqlserver.oid_name <span id="compiler_easydb_sqlserver_oidname"></span> | This property specifies the name of the OID column generated when [iscobol.compiler.easydb.light_cursors](./Configuration-Properties#compiler_easydb_light_cursors) is set to 2. It’s considered only for Microsoft SQL Server. <br><br>The default value is "OID". |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.sqlserver.prefix | This property specifies the prefix that will be put before each indexed file name to build the bridge program name when *easydb.sqlserver*=true.<br>The default value is "srv". |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.test_not_numeric | This property allows you to write zeros instead of non-numeric values in numeric key fields, avoiding data conversion errors that might occur with some JDBC drivers.<br>Possible values are: <br><br>**0** = No action<br>*1* = Write zero instead of non-numeric values<br>**2** = Same as "1", but with trace. The trace is stored in the file pointed by the [iscobol.edbi.notnum.tracefile](#edbi_notnum_tracefile) runtime setting. <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.compiler.easydb.unlock_all (boolean) | **True** = Enable the support for UNLOCK ALL statement.<br>**False** = It’s not possible to perform UNLOCK ALL. <br><br>The default value is False. |

#### Service Bridge Configuration

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Property | Meaning |
| iscobol.compiler.servicebridge (boolean) <span id="compiler_servicebridge"></span> | **True** = The Compiler generates bridge classes that allow the COBOL programs to be used as a Web Service.<br>**False** = No bridge class is generated. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.compiler.servicebridge.bean <span id="compiler_servicebridge_bean"></span> | This property specifies the service type for which a bean client program should be generated along with the bridge program. Valid values are: <br><br>**SOAP** = Generate beans for SOAP Web Services<br>**REST** = Generate beans for REST Web Services |
| iscobol.compiler.servicebridge.bean.package <span id="compiler_servicebridge_bean_package"></span> | This property specifies the package for the bean class. <br><br>By default, there’s no package. |
| iscobol.compiler.servicebridge.bean.prefix <span id="compiler_servicebridge_bean_prefix"></span> | This property specifies the prefix to be applied to the bean client program name. <br><br>The default value is "bean". |
| iscobol.compiler.servicebridge.bean.url <span id="compiler_servicebridge_bean_url"></span> | This property specifies the URL to which the bean client program must connect. <br><br>The default value is [http://localhost:8080/services](https://www.example.com) |
| iscobol.compiler.servicebridge.type <span id="compiler_servicebridge_type"></span> | This property specifies what type of service will be provided by the bridge classes. Possible values are: <br><br>**SOAP** = SOAP Web Service<br>**REST** = REST Web Service The default value is "SOAP". |
| iscobol.compiler.servicebridge.rest.prefix <span id="compiler_servicebridge_rest_prefix"></span> | This property specifies the prefix that will be put before the COBOL program name to build the bridge program name when servicebridge.type=REST. <br><br>The default value is "rest". |
| iscobol.compiler.servicebridge.soap.charset | This property specifies the encoding that should be used int the SOAP service response. <br><br>All the canonical names listed in the following Java documentation can be used as value for this property: <br><br>https://docs.oracle.com/javase/8/docs/technotes/guides/intl/encoding.doc.html <br><br>The default value is "UTF-8" |
| iscobol.compiler.servicebridge.soap.namespace_suffix <span id="compiler_servicebridge_soap_namespace_suffix"></span> | This property specifies the string to be appended to the value of [iscobol.compiler.servicebridge.soap.namespace_suffix](#compiler_servicebridge_soap_namespace_suffix) in order to compose the final namespace. <br><br>By default, the COBOL program name is used. |
| iscobol.compiler.servicebridge.soap.prefix <span id="compiler_servicebridge_soap_prefix"></span> | This property specifies the prefix that will be put before the COBOL program name to build the bridge program name when [iscobol.compiler.servicebridge.type](#compiler_servicebridge_type)=SOAP. <br><br>The default value is "soap". |
| iscobol.compiler.servicebridge.soap.url <span id="compiler_servicebridge_soap_url"></span> | This property specifies the Web Service base URL. <br><br>The default value is [http://localhost:8080](https://www.example.com) |
| iscobol.compiler.servicebridge.soap.style <span id="compiler_servicebridge_soap_style"></span> | This property specifies the SOAP messaging style.<br>Possible values are: <br><br>**Document** = use Document style<br>**RPC** = use RPC style <br><br>The Document style indicates that the SOAP body contains a XML document which can be validated against pre-defined XML schema document.RPC indicates that the SOAP message body contains an XML representation of a method call and uses the names of the method and its parameters to generate XML structures that represent a method’s call stack. <br><br>The default value is "RPC". |
| iscobol.compiler.servicebridge.soap.namespace <span id="compiler_servicebridge_soap_namespace"></span> | This property specifies the unique namespace in order for client applications to distinguish your SOAP Web Service from other services on the Web. <br><br>The default value is "http://tempuri.org" |
| iscobol.compiler.servicebridge.package | This property specifies a package for the generated bridge class. <br><br>By default, the bridge classes have no package. |

#### General Runtime Configuration

(\*) The asterisk after the property name means that the property is static, it’s inquired only once by the Framework and then changing it using the SET ENVIRONMENT statement has no effect.

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.array_cache <span id="array_cache"></span> | This property represents the number of OCCURS elements stored in memory for increasing performances. <br><br>Setting this property to 0 or to a negative value, disables the array caching feature. <br><br>The default value is 101. |
| iscobol.array_check * <span id="array_check"></span> | **-1** = Array boundaries are checked at Runtime in order to provide more details in case of "out of bounds" errors. If a program addresses an item that is outside the valid range, an error message is written to the log ([iscobol.tracelevel](#tracelevel) must be set to a value greater than zero). The error message informs about the data item name and the problematic index value.<br>**0** = Array boundaries are not checked. If a program addresses an item that is outside the valid range, a generic "out of bounds" error message is shown and the program exits.<br>**1** = Array boundaries are checked at Runtime in order to provide more details in case of "out of bounds" errors. If a program addresses an item that is outside the valid range, an error message is shown and the program exits. The error message informs about the data item name and the problematic index value. <br><br>**Note** - for an accurate error message, program shouldn’t be compiled with the [-ostrip](../../Compiler-and-Runtime/Compiler/Compiler-Options#output-options) option. <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.call_cancel.hook | This property specifies the name of a class that the runtime will invoke before and after each CALL and CANCEL statement.<br>The class must implement the [CallHandler Interface (com.iscobol.rts.CallHandler)](../../../Appendices/Internal-Objects/CallHanlder-Interface/CallHandler-Interface).<br>Refer to the sample installed with the isCOBOL SDK under *sample/extend-and-customize/program-hook* for a demonstration. <br><br>**Note** - The CALL PROGRAM statement and calls to C functions are not affected by this property. |
| iscobol.call_program.set_switches (boolean) | **True** = The program name in a CALL PROGRAM statement is parsed for trailing "/A", "/B",...,"/Z". If found, the corresponding switches are activated. For example, the statement:<br>CALL PROGRAM "SUB/A/C".<br>calls SUB and activates his switch "A" and switch "C".<br>**False** = The program name in a CALL PROGRAM statement is used as is. For example, the statement:<br>CALL PROGRAM "SUB/A/C".<br>looks for a program called C in the directory SUB/A. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.call_run.sync (boolean) <span id="call_run_sync"></span> | **True** = The calling program in a CALL RUN waits for the called program to terminate before proceeding.<br>**False** = The calling program in a CALL RUN doesn’t wait for the called program to terminate before proceeding. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.check.numeric_content * <span id="check_numeric_content"></span> | **-1** = an exception is written to the log for USAGE DISPLAY numeric and numeric-edited variables that don’t contain a number. [iscobol.tracelevel](#tracelevel) must be set to a value greater than zero.<br>**0** = no exceptions are thrown for USAGE DISPLAY numeric and numeric-edited variables that don’t contain a number.<br>**1** = an exception is thrown for USAGE DISPLAY numeric and numeric-edited variables that don’t contain a number. <br><br>The default value is 0. <br><br>The check is performed when the Framework reads bytes from the memory and transform them to a number, for example when the program displays numeric data items or when arithmetic operations are performed. <br><br>For numeric edited items the exception is thrown if there isn’t a numeric digit in correspondence with the '9' characters of the picture. |
| iscobol.checkdiv * <span id="checkdiv"></span> | This property allows you to specify an alternate runtime response to a divide by zero condition when the statement does not include a ON SIZE ERROR clause. <br>Valid values are: <br><br>**-1** = The error message "Attempt to divide by zero" is written to the log ([iscobol.tracelevel](#tracelevel) must be set to a value greater than zero) and the program aborts.<br>**0** = Results are undefined.<br>**1** = The program aborts with the error message "Attempt to divide by zero".<br>**2** = The result is zero.<br>**3** = The result is the dividend, as if the division was by 1 instead of by zero. <br><br>**Note** - for an accurate error message, program shouldn’t be compiled with the [-ostrip](../Compiler/Compiler-Options#output-options) option. <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.code_prefix <span id="code_prefix"></span> | This property lists the paths and the jar libraries in which programs are searched. Multiple values must be separated by the line feed character or by the current operating system path separator.<br>Within configuration files, the line feed character is "\n". In COBOL programs, the line feed character is x"0a".<br>An asterisk can be specified at the end of a path to include all the jars in that path. <br><br>code_prefix is used by:<br>• [CALL](../../../Language-Reference/Procedure-Division-Statements/CALL) STATEMENT<br>• *isrun* and *iscrun* commands<br>• isCOBOL Server<br>• WebDirect <br><br>CLASSPATH is read first, then code_prefix is read. This means that if the same program is found in both CLASSPATH and code_prefix, it’s loaded from CLASSPATH. If code_prefix is not set, CLASSPATH is used instead.<br>Note that [Wrappers](../../../Introduction/Wrappers/wrappers) automatically add the current directory, the jar libraries of the isCOBOL *lib* directory and the jar libraries of the isCOBOL **jars** directory to the Classpath. <br><br>All classes loaded from code_prefix are loaded into memory each time they are called, if the program cancels these classes from memory (see [CANCEL](../../../Language-Reference/Procedure-Division-Statements/CANCEL) STATEMENT). A class is reloaded from disk only if the disk file last modification timestamp is different from the last modification timestamp of the class loaded in memory.<br>Programs loaded from CLASSPATH, instead, are always stored in memory until the Runtime Framework terminates and the [CANCEL](../../../Language-Reference/Procedure-Division-Statements/CANCEL) STATEMENT just initializes their DATA DIVISION. <br><br>When code_prefix is set, it’s possible to specify a path in the name of the programs, e.g.<br>• CALL "/path/to/PROG1"<br>• isrun path\to\PROG1 <br><br>COBOL classes (programs that have CLASS-ID in their IDENTIFICATION DIVISION) are loaded from the code_prefix only if they're in the same location as the COBOL program (a program that has PROGRAM-ID in its IDENTIFICATION DIVISION) that invokes them and they're cancelled and reloaded along with that COBOL program. However, loading COBOL classes from the code_prefix may create issues; for example it’s not possible to implement the same interface in two different COBOL classes without causing a ClassCastException. Loading COBOL classes from the code_prefix is discouraged. COBOL classes should always be loaded from the CLASSPATH. |
| iscobol.code_prefix.reload * | This property can be set along with [iscobol.code_prefix](#code_prefix) to alter the class loading logic. <br><br>Possible values are: <br><br>**1** = The runtime accesses the disc in order to find if the program class file changed. If the program class file has changed, the runtime reloads the class from disc.<br>**0** = The runtime reloads from disc only those programs that were unloaded by [C$UNLOAD](../../../Appendices/Library-Routines/C$UNLOAD). No check is performed on the program class file last modification date. A dedicated ClassLoader for each program is used, hence you can unload specific programs via the [C$UNLOAD](../../../Appendices/Library-Routines/C$UNLOAD) routine. CLASS-ID programs are unloaded only along with the program that instantiated them.<br>**2** = The runtime reloads from disc only those programs that were unloaded by [C$UNLOAD](../../../Appendices/Library-Routines/C$UNLOAD). No check is performed on the program class file last modification date. A common ClassLoader is used, hence the [C$UNLOAD](../../../Appendices/Library-Routines/C$UNLOAD) routine always unloads all the programs. This value is particularly useful in a thin client environment to have the next client sessions start fresh with updated classes. <br><br>In a thin client environment the unload of programs can be managed with the administration panel. See [Unloading programs](../../../../is-cobol-AS/Usage-of-isCOBOL-Client/Usage-of-isCOBOL-Client#unloading-programs) for more information. <br><br>The default value is 1. |
| iscobol.conf * | This property specifies a configuration file to be used by the Framework. This configuration file is used along with other configuration sources as explained in [Configuration](./Configuration). <br><br>This property can be specified only on the command line. Setting it in a configuration file has no effect.<br>The [-c](../../Compiler-and-Runtime/Runtime-Framework/Runtime-Options#-c) runtime option has the same effect of this property. <br><br>**Note** - this property can’t be set as system environment variable. Setting CONF using the SET command on Windows or the EXPORT command on Unix/Linux has no effect. |
| iscobol.conf.copy * | This property specifies a configuration file to be added to the current configuration.<br>Relative pathnames are resolved according to the Framework’s working directory. <br><br>This property should be set in a configuration file.<br>Multiple entries of this property can appear in the configuration file.<br>The line where **iscobol.conf.copy** is written is the position where the new properties will be included. <br><br>This property can't be set dynamically by COBOL programs. If you need to add a configuration file to the current configuration in the COBOL program, use the [C$CONFIG](../../../Appendices/Library-Routines/C$CONFIG/C$CONFIG) routine.<br>This property can't be inquired. <br><br>Example:<br>Suppose having a file named *app.properties* with the following entries: <br><br>iscobol.code_prefix=/opt/myapp/class<br>iscobol.conf.copy=default.properties<br>iscobol.file.prefix=/opt/myapp/data <br><br>A runtime session launched with the *app.properties* configuration file will use all the configuration entries in the *default.properties* file plus the *file.prefix* setting and the *code_prefix* setting specified in the *app.properties* configuration file. If *default.properties* includes a *code_prefix* setting, it will override the *code_prefix* setting in *app.properties*. If *default.properties* includes a *file.prefix* setting, it will be overridden by the *file.prefix* setting in *app.properties*. |
| iscobol.conf.only * | This property specifies a configuration file to be used exclusively by the Framework. No other configuration sources are inquired when this property is set. <br><br>This property can be specified only on the command line. Setting it in a configuration file has no effect.<br>The [-conly](../../Compiler-and-Runtime/Runtime-Framework/Runtime-Options#-conly) runtime option has the same effect of this property. <br><br>**Note** - this property can’t be set as system environment variable. Setting CONF.ONLY using the SET command on Windows or the EXPORT command on Unix/Linux has no effect. |
| iscobol.conf.var_delimiters | This property allows you to set delimiters for free text (comments) and Java variables within configuration property values. <br><br>For example, having<br>iscobol.conf.var_delimiters=/*.\*/<br>iscobol.jver=/\*java.version\*/<br>iscobol.hello=Hello,/\* this is a comment \*/ World! <br><br>the configuration variable jver will have the value “1.8.0_261” (assuming that you’re working with this JVM) and the configuration variable hello will have the value “Hello, World!” <br><br>Refer to the table in [C$GETENV](../../../Appendices/Library-Routines/C$GETENV) documentation for a list of Java properties that you can include between delimiters. |
| iscobol.crypt.algorithm | This property specifies the symmetric-key algorithm used by [C$DECRYPT](../../../Appendices/Library-Routines/C$ENCRYPT) and [C$ENCRYPT](../../../Appendices/Library-Routines/C$ENCRYPT) library routines.<br>See the Cipher section in the [Java Cryptography Architecture Standard Algorithm Name Documentation](https://docs.oracle.com/javase/8/docs/technotes/guides/security/StandardNames.html#Cipher) for information about standard algorithm names. <br><br>The default value is "AES". |
| iscobol.current_date | This property changes the format used in CURRENT-DATE (IBM DOS/VS COBOL compatibility). <br><br>**0** = MM/DD/YY is used.<br>**1** = DD/MM/YY is used. <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.default_options | This property specifies the default command line options used by the runtime (iscrun) and the client (iscclient). <br><br>When used with iscrun, if the value of this property includes either the -c option or the -conly option and the configuration file pointed to by this option includes another iscobol.default_options setting, such setting is ignored.<br><br> When used with iscclient, if the value of this property includes the -lc option and the configuration file pointed to by this option includes another iscobol.default_options setting, this second setting of iscobol.default_options is ignored. |
| iscobol.default_program | This property specifies the name of the program to be executed when no program is specified on the command line. The name of the program specified by this property can be followed by a list of parameters separated by space that the program will receive as chaining parameters. <br><br>Example:<br>iscobol.default_program=MAIN_MENU company1 user1 <br><br>In thin client environment the property can be specified either in the remote configuration file or in the local configuration file. If the property is specified in both configuration files, then the value in the local configuration file is considered. |
| iscobol.dispsign (boolean) * | **True** = The displayed values for signed binary, signed packed-decimal, or overpunch signed zoned-decimal items are always formatted with a leading separate sign<br>**False** = Formatting for displayed values of signed numeric items is compatible with prior versions of Enterprise COBOL. Overpunch signs are generated in some cases <br><br>The default value is True |
| iscobol.dll_convention | This property sets the convention used when the DLL function is called. <br><br>**0** = C convention.<br>**1** = PASCAL (or WINAPI) convention. <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.encoding * | This property specifies the encoding used to decode the content of alphanumeric data items. It affects only in-memory conversions.<br>Due to this limited effect, it’s recommended to use the *file.encoding* Java property instead, as the Java property specifies the encoding for every operation, including i-o. <br><br>The valid values for *iscobol.encoding* and *file.encoding* are the canonical names listed at https://docs.oracle.com/javase/8/docs/technotes/guides/intl/encoding.doc.html. <br><br>*iscobol.encoding* can appear only in the configuration file. Setting it with the SET ENVIRONMENT statement has no effect. <br><br>*file.encoding* can’t appear in the configuration file, because it’s not an isCOBOL configuration entry (it lacks the "iscobol." prefix) and can’t be set with the SET ENVIRONMENT statement. It must be set on the command line, for example: <br><br>iscrun -J-Dfile.encoding=UTF-8 PROG <br><br>In thin client environment it’s good practice to use the same encoding on both client side and server side, for example: <br><br>iscserver -J-Dfile.encoding=CP1252 -hostname 192.168.0.101 -port 10999<br>iscclient -J-Dfile.encoding=CP1252 -hostname 192.168.0.101 -port 10999 PROG <br><br>The default encoding depends on the operating system. <br><br>**Note** - the *file.encoding* Java property is used also by the Compiler. Use it to compile source files that were saved with a particular encoding. For example, in order to compile a source file saved in UTF-8 format, use: <br><br>iscc -J-Dfile.encoding=UTF-8 PROG.cbl |
| iscobol.floating_point_format * | This property specifies the format used to store Float and Double values. <br><br>Possible values are:<br>• ibm_hfp<br>• ieee_754 <br><br>Under [-cm](../../Compiler-and-Runtime/Compiler/Compiler-Options#compatibility-options) option and [-cv](../../Compiler-and-Runtime/Compiler/Compiler-Options#compatibility-options) option this property affects also COMP-1 and COMP-2 data items, as they’re treated as Float and Double respectively. <br><br>The default value is "ieee_754". |
| iscobol.display_message <span id="display_message"></span> | This property defines how messages are shown to the user. It affects both messages displayed by the program through DISPLAY MESSAGE BOX statement and error messages shown by the runtime system. <br><br>In thin client environment it is also evaluated on the client side for the Client connection error messages. <br><br>Possible values are: <br><br>**0** = All messages are shown in a message box.<br>**1** = All messages are sent to sysout. In a thin client environment, runtime error messages are sent to the server sysout while messages displayed by the program are sent to the client sysout.<br>**2** = All messages are sent to syserr. In a thin client environment, runtime error messages are sent to the server syserr while messages displayed by the program are sent to the client syserr.<br>**3** = Messages displayed by the program are sent to the syserr (client syserr in thin client) while runtime error messages are printed to a file named <program_name\><number\>.ads.log (where <number\> is a progressive number calculated by the runtime). The file name and location can be customized via the [iscobol.exception.dumpfile](#exception_dumpfile) property. <br><br>Any other value is equivalent to 2. <br><br>The default value is 0. <br><br>(iscobol.exception_message is still supported for backward compatibility, but this property affects only messages produced after the first COBOL program has been loaded) |
| iscobol.display_message_timeout | This property specifies a timeout for the error message boxes generated by the runtime system.<br>The timeout is expressed in thousands of seconds.<br>When the timeout expires, the error message box is automatically closed as if the user pressed the OK button. <br><br>In a standalone runtime environment, when the property is set in the runtime configuration, the runtime error messages are affected.<br>In a thin client environment, the property must be set in the client local configuration and it affects both runtime error messages and connection error messages. <br><br>By default there is no timeout and error message boxes stay on video until the user closes them. |
| iscobol.env.trunc_on_null | **True** = Configuration values are truncated at the first 0x00<br>**False** = 0x00 in configuration values is accepted as any other character <br><br>The default value is False |
| iscobol.exception.dump (boolean) | **True** = Produces "Abend Diagnostic Snapshot" (ADS) in addition to Java exceptions<br>**False** = No ADS is produced in addition to Java exceptions <br><br>The resulting exception is shown on video or printed to file according to the properties [iscobol.display_message](#display_message) and [iscobol.exception.message](#exception_message). <br><br>The default value is False |
| iscobol.exception.dumpfile <span id="exception_dumpfile"></span> | This property specifies the pathname of the file generated by setting [iscobol.display_message](#display_message)=3 or [iscobol.exception.message](#exception_message)=3. <br><br>The following special characters are supported in the value of this property: <br>%p, program name<br>%d, current date in the form YYYYMMDD<br>%t, current time in the form HHMMSSTTT<br>%u, username<br>%h, hostname <br><br>If the value begins with the "+" character, then the report is appended to the specified file, otherwise the new report overwrites the specified file. <br><br>Example:<br>iscobol.exception.dumpfile=/tmp/%p.dump <br><br>If this property is not set, a file named <program_name>\<number\>.ads.log (where <number\> is a progressive number calculated by the runtime) is generated by default in the working directory. <br>(iscobol.exception.prefix is still supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.exception.java (boolean) <span id="exception_java"></span> | **True** = Internal java methods are traced in exception messages.<br>**False** = COBOL paragraph names are traced in exception messages. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.exception.message <span id="exception_message"></span> | This property defines how exception messages are shown to the user. It affects only error messages shown by the runtime system. <br><br>**0** = Messages are shown in a message box.<br>**1** = Messages are sent to sysout. In a thin client environment, they’re sent to the server sysout.<br>**2** = Messages are sent to syserr. In a thin client environment, they’re sent to the server syserr.<br>**3** = Messages are printed to a file named <program_name\><number\>.ads.log (where <number\> is a progressive number calculated by the runtime). The file name and location can be customized via the [iscobol.exception.dumpfile](#exception_dumpfile) property. <br>Any other value is equivalent to 2. <br><br>The default value is 0. <br><br>Note that only the messages produced after the first COBOL program has been loaded (e.g. a ArrayOutOfBoundsException generated by OCCURS overflow) are affected by this setting. In order to affect all messages (e.g. a invalid command line error) set [iscobol.display_message](#display_message) instead. |
| iscobol.help_program <span id="help_program"></span> | This property identifies the COBOL program to be called for item’s help. The program is called with a implicit CALL statement so the program class can be loaded either from the Class Path or from the [iscobol.code_prefix](#code_prefix).<br>See [Help automation](../../../User-Interface/Working-With-UI-and-Controls/Creating-a-control/Help-automation) for more details. |
| iscobol.help_program_mouse_stop_delay | This property enables the invocation of the help program specified by [iscobol.help_program](#help_program) when the user leaves the mouse pointer over a control.<br>The value of this property is the number of milliseconds that the runtime must wait before invoking the help program.<br>Setting this property to 0 disables the feature. In this case the help program can be invoked only via keyboard. See [Help automation](../../../User-Interface/Working-With-UI-and-Controls/Creating-a-control/Help-automation) for more details. <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.hot_event.*ProgramName* | This property associates an event or a series of events with a handler program. The handler program will automatically be executed when the event occurs. Use the event number to specify the desired event. To define a series of events specify their numbers comma-separated. <br><br>Example: <br>iscobol.hot_event.myprog=16400 <br><br>(MYPROG.class will run automatically when the event type is 16400, so when a msg-bitmap-clicked event is fired). <br><br>iscobol.hot_event.myprog=16392,16393 <br><br>(MYPROG.class will run automatically when the event type is 16392 or 16393, so when either a msg-begin-entry event or a msg-finish-entry event is fired). <br><br>Refer to the [isgui.def](../../../Appendices/Copybooks/isgui.def) copybook for the list of events and their numbers.<br>See [The Hot Event feature](../../../User-Interface/Working-With-UI-and-Controls/Creating-a-control/Embedded-Procedures/Event-handling#the-hot-event-feature) for more information. <br><br>This property is loaded along with the program so if you set it dynamically with SET ENVIRONMENT it will affect the called programs and not the current program. <br><br>Window events can’t be associated to a program through this property. |
| iscobol.hot_key.*ProgramName* | This property associates an exception value, a series of exception values or a range of exception values with a hotkey program. The hotkey program will automatically be executed when the exception occurs. To define a series of exception values, separate them by comma. To define a range of exception values specify the minimum value followed by a dash and the maximum value. <br><br>Example: <br><br>iscobol.hot_key.myprog=100 <br><br>(MYPROG.class will run automatically when the crt status is 100). <br><br>iscobol.hot_key.myprog=100,111,150 <br><br>(MYPROG.class will run automatically when the crt status is 100, 111 or 150). <br><br>iscobol.hot_key.myprog=1-5 <br><br>(MYPROG.class will run automatically when crt-status is 1, 2, 3, 4 or 5). <br><br>This property is loaded along with the program so if you set it dynamically with SET ENVIRONMENT it will affect the called programs and not the current program. |
| iscobol.json_parse.read_on_mismatch (boolean) | **True** = The JSON PARSE statement reads all matching items and doesn’t fail if there’s a mismatch between the Working-Storage item and the JSON stream.<br>**False** = The JSON PARSE statement fails if there’s a mismatch between the Working-Storage item and the JSON stream. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.jvm_options | This property specifies the command-line options to be passed to a new Java Virtual Machine (JVM) launched by a running isCOBOL process. isCOBOL instantiates new JVMs in these two cases: <br><br>• when the -d option is used to run in debug. The Debugger starts a new JVM to run the program.<br><br>• when [iscobol.as.multitasking](#as_multitasking) is set to a value greater than 0, causing the Application Server to start a separate JVM process for each connection. <br><br>Multiple options must be separated by a space. For example, you can change the default value of -Xmx128m and -Xmx128m with this property setting: <br>iscobol.jvm_options=-Xms1024m -Xmx124m <br><br>**Note** - This property does not affect currently running processes because the configuration variables are read after the initial JVM is already started. The command-line is the only place you can set options for the initial JVM. <br><br>The default value is "-Xms128m -Xmx128m". |
| iscobol.literal.numeric.comp (boolean) | **True** = literals are treated as USAGE COMP in LENGTH OF functions and other integer functions.<br>**False** = literals are treated as USAGE DISPLAY in LENGTH OF functions and other integer functions. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.little_endian (boolean)* | **True** = Native numeric data items are stored in Little Endian format.<br>**False** = Native numeric data items are stored in Big Endian format. <br><br>The default value is system dependent. <br><br>This property is deprecated and shouldn’t be set as the current JVMs automatically use the correct endianness. |
| iscobol.logclass | This property specifies an alterante class to manage the trace of the runtime activity. The class must implement the *com.iscobol.logger.Logger* interface. See [Slf4jLogger Class (com.iscobol.logger.Slf4jLogger)](../../../Appendices/Internal-Objects/Slf4jLogger-Class) for further details. |
| iscobol.logfile <span id="logfile"></span> | This property specifies the path of the log file. Backslashes must be doubled. For example, the path "C:\MyLogDir\MyLogFile" would be "C:\\MyLogDir\\MyLogFile". <br><br>Note: To produce a trace log, set [iscobol.tracelevel](#tracelevel) to a non-zero value. <br><br>The isCOBOL framework uses the java.util.logging package, and there are many configuration options. <br><br>For example, you can specify "%h" in the iscobol.logfile and it will be replaced by the user's home directory. <br><br>You can specify %yyyy, %mm, %dd, %hh, %nn, %ss and %cc and they will be replaced with the current year, month, day, hour, minute, second and hundredth of second respectively. <br><br>You can specify a "%u" in the iscobol.logfile and it will be replaced with a unique number at runtime to resolve conflicts. <br><br>The %u is replaced by a unique number, 0, 1, 2, .... The logic to determine the unique number is to use the lowest number that is not in current use by a process. The log files are "locked" by creating a ".lck" file, and are unlocked by deleting that ".lck" file. So if the filename is fred%u.log and fred0.log.lck exists, the process will create fred1.log (and fred1.log.lck). If fred0.log.lck does not exist then the process will overwrite fred0.log. It does not get appended to. <br><br>(The javadoc for FileHandler says "If the FileHandler tries to open the filename and finds the file is currently in use by another process it will increment the unique number field and try again. This will be repeated until FileHandler finds a file name that is not currently in use") <br><br>See https://docs.oracle.com/javase/8/docs/api/index.html?java/util/logging/FileHandler.html for other pattern components and logging properties. <br><br>To include a process ID in the log filename on UNIX/Linux, create a shell script and use $$ to substitute the process id of the current shell. For example, to create a log file named myapp followed by an underscore and the process id of the shell, specify "-Discobol.logfile=myapp_$$.log" on your java command line. |
|  | Note that these log files will accumulate until they are deleted or until the process id wraps around. <br><br>If you do not set iscobol.logfile then the trace log will be written to $ISCOBOL/bin/isrun.log where $ISCOBOL is the isCOBOL installation directory. <br><br>The iscobol.logfile value should not be enclosed in double-quotes, even if there are embedded spaces in the path. On Windows, you can use forward slashes or double-backslashes. For example, any of the following will work: <br><br>iscobol.logfile=C:\\parent dir\\sub dir\\myapp.log<br>iscobol.logfile=C:/parent dir/sub dir/myapp.log<br>iscobol.logfile=/parent dir/sub dir/myapp.log<br>iscobol.logfile=%h/myapp%u.log <br><br>To include portions of the current date and time in the log path name, you can use the following patterns: <br><br>"%yyyy" the current year<br>"%mm" the current month (01-12)<br>"%dd" the current day (01-31)<br>"%hh" the current hour (00-23)<br>"%nn" the current minute (00-59)<br>"%ss" the current second (00-59)<br>"%cc" the current hundred of second (00-99) <br><br>If these patterns are used in the directory part of the path name, the runtime takes care of creating the necessary subfolders, if they don’t exist. <br><br>For example, if you set "iscobol.logfile=%h/logs/%hh%nn/iscobol.log" and launch the runtime at 3:01 PM, you will obtain the following log file: /home/username/logs/1501/iscobol.log. <br><br>In multithread environments, a separate log file for each thread is generated. The name of these log files has the format <name\>.#, where <name\> is the value of the *iscobol.logfile* property and # is a ordinal number assigned by isCOBOL to each new thread. |
| iscobol.logfile.append <span id="logfile_append"></span> | **True** = Append content to the existing log file, if it exists<br>**False** = Overwrite the existing log file, if it exists <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.logfile.maxlen <span id="logfile_maxlen"></span> | This property specifies the maximum number of bytes that can be written in the log file. When this number is reached, the runtime cleans the log file before writing new information. You can set [iscobol.logfile.number](#logfile_number) to a value greater than 1 in order to preserve one of more copies of the log files before they’re erased. <br><br>By default there’s no limit in the size of the log file. |
| iscobol.logfile.number <span id="logfile_number"></span> | This property specifies how many log files can be kept on disk when [iscobol.logfile.maxlen](#logfile_maxlen) is set. The name of these log files is a concatenation between the value of [iscobol.logfile](#logfile) and a progressive number starting from zero. It’s good practice to set this property to a value not less than 2 in order to preserve a bit of history of the program activity. <br><br>The default value is 1, that means only one log on disk. |
| iscobol.logfile.thread (boolean) | **True** = The thread number is traced for every logged operation.<br>**False** = The thread number is not traced in the log. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.memory.alpha_edited (boolean) | **True** = Accept and VALUE clause on alphabetic-edited and alphanumeric-edited items overwrites editing characters.<br>**False** = Editing characters in alphabetic-edited and alphanumeric-edited items are always preserved. <br><br>For example, having the following data item:  <br><br>77 VAR1X-E PIC X/X/X VALUE "ABC". <br><br>If the property is true, VAR1X-E will contain “ABC” otherwise it will contain "A/B/C".<br>The program accepts VAR1X-E and the user types "AAA".<br>If the property is true, VAR1X-E will contain “AAA” otherwise it will contain “A/A/A”. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.national.acu_compatibility (boolean)* | **True** = every MOVE to a PIC N data item performed under [-cndbcs](../../Compiler-and-Runtime/Compiler/Compiler-Options#compatibility-options) option and every MOVE to a PIC G data item store the character in two bytes. <br><br>**False** = only MOVE ALL to an edited PIC N data item performed under [-cndbcs](../../Compiler-and-Runtime/Compiler/Compiler-Options#compatibility-options) option and MOVE ALL to an edited PIC G data item store the character in two bytes. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.os.name | This property specifies the value that is returned in the corresponding data item of the SYSTEM-INFORMATION group item. |
| iscobol.properties.acu_compat (boolean) | **True** = Activates Acucobol-GT compatibility on configuration, that means:<br>• system environment variables have precedence over configuration properties<br>• system environment variables are listed along with configuration properties by the [C$LIST_ENVIRONMENT](../../../Appendices/Library-Routines/C$LIST_ENVIRONMENT/C$LIST_ENVIRONMENT) library routine<br>• file name translation is repeated until no new translation is found<br>• [OPENSAVE-BROWSE-FOLDER](../../../Appendices/Library-Routines/C$OPENSAVEBOX/OPENSAVE-BROWSE-FOLDER) starts from Computer rather than the current directory if opnsav-default-dir isn’t set <br><br>**False** = Doesn’t activate Acucobol-GT compatibility on configuration <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.recursion_data_global (boolean) * | **True** = Working-Storage and FD data are shared between programs called in recursion.<br>**False** = Each recursive program has its own Working-Storage and FD. <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.remote.code_prefix <span id="remote_code_prefix"></span> | This property calls remote programs by specifying the hostname and port on which an isCOBOL Server is listening. Values must begin with "isc://". Multiple values must be separated by "\n". For example: <br><br>isc://hostname1:portnumber1\nisc://hostname2:portnumber2 <br><br>synchronous CALLs are executed by default. <br><br>asynchronous CALLs are also supported using the syntax CALL THREAD. <br><br>CALLs to client programs using the syntax CALL CLIENT as well as opening data files client-side by assigning them to the class “com.iscobol.io.RemoteRelative” are not supported. Remote programs cannot access client resources as it happens in the standard thin client environment. <br><br>If the called programs were compiled with -cp, then the iscp protocol must be used instead of isc, e.g. <br><br>iscp://hostname1:portnumber1\nisc://hostname2:portnumber2 |
