| iscobol.resource.file<br>iscobol.resource.country<br>iscobol.resource.language<br>iscobol.resource.variant | These properties are used to define the name of the resource file for localization. <br><br>The name is composed as follows (square brackets enclose optional elements): file[_language[_country[variant]]].properties <br><br>These properties are loaded along with the program so if you set them dynamically with SET ENVIRONMENT they will affect the called programs and not the current program.<br>A sample of this feature is installed with isCOBOL in the folder $ISCOBOL_HOME/sample/multilanguage. <br><br>These properties affect also the language of message box buttons and framework dialogs (as described at [Language in message boxes and framework dialogs](../../../User-Interface/Working-With-UI-and-Controls/Language-in-message-boxes-and-framework-dialogs)) and the default print page format (as described at [winprint-curr-papersize](../../../Appendices/Library-Routines/WIN$PRINTER/WINPRINT-SET-PRINTER-EX)). |
| iscobol.rm.development_mode (boolean) | **True** = Activates the RM/COBOL development mode.<br>**False** = Disables the RM/COBOL development mode. <br><br>The default value is False. <br><br>**Note** - this setting has currently no effect. |
| iscobol.rundebug * | This property specifies how the Runtime interacts with the remote debuggers. <br><br>**0** = Remote debugging is not possible.<br>**1** = The first program will start and run immediately. The remote debug session waits and listens for a program that is compiled for debug to launch, connecting to the program when it detects it. This is useful when you only want to debug some of your application’s programs.<br>**2** = The runtime framework will start in debug mode, pausing to connect to a remote debugger before running the program. This is useful when all your programs are compiled for debug and you want to start your remote debugger at the very first line of the application. <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.rundebug.auto_pause (boolean) * | **True** = When debugging a remote application, you enter in debug as soon as a program compiled in debug mode is loaded.<br>**False** = When debugging a remote application, you enter in debug only when a breakpoint is reached or the Pause button is pressed on the Graphical Debugger’s tool-bar. <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.rundebug.redirect_streams (boolean) * | The property affects only the remote debugging of a stand-alone program launched with a command like *java -Discobol.rundebug=2 -Discobol.rundebug.redirect_streams=0 PROGRAM_NAME*.<br>It doesn't work in thin client environment or with the stand-alone Debugger. <br><br>**True** = The stdin, stdout and stdserr streams are managed by the Debugger. When an ACCEPT FROM SYSIN is performed, you have to input the data in the Debugger’s [Command Area](../../Debugger/Debugger-Window/Command-Area). The stdout and stderr streams are caught by the Debugger’s [Console](../../Debugger/Debugger-Window/Informatiom-Window) tab, as long as the [Console](../../Debugger/Debugger-Window/Informatiom-Window) is attached; if the [Console](../../Debugger/Debugger-Window/Informatiom-Window) is detached, then stdout and stderr are displayed on the Runtime console.When debugging from the IDE, the stdin, stdout and stdserr streams are managed in the [Console](../../Debugger/Debugger-Window/Informatiom-Window) view.<br>**False** = The stdin, stdout and stderr streams are managed on the Runtime console. For example, when an ACCEPT FROM SYSIN is performed, you have to input the data in the Runtime console. This is particularly useful if the input is passed through a text file on the command line (e.g. iscrun MYPROG < input.txt) or if the console output must be saved to a text file (e.g. iscrun MYPROG > output.txt). <br><br>The default value is True. <br><br>**Note** - this property must be set to False when your’re remote debugging with the [Veryant isCOBOL Extension for Visual Studio Code](../../../../is-cobol-extension-for-VScode/key-topics), otherwise unexpected behaviors may occur. <br><br>(iscobol.redirect_streams is supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.runtime.compile_flags.mandatory * | List of compile options that must have been used. Programs not compiled with these options will not be executed. |
| iscobol.runtime.compile_flags.prohibited * | List of compile options that couldn’t be used. Programs compiled with these options will not be executed. |
| iscobol.runtime.currency * | This property specifies the currency symbol for programs that don’t explicitly define it in their Special-Names. Set it to the desired character. The program must be compiled with the [-sdcs](../../Compiler-and-Runtime/Compiler/Compiler-Options#source-options) option for this feature to take effect. |
| iscobol.runtime.decimal_point_is_comma (boolean) * | **True** = Every program compiled with the [-sddp](../../Compiler-and-Runtime/Compiler/Compiler-Options#source-options) option shows the comma as the decimal separator and the dot as the thousand separator, regardless of the DECIMAL-POINT clause in the Special-Names.<br>**False** = Every program compiled with the [-sddp](../../Compiler-and-Runtime/Compiler/Compiler-Options#source-options) option shows the dot as the decimal separator and the comma as the thousand separator, regardless of the DECIMAL-POINT clause in the Special-Names. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.runtime.native.dynamic.ignore_errors (boolean) * | **True** = No errors are returned if the dyncall native library cannot be loaded during startup.<br>**False** = An error is returned if the dyncall native library cannot be loaded during startup. <br><br>The default value is True. <br><br>Since the cause of the error may be shown at the bottom of the exception stack, when you set this property to false it’s suggested you also set [iscobol.exception.java (boolean)](#exception_java) to true. <br><br>(iscobol.runtime.native.ignore_errors is supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.runtime.native.static.ignore_errors (boolean) * | **True** = No errors are returned if the stacall native library cannot be loaded during startup.<br>**False** = An error is returned if the stacall native library cannot be loaded during startup. <br><br>Since the cause of the error may be shown at the bottom of the exception stack, when you set this property to false it’s suggested you also set [iscobol.exception.java (boolean)](#exception_java) to true. <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.runtime.preload * <span id="runtime_preload"></span> | List of programs that should automatically be loaded at startup.<br>Multiple program names must be separated by space or comma.<br>The preloading is supported for standard programs and programs with entry points, but not for CLASS-ID programs.<br>The program class is loaded in the JVM Metaspace allowing the runtime to save time when the program will be executed later.<br>If the preloading fails, no error is raised, but the event is traced in the log. |
| iscobol.runtime.version | This property returns the version number of the Runtime Framework. |
| iscobol.shared_dlopen_null (boolean) | **True** = C functions called by the COBOL program are also searched for in the current process.<br>**False** = C functions called by the COBOL program are not searched for in the current process. <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.shared_library_list * | List of dynamic libraries that should automatically be loaded at startup. This feature allows you to avoid calling the library name before using its functions in the program. Multiple paths must be separated by \n character sequence or by the current operating system path separator. |
| iscobol.station | This property specifies the value that is returned in the corresponding data item of the SYSTEM-INFORMATION group item. |
| iscobol.substring.check * <span id="substring_check"></span> | **-1** = String boundaries are checked at Runtime in order to provide more details in case of "out of bounds" errors. If a program addresses an invalid character position, an error message is written to the log ([iscobol.tracelevel](#tracelevel) must be set to a value greater than zero). The error message informs about the data item name and the problematic character position.<br>**0** = String boundaries are not checked. If a program addresses an item that is outside the valid range, a generic "out of bounds" error message is shown and the program exits.<br>**1** = String boundaries are checked at Runtime in order to provide more details in case of "out of bounds" errors. If a program addresses an invalid character position, an error message is shown and the program exits. The error message informs about the data item name and the problematic character position. <br><br>**Note** - for an accurate error message, program shouldn’t be compiled with the [-ostrip](../../Compiler-and-Runtime/Compiler/Compiler-Options#output-options) option. <br><br>The default value is 0. <br><br>(iscobol.substring_check is supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.substring.unbound (boolean) * | **True** = data items can be referenced over their bounds as long as [iscobol.substring.check *](#substring_check) is not enabled.<br>**False** = data items can’t be referenced over their bounds. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.substring.zero_len_all (boolean) * | **True** = a reference modifier with length = 0 behaves as if length was omitted (e.g. src-item(2:0) behaves like src-item(2:), so that the content of src-item from byte 2 to the end is returned).<br>**False** = a reference modifier with length = 0 returns a variable whose length is 0 (e.g. src-item(2:0) returns ""). <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.switches * | This property sets the switches as a sequence of numbers separated by commas. <br><br>For example, if the program contains 10 switches and you wish to activate the first two and the fifth, set iscobol.switches=1,2,5 <br><br>If the the switch name is a letter, such as    <br>switch "A" ...    <br>switch "C" ...    <br>switch "Z" ...<br>Then you need to specify the ordinal number of the letter in order to activate the corresponding switch, e.g. iscobol.switches=1,3,26. |
| iscobol.terminal.info.name<br>iscobol.terminal.info.reverse<br>iscobol.terminal.info.blink<br>iscobol.terminal.info.underline<br>iscobol.terminal.info.dual_intensity<br>iscobol.terminal.info.132column<br>iscobol.terminal.info.color<br>iscobol.terminal.info.drawing<br>iscobol.terminal.info.screen.lines<br>iscobol.terminal.info.screen.columns<br>iscobol.terminal.info.printer<br>iscobol.terminal.info.attributes<br>iscobol.terminal.info.graphic<br>iscobol.terminal.info.screen.usable.height<br>iscobol.terminal.info.screen.usable.width<br>iscobol.terminal.info.screen.physical.height<br>iscobol.terminal.info.screen.physical.width | These properties set the values returned in the corresponding data items of the TERMINAL-ABILITIES group item. |
| iscobol.terminal.info.refresh_monitor (boolean) * | **True** = The runtime updates the screen resolution information each time TERMINAL-INFO is accepted from TERMINAL-ABILITIES.<br>**False** = The runtime obtains the screen resolution information only the first time TERMINAL-INFO is accepted from TERMINAL-ABILITIES. The stored values are returned to the next ACCEPT statements. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.tracelevel <span id="tracelevel"></span> | This property allows the user to define the events to be traced. Valid values (which can be added together) are:<br>**1** = Settings of configuration properties and JVM options. Configuration properties set in the external environment are not traced in the logfile. Only the properties found in the configuration file, the properties set in the runtime command-line and the properties set by the programs are traced. The list of processed configuration files is also traced. For security reasons, the value of these settings is shown as encrypted in the log file:    <br>iscobol.file.encryption.key    <br>iscobol.file.index.password    <br>iscobol.jdbc.password    <br>iscobol.net.ssl.key_store_password    <br>iscobol.net.ssl.trust_store_password    <br>iscobol.print.attribute.owner_password    <br>iscobol.print.attribute.user_password    <br>iscobol.sqlserver.password    <br>iscobol.user.password<br>**2** = Program starts and program ends.<br>**4** = Paragraph starts and paragraph ends as well as method starts and method ends (program must be compiled with -d or -dx).<br>**8** = File I/O activities.<br>**16** = Content of keys (works only in conjunction with 8).<br>**32** = Content of record (works only in conjunction with 8).<br>**64** = Client / Server activity in terms of connection and disconnection (isCOBOL Server environment).<br>**128** = RPC calls for communication between client and server (isCOBOL Server environment).<br>**256** = SQL activity.<br>**512** = Complementary information to the one shown by pressing the [Alt+Pause keyboard combination](../../../Appendices/Troubleshooting/Runtime-Issues#alt+pause-keyboard-configuration).<br>**1024** = Library routine starts and library routine ends. It works only in conjunction with the value 2.<br>**2048** = Internal information useful to programmers. This value activates the following traces:<br>• ESQL cursors life cycle<br>• Errors in the loading of fonts specified by iscobol.font settings  <br><br>The default value is 0. <br><br>To produce a trace log, set [iscobol.tracelevel](#tracelevel) to a non-zero value. With the default value of 0, the system does not create a log since it would be empty. <br><br>The following are some useful settings: <br><br>*iscobol.tracelevel*=3 includes config settings and program starts and ends.<br>*iscobol.tracelevel*=7 includes config, program starts/ends, and paragraph starts/ends.<br>*iscobol.tracelevel*=11 includes config, program starts/ends and file i/o (i.e. everything except for paragraph starts/ends).<br>*iscobol.tracelevel*=43 includes config, program starts/ends and file i/o including the content of read records.<br>*iscobol.tracelevel*=15 includes config, program and paragraph starts/ends, and file i/o.<br>*iscobol.tracelevel*=63 traces everything of the above. <br><br>You can add custom information to the logfile by calling the [C$WRITELOG](../../../Appendices/Library-Routines/C$WRITELOG) routine. <br><br>The log file name is controlled by the property [iscobol.logfile](#logfile). If the property is not set, then a file named “iscobol0.log” is created in the temp folder. <br><br>When the trace level is greater than zero, the stack of blocking exceptions is captured in the log in addition to being displayed on the user interface. |
| iscobol.upper_lower_method | This property defines the strategy used by the Framework in order to convert a string either to upper case or to lower case. <br><br>Possible values are:<br>**1** = use the methods toUpperCase() and toLowerCase() of the java.lang.String class<br>**2** = use the methods toUpperCase() and toLowerCase() of the java.lang.Character class <br><br>The default value is 1. <br><br>In most cases, the result is the same. A difference may occur for some special characters; for example, the symbol "ß" is translated to "SS" when made upper case with method 1. |
| iscobol.use_for_debugging (boolean) * | **True** = Enable the USE FOR DEBUGGING declarative for programs compiled with the [-cv](../../Compiler-and-Runtime/Compiler/Compiler-Options#compatibility-options) option.<br>**False** = The USE FOR DEBUGGING declarative is ignored. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.utf16.little_endian (boolean) * | **True** = The content of PIC N items is stored in UTF-16 Little Endian encoding.<br>**False** = The content of PIC N items is stored in UTF-16 Big Endian encoding. <br><br>The default value is False. <br><br>This property is useful only to pass UTF-16 data to C functions, according to the endianness expected by the C function. |

#### RemoteCompiler Configuration

RemoteCompiler properties cannot be set by SET ENVIRONMENT within the program. They must appear in the external configuration.

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.remotecompiler.compileonserver (boolean) | **True** = After precompiling, the RemoteCompiler compiles the translated cbl to class on the server and sends the resulting class along with the translated cbl to the client.<br>**False** = After precompiling, the RemoteCompiler sends the translated cbl to the client. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.remotecompiler.conf | This property specifies the name of the RemoteCompiler configuration file. |
| iscobol.remotecompiler.createerrorfiles (boolean) | **True** = The RemoteCompiler will ask preprocessors to create error files.<br>**False** = No error files will be created by preprocessors. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.remotecompiler.createlistingfiles (boolean) | **True** = The RemoteCompiler will ask preprocessors to create listing files.<br>**False** = No listing files will be created by preprocessors. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.remotecompiler.host | This property specifies the name of the server where the RemoteCompiler is active and listening. The default value is ‘localhost’. |
| iscobol.remotecompiler.port | This property specifies the port on the server where the RemoteCompiler is listening. The default value is 11999. |
| iscobol.remotecompiler.preprocnames | This property specifies the precompilers that will run server side during a remote compilation. Multiple names must be separated by commas.<br>The special value “ALL” can be used to instruct the RemoteCompiler to execute every preprocessor defined in its configuration. The same effect is obtained by omitting this property. The special value “NONE” can be used to avoid pre-compiling on the server machine and perform a standard COBOL compilation only. |
| iscobol.remotecompiler.translateddir | This property specifies an alternate folder in which to store translated files. By default they’re stored in the same folder as the original source file. |

#### WebDirect Configuration

WebDirect properties cannot be set by SET ENVIRONMENT within the program. They must appear in the external configuration.

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.wd2.additional_javascript | This property allows users to load additional java scripts to be used in the web application. The js files specified in the property are loaded during startup, and are available throughout the application. The js files must be placed in the resources/js subfolder of the web application, and only their file name, complete with extension, must be specified as value of the property (i.e.: *iscobol.wd2.additional_javascript=script1.js*). Multiple values must be separated by \n or by the system path separator (i.e.: *iscobol.wd2.additional_javascript=script1.js\nscript2.js*). |
| iscobol.wd2.additional_stylesheet <span id="wd2_additional_stylesheet"></span> | This property allows users to load additional CSS stylesheets to be used in the web application. The CSS files specified in the property are loaded during startup, and are available throughout the application. The stylesheet files must be placed in the resources/css subfolder of the web application, and only their file name, complete with extension, must be specified as value of the property (i.e.: *iscobol.wd2.additional_stylesheet=mycustom.css*). Multiple values must be separated by \n or by the system path separator (i.e.: *iscobol.wd2.additional_stylesheet=mycustom.css\nmycustom2.css*). |
| iscobol.wd2.doubleclick_speed | This property specifies the amount of milliseconds between two mouse clicks to consider them a double click. <br><br>The default value is 300. |
| iscobol.wd2.style <span id="wd2_style"></span> | This property allows basic styling for controls in the application. <br>Changing the values will cause runtime-generated CSS stylesheets to be created with different parameters, if no values are assigned to CSS-BASE-STYLE-NAME or to CSS-STYLE-NAME. <br>Allowed values are:<br>• **bs** = Bootstrap styling (see http://getbootstrap.com/ for details)<br>• **default** = default styling<br>• **os** = OS styling<br>• **trendy** = Trendy styling <br><br>These values refer to ZK embedded styles. <br><br>The default value is *default*. |
| iscobol.wd2.wait_message | This property specifies the wait message that is shown by WebDirect during processing. <br><br>The default value is: "Loading... Please wait until this screen is completely loaded." |
| iscobol.wd2.mobile_numpad (boolean) | This property takes effect when a WebDirect application is used from a mobile device. <br><br>**True** = The numpad is shown instead of the standard keyboard when a numeric input is required.<br>**False** = The standard keyboard is shown for every kind of input. <br><br>The default value is False. |

#### HTTPClient Configuration.

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.http.ignore_certificates (boolean) * | **True** = If the handshaking fails due to invalid certificates, continue and connect anyway.<br>**False** = If the handshaking fails due to invalid certificates, stop. <br><br>The default value is False. <br><br>See [Connecting to a SSL-enabled web service](../../../../is-cobol-EIS/Appendices/Connecting-to-a-SSL-enabled-web-service) for more information. <br><br>Note - this property should be set to true only for test purposes. It’s not good practice to ignore handshaking errors. |
| iscobol.httpclient.logfile | This property specifies the pathname of the log file where the HTTPClient activity is traced if [iscobol.httpclient.logging (boolean)](#httpclient_logging) is set to true.<br>Relative pathnames are resolved according to the runtime working directory.<br>The log includes the request date and time, the request content, the response code, the response headers and the response content.<br>If the file already exists, the new content is appended to it. |
| iscobol.httpclient.logging (boolean) <span id="httpclient_logging"></span> | **True** = Trace the HTTPClient activity.<br>**False** = Don’t trace the HTTPClient activity. <br><br>The default value is False. |

#### HTTPHandler Configuration

(\*) The asterisk after the property name means that the property is static, it’s inquired only once by the Framework and then changing it using the SET ENVIRONMENT statement has no effect.

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.http.cgi_clear_missing_values (boolean) * | **True** = Set the value of numeric data items to zero and non-numeric data items to spaces if a CGI variable is empty or does not exist.<br>**False** = Don’t clear the value of data items if a CGI variable is empty or does not exist. <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.http.cgi_content_type * | This property specifies the MIME type of the CGI output. <br><br>The default value is "text/html". |
| iscobol.http.cgi_no_cache (boolean) * | **True** = "Pragma: no-cache" is added to the HTTP response header.<br>**False** = "Pragma: no-cache" is not added to the HTTP response header. The default value is True. |
| iscobol.http.cookies_as_fields (boolean) * | **True** = All the cookies can be read through the accept() method as if they were input fields. The cookie name is case sensitive.<br>**False** = Cookies can’t be read through the accept() method. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.http.form.encoding * | This property specifies the character set used by HTML forms that send data through HTTP (e.g. UTF-8). If not set, the framework tries to calculate the current character set itself. |
| iscobol.http.html_template_prefix * <span id="http_html_template_prefix"></span> | This property lists the paths which HTML files are searched by *HTTPHandler:>processHtmlFile()*. Multiple values must be separated by the line feed character or by the current operating system path separator.<br>Within configuration files, the line feed character is "\n". In COBOL programs, the line feed character is x"0a". |
| iscobol.http.mtom_enabled (boolean) * <span id="http_mtom_enabled"></span> | **True** = MTOM (Message Transmission Optimization Mechanism) enabled: a receiver MUST accept both a non-optimized and an optimized message, and a sender MAY send an optimized message, or a non-optimized message. The heuristics used by a sender to determine whether to use optimization or not are implementation-specific.<br>**False** = MTOM (Message Transmission Optimization Mechanism) disabled. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.http.servlet.prefix | This property specifies the prefix to put before the program name in the URL in order to build the name of the actual COBOL program. For example, given the following URL: <br>[*http://localhost:8080/myservlet/servlet/isCobol(PROG)*](https://www.example.com) , if *iscobol.http.servlet.prefix=soap*, the framework will run the program SOAPPROG instead of PROG. |
| iscobol.http.stateless (boolean) * | **True** = Programs are automatically cancelled from memory when the main program returns (stateless).<br>**False** = Programs remain in memory unless explicitly cancelled by a CANCEL statement (stateful). <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.http.upload.directory * <span id="http_upload_directory"></span> | This property specifies the directory where files uploaded through HTTP must be stored. A HTTP error is returned if the user tries to upload a file whose size exceeds the value of this property. <br><br>By default, the user temp directory is used. |
| iscobol.http.upload.max_size * | This property specifies the maximum size in bytes allowed for file upload through HTTP. <br><br>The default value is 1048576. |
| iscobol.http.upload.prefix * <span id="http_upload_prefix"></span> | This property specifies an optional prefix that must be applied to the name of the files uploaded through HTTP. |
| iscobol.http.value_prefix_colon (boolean) <span id="http_value_prefix_colon"></span> | **True** = Embedded values in the HTML code are expected to be prefixed by colon, E.g. *<html\>hello :myvar world</html\>*.<br>**False** = Embedded values in the HTML code are expected to be enclosed by double percent sign, E.g. *<html\>hello %%myvar%% world</html\>*. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.rest.default_stream <span id="rest_default_stream"></span> | This property specifies the default Content-type to be used when Content-type is not available in the HTTP header. It is considered by the HTTPHandler methods [acceptEx](../../../../is-cobol-EIS/Appendices/HTTPHandler-class/acceptEx) and [displayEx](../../../../is-cobol-EIS/Appendices/HTTPHandler-class/displayEx) as well as the HTTPClient methods [doPostEx](../../../../is-cobol-EIS/Appendices/HTTPClient-class/doPostEx) and [getResponseEx](../../../../is-cobol-EIS/Appendices/HTTPClient-class/getResponseEx). <br><br>The possible values are "xml" or "json". <br><br>The default value is "json". |
| iscobol.rest.log.<methodName\> (boolean) | **True** = Enables logging of the REST activity at global level.<br>**False** = No log of the REST activity is performed at global level. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.rest.log. (boolean) | **True** = Enables logging of the REST activity for the specified method name .<br>**False** = No log of the REST activity is performed the specified method name. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.rest.log.folder | This property specifies the folder where REST activity log files are generated.<br>The log file name is generated dynamically using the following pattern: *{methodname}-{SESSIONID}.log*. <br><br>Backslashes must be doubled. For example, the path "C:\MyLogDir" would be "C:\\MyLogDir". <br><br>The isCOBOL framework uses the java.util.logging package, and there are many configuration options. <br><br>For example, you can specify "%h" in the iscobol.logfile and it will be replaced by the user's home directory. <br><br>You can specify %yyyy, %mm, %dd, %hh, %nn, %ss and %cc and they will be replaced with the current year, month, day, hour, minute, second and hundredth of second respectively. |
| iscobol.soap.log.<methodName\> (boolean) | **True** = Enables logging of the SOAP activity at global level.<br>**False** = No log of the SOAP activity is performed at global level. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.soap.log. (boolean) | **True** = Enables logging of the SOAP activity for the specified method name .<br>**False** = No log of the SOAP activity is performed the specified method name. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.soap.log.folder | This property specifies the folder where SOAP activity log files are generated.<br>The log file name is generated dynamically using the following pattern: *{methodname}-{SESSIONID}.log*. <br><br>Backslashes must be doubled. For example, the path "C:\MyLogDir" would be "C:\\MyLogDir". <br><br>The isCOBOL framework uses the java.util.logging package, and there are many configuration options. <br><br>For example, you can specify "%h" in the iscobol.logfile and it will be replaced by the user's home directory. <br><br>You can specify %yyyy, %mm, %dd, %hh, %nn, %ss and %cc and they will be replaced with the current year, month, day, hour, minute, second and hundredth of second respectively. |
| iscobol.soap.wsdl.location <span id="soap_wsdl_location"></span> | This property specifies the location of the wsdl file. It should point to a file system path where wsdl files are copied, ie: <br><br>iscobol.soap.wsdl.location=/path/to/wsdl  <br><br>Both full paths and relative paths are allowed. Relative paths are relative to the servlet container service working directory. <br><br>The servlet appends the webservice name and ".wdsl" to this path to form a path name, which, if found, is then downloaded.<br>If the file is not found or the property is not set, and HTTP error 404 is returned. <br><br>The download can be achieved using an url such as: <br><br>[http://localhost:8080/test/servlet/SONGS?wsdl](https://www.example.com) |

#### User Interface Configuration

(\*) The asterisk after the property name means that the property is static, it’s inquired only once by the Framework and then changing it using the SET ENVIRONMENT statement has no effect.

##### Graphical User Interface (GUI)

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.accept_timeout | This property causes all ACCEPT and DISPLAY MESSAGE BOX statements to time out just as if the BEFORE TIME clause was present. The property value specifies the timeout period, in seconds. <br>This timeout is applied to every statement that can have a BEFORE TIME clause specified for it unless such clause has already been explicitly coded for the statement. <br><br>The default value is 0, meaning no timeout value will be applied. <br><br>When this property is set every ACCEPT is performed on a window and the runtime will no longer be able to ACCEPT user input from the console. |
| iscobol.auto_input_mode (boolean) * | **True** = The IME (Input Method Editor) activates itself automatically when the focus moves to an input field associated with a national data item.<br>**False** = The IME (Input Method Editor) doesn’t activate itself automatically when the focus moves to an input field associated with a national data item. It’s the user's duty to activate the IME before inputting data. <br><br>This property has effect when the active keyboard supports IME (for example if the active keyboard is Japanese) and it affects both graphical screens and character based screens. <br><br>The default value is False |
| iscobol.background_intensity * | This property defines the default intensity of the background color of the windows. <br>Valid values are: <br><br>**0** = The default intensity for the output device is used.<br>**1** = The background intensity is forced to low.<br>**2** = The background intensity is forced to high. <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.bitmap_scale.best_quality (boolean) | **True** = The runtime tries to obtain the best quality during the scale of a bitmap, even if the process may require more time.<br>**False** = The runtime scales bitmaps without particular optimizations, completing the process in the shortest time possible. <br><br>This property affects the [W$SCALE](../../../Appendices/Library-Routines/W$SCALE) routine and the [Bitmap-Scale](../../../User-Interface/Controls-Reference/BITMAP/Properties/Bitmap-Scale) property of the Bitmap control. <br><br>The default value is False |
| iscobol.bitmap.load_method | This property specifies which internal method must be used to load a bitmap.<br>The standard method used by default is suitable for most bitmap files.<br>You might consider using the alternative method when the standard method fails.<br>Valid values are:<br>**1** = Use the standard method.<br>**2** = Use the alternative method. <br><br>The default value is 1. |
| iscobol.colormap.default * | This property associates a color for DISPLAY without any attributes. <br>The value is the sum between *ForegroundColor* and *BackgroundColor* described in [Using standard COBOL values](../../../User-Interface/Working-With-UI-and-Controls/Creating-a-control/Color-management/Using-standard-COBOL-values/Using-standard-COBOL-values). <br><br>For example if you want a blue foreground on a white background, set *iscobol.colormap.default=258* where 258 is the result of the sum between bckgrnd-white (256) and blue (2). |
| iscobol.colormap.high * | This property associates a color for DISPLAY with the HIGH attribute. <br>The value is the sum between *ForegroundColor* and *BackgroundColor* described in [Using standard COBOL values](../../../User-Interface/Working-With-UI-and-Controls/Creating-a-control/Color-management/Using-standard-COBOL-values/Using-standard-COBOL-values). <br><br>For example if you want a bright blue foreground on a white background, set *iscobol.colormap.high=266* where 266 is the result of the sum between bckgrnd-white (256) and bright-blue (10). |
| iscobol.colormap.low * | This property associates a color for DISPLAY with the LOW attribute. <br>The value is the sum between *ForegroundColor* and *BackgroundColor* described in [Using standard COBOL values](../../../User-Interface/Working-With-UI-and-Controls/Creating-a-control/Color-management/Using-standard-COBOL-values/Using-standard-COBOL-values). <br><br>For example if you want a blue foreground on a white background, set *iscobol.colormap.low=258* where 258 is the result of the sum between bckgrnd-white (256) and blue (2). |
| iscobol.font.<fontname\>.cell * | This property specifies a custom cell size associated with the font <fontname\>. The runtime does not calculate the cell size as usual but uses the values specified by the user through the property instead. The value format is x,y where x and y are the width and height of the cell in pixels. <br><br><fontname\> identifies a font by name, state and size. Upper-case characters are allowed here. State and size are optional and must be separated by dash if specified. If the font name contains spaces, then the \ character must be used to escape the spaces when the setting is done in the configuration file, while the whole setting must be enclosed between quotes if the setting is done in the command line. For example, if you want to force the cell size 10x10 for the Courier New font in bold state with size 12, you will write the following entry in the configuration file: <br><br>iscobol.font.Courier\ New-bold-12.cell=10,10 <br><br>Each time the program loads the above font with the W$FONT routine and uses it on a graphical window, the custom cell size will be used. |
| iscobol.font.default * <span id="font_default"></span> | This property specifies the font name to be used for the quick-loaded font "DEFAULT".<br><br>The value format is: FontName-FontStyle-FontDim <br><br>• *FontName* is the name of the font. The default value is Sans Serif.<br>• *FontStyle* is the style of the font such as bold, italic, or bolditalic.<br>• *FontDim* is the dimension of the font. <br><br>If *FontName* cannot be found, then *FontStyle* and *FontDim* are applied to the standard default font (Sans Serif); no error is raised. |
| iscobol.font.default.cell * | This property specifies a custom cell size for the default font. The value format is *x*,*y* where *x* and *y* are the width and height of the cell in pixels. |
| iscobol.font.fixed * <span id="font_fixed"></span> | This property specifies the font name to be used for the quick-loaded font "FIXED".<br>The value format is: FontName-FontStyle-FontDim <br><br>• *FontName* is the name of the font. The default value is Monospaced.<br>• *FontStyle* is the style of the font such as bold, italic or bolditalic.<br>• *FontDim* is the dimension of the font. <br><br>If *FontName* cannot be found, then *FontStyle* and *FontDim* are applied to the standard fixed font (Monospaced); no error is raised. |
| iscobol.font.fixed.cell * | This property specifies a custom cell size for the fixed font. The value format is *x*,*y* where *x* and *y* are the width and height of the cell in pixels. |
| iscobol.font.handling * | This property controls the aliasing applied to fonts. <br><br>The value of this property identifies a hint that the runtime passes to the underlying Swing control. If the Swing control honors the hint, you will notice a different font aliasing. <br><br>Possible hints are:<br>• ANTIALIAS_DEFAULT: rendering is done with a default antialiasing mode chosen by the implementation.<br>• ANTIALIAS_ON: rendering is done with antialiasing.<br>• ANTIALIAS_OFF: rendering is done without antialiasing. <br><br>Possible values for this property are:<br>**0** = The hint ANTIALIAS_DEFAULT is used for all controls.<br>**1** = The hint ANTIALIAS_ON is used for all controls<br>**2** = The hint ANTIALIAS_OFF is used for all controls<br>**3** = The ANTIALIAS_OFF hint is applied to Frame, List-Box, and Grid controls, consistent with its behavior in version 2013 R2 and earlier. <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.font.large * <span id="font_large"></span> | This property specifies the font name to be used for the quick-loaded font "LARGE".<br>The value format is: FontName-FontStyle-FontDim <br><br>*FontName* is the name of the font. The default value is SansSerif.<br>*FontStyle* is the style of the font such as bold, italic or bolditalic.<br>*FontDim* is the dimension of the font. <br><br>If *FontName* cannot be found, then *FontStyle* and *FontDim* are applied to the standard large font (Sans Serif); no error is raised. |
| iscobol.font.large.cell * | This property specifies a custom cell size for the large font. The value format is *x*,*y* where *x* and *y* are the width and height of the cell in pixels. |
| iscobol.font.medium * <span id="font_medium"></span> | This property specifies the font name to be used for the quick-loaded font "MEDIUM".<br>The value format is: FontName-FontStyle-FontDim <br><br>*FontName* is the name of the font. The default value is SansSerif.<br>*FontStyle* is the style of the font such as bold, italic or bolditalic.<br>*FontDim* is the dimension of the font. <br><br>If *FontName* cannot be found, then *FontStyle* and *FontDim* are applied to the standard medium font (Sans Serif); no error is raised. |
| iscobol.font.medium.cell * | This property specifies a custom cell size for the medium font. The value format is *x*,*y* where *x* and *y* are the width and height of the cell in pixels. |
| iscobol.font.small * <span id="font_small"></span> | This property specifies the font name to be used for the quick-loaded font "SMALL".<br>The value format is: FontName-FontStyle-FontDim <br><br>*FontName* is the name of the font. The default value is SansSerif.<br>*FontStyle* is the style of the font such as bold, italic or bolditalic.<br>*FontDim* is the dimension of the font. <br><br>If *FontName* cannot be found, then *FontStyle* and *FontDim* are applied to the standard small font (Sans Serif); no error is raised. |
| iscobol.font.small.cell * | This property specifies a custom cell size for the small font. The value format is *x*,*y* where *x* and *y* are the width and height of the cell in pixels. |
| iscobol.font.traditional * <span id="font_traditional"></span> | This property specifies the font name to be used for the quick-loaded font "TRADITIONAL".<br>The value format is: FontName-FontStyle-FontDim <br><br>*FontName* is the name of the font. The default value is Monospaced.<br>*FontStyle* is the style of the font such as bold, italic or bolditalic.<br>*FontDim* is the dimension of the font. <br><br>If *FontName* cannot be found, then *FontStyle* and *FontDim* are applied to the standard traditional font (Monospaced); no error is raised. |
| iscobol.font.traditional.cell * | This property specifies a custom cell size for the traditional font. The value format is *x*,*y* where *x* and *y* are the width and height of the cell in pixels. |
| iscobol.foreground_intensity * | This property defines the default intensity of the foreground color of the windows. Valid values are: <br><br>**0** = The default intensity for the output device is used.<br>**1** = The foreground intensity is forced to low.<br>**2** = The foreground intensity is forced to high. <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.gui.accept.before_time.repeat (boolean) | **True** = The Accept timer is reset after each time the user inputs a new digit.<br>**False** = The Accept timer stops as soon as the user inputs the first digit. <br><br>The default value is False |
| iscobol.gui.apply_window_color (booloean)* | **True** = Control whose colors are not specified by the program inherit colors from the parent window.<br>**False** = Control whose colors are not specified by the program inherit colors from the Look And Feel. <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.gui.column_separation | This property specifies the default separation distance between columns in List-Box and Grid. The value is expressed in 10ths of characters. <br><br>Default value is 5. |
| iscobol.gui.*Control*.event <span id="gui_controlevent"></span> | This property specifies the value of [Event-List](../../../Appendices/Graphical-Control-List#event_list) property for all controls of class *Control* which [Event-List](../../../Appendices/Graphical-Control-List#event_list) property is not specified. <br><br>This property has effect only in WebDirect environment and overrides [iscobol.gui.events_list](#gui_events_list). <br><br>*Control* can be one of the following:<br>• bitmap<br>• checkbox<br>• combobox<br>• dateentry<br>• entryfield<br>• grid<br>• listbox<br>• pushbutton<br>• radiobutton<br>• scrollbar<br>• tab<br>• treeview<br>• webbrowser <br><br>The value of this property is the list of numeric values of event constants as defined in the [isgui.def](../../../Appendices/Copybooks/isgui.def) copybook. Multiple values must be separated by comma. |
| iscobol.gui.cscompress <span id="gui_cscompress"></span> | This property allows you to activate the compression of data that’s exchanged between the UI manager (client) and the code manager (server). It affects both stand alone and thin client executions.<br>The server sends buffered data to the client when executing one of the following statements<br>• ACCEPT<br>• INQUIRE<br>• SET INPUT WINDOW<br>• SET I-O WINDOW<br>• MODFIY *window-handle* VISIBLE\|ENABLED when [iscobol.gui.cstimeout *](#gui_cstimeout) has expired or when the buffer size reaches the value specified by [iscobol.gui.csmaxbuffersize *](#gui_csmaxbuffersize). <br><br>This property affects also the bufferization of print jobs. <br><br>The compression is performed only when the data size is equal or greater than the value specified by [iscobol.gui.csminsizecompress *](#gui_csminsizecompress). <br>Valid values are: <br><br>**0** = no compression<br>**1** = best compression<br>**2** = fastest compression <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.gui.csmaxbuffersize <span id="gui_csmaxbuffersize"></span>  | This property sets the maximum size (in bytes) of the buffered data for the communication between the UI manager (client) and the code manager (server). It affects both stand alone and thin client executions. The server sends buffered data to the client when executing one of the following statements<br>• ACCEPT<br>• INQUIRE<br>• SET INPUT WINDOW<br>• SET I-O WINDOW<br>• MODIFY *window-handle* VISIBLE\|ENABLED when [iscobol.gui.cstimeout *](#gui_cstimeout) has expired or when the buffer is full. <br><br>This property affects also the bufferization of print jobs. <br><br>Setting this property to a lower value will result in more frequent updates to the user interface. <br><br>A high value reduces the communication between client and server, but consumes more memory. A low value increases the communication between client and server, but saves memory. <br><br>The default value is 1048576. |
| iscobol.gui.csminsizecompress * <span id="gui_csminsizecompress"></span> | This property specifies the minimum size in bytes for the compression of the client-server buffer activated by the property [iscobol.gui.cscompress *](#gui_cscompress). Only when this amount of bytes is reached the compression is applied. <br><br>The default value is 32768. |
| iscobol.gui.cstimeout * <span id="gui_cstimeout"></span> | This property sets the timeout (in milliseconds) for the communication between the UI manager (client) and the code manager (server). It affects both stand alone and thin client executions. The server sends buffered data to the client when executing one of the following statements<br>• ACCEPT<br>• INQUIRE<br>• SET INPUT WINDOW<br>• SET I-O WINDOW<br>• MODFIY *window-handle* VISIBLE\|ENABLED when the buffer size reaches the value specified by [iscobol.gui.csmaxbuffersize *](#gui_csmaxbuffersize) or when the timeout specified by this property has expired. <br><br>This property doesn’t affect the bufferization of print jobs. <br><br>Setting this property to a lower value will result in more frequent updates to the user interface. <br><br>A high value reduces the communication between client and server, but consumes more memory. A low value increases the communication between client and server, but saves memory. <br><br>This property is automatically set to zero by the isCOBOL Debugger during the stand-alone debug. During the remote debug is your duty to set this property to zero, otherwise you might not see the result of a DISPLAY or MODIFY as soon as you step into the statement. <br><br>Default value is 500. |
| iscobol.gui.curr_bcolor <span id="gui_curr_bcolor"></span> | This property specifies the background color of the current control. It affects ComboBox and EntryField controls. The runtime automatically applies the color when the control get focus and removes the color when the control loses focus.<br>The property accepts numeric values of standard and RGB colors. <br>See [Color management](../../../User-Interface/Working-With-UI-and-Controls/Creating-a-control/Color-management/Color-management) for the list of possible values.<br>The property is ignored by read-only fields. <br><br>For example, if you want a bright blue background on the current field, set *iscobol.gui.curr_bcolor=9*, or, if you prefer to use RGB values, set *iscobol.gui.curr_bcolor=-255* <br><br>(iscobol.gui.curr_ef_bcolor is supported for backward compatibility and it affects only EntryFields) |
| iscobol.gui.curr_border_color | This property specifies the border color of the current control. It affects only EntryField controls. The runtime automatically applies the color when the control get focus and removes the color when the control loses focus.<br>The property accepts numeric values of standard and RGB colors. <br>See [Color management](../../../User-Interface/Working-With-UI-and-Controls/Creating-a-control/Color-management/Color-management) for the list of possible values. <br><br>For example, if you want a bright blue border on the current field, set *iscobol.gui.curr_border_color=9*, or, if you prefer to use RGB values, set *iscobol.gui.curr_border_color=-255* |
| iscobol.gui.curr_border_width | This property specifies the width in pixels of the border of the current control. It affects only EntryField controls. The runtime automatically changes the border width when the control get focus and restores the original width when the control loses focus. <br><br>This property should be set to a list of 4 numeric values separated by space that identify the top border, the left border, the bottom border and the right border widths respectively.<br>For example, in order to make the top and bottom border bigger when the control gets the focus, set *iscobol.gui.curr_border_width=5 15 1*. |
| iscobol.gui.curr_fcolor <span id="gui_curr_fcolor"></span> | This property specifies the foreground color of the current control. It affects ComboBox and EntryField controls. The runtime automatically applies the color when the control get focus and removes the color when the control loses focus.<br>The property accepts numeric values of standard and RGB colors. <br>See [Color management](../../../User-Interface/Working-With-UI-and-Controls/Creating-a-control/Color-management/Color-management) for the list of possible values.<br>The property is ignored by read-only fields. <br><br>For example, if you want a bright blue foreground on the current field, set *iscobol.gui.curr_fcolor=9*, or, if you prefer to use RGB values, set *iscobol.gui.curr_fcolor=-255* <br><br>(iscobol.gui.curr_ef_fcolor is supported for backward compatibility and it affects only EntryFields) |
| iscobol.gui.date_entry.cutoff | This property establishes the two-digit years that will be interpreted by the program as being in the 20th Century and the two-digit years that will be interpreted by the program as being in the 21st Century. <br>When set to a negative value, Java defaults are used. When set to a value greater than 99, only the last two digits of the value are considered. This setting affects a DateEntry with the [Numeric](../../../User-Interface/Controls-Reference/DATE-ENTRY/Styles/Numeric) style where a two-digit year instead of a four-digit year is input. <br><br>The default value is -1. |
| iscobol.gui.date_entry.century_date | This property is used to set the default century date format for date-entry control. <br><br>Default value depends on the current locale. |
| iscobol.gui.date_entry.display_format | This property is used to set the display format for date-entry control. <br><br>Default value depends on the current locale. |
| iscobol.gui.date_entry.errormessage <span id="gui_dateentry_errormessage"></span> | This property specifies the text of the error message shown when a DateEntry validation fails. The DateEntry validation si activated by the property [iscobol.gui.date_entry.validate (boolean)](#gui_dateentry_validate). |
| iscobol.gui.date_entry.long_date | This property is used to set the default long date format for date-entry control. <br><br>Default value depends on the current locale. |
| iscobol.gui.date_entry.time | This property is used to set the default time format for date-entry control. <br><br>Default value is “HH:mm:ss”. |
| iscobol.gui.date_entry.validate (boolean) <span id="gui_dateentry_validate"></span> | **True** = A date validation is automatically performed when the user leaves a DateEntry field. If the date is not valid, an error message is shown and the focus is kept on the field. You can configure the message text by setting [iscobol.gui.date_entry.errormessage](#gui_dateentry_errormessage).<br>**False** = No validation is automatically performed when the user leaves a DateEntry field. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.gui.disabled_field_color | This property specifies the color for disabled Entry-Field and Combo-Box. It can be set to a single value to specify a combined color or to two distinct values separated by a comma to specify background-color and foreground-color. Negative values are considered RGB. For example, to set disabled fields to background blue and foreground white you can set either<br>*iscobol.gui.disabled_field_color=72*<br>or<br>*iscobol.gui.disabled_field_color=1,16*<br>or<br>*iscobol.gui.disabled_field_color=-128,-16777215* <br><br>It is possible to append “;1” or “;0” to specify if this setting should override the COLOR property of the control:<br>**0** = the color specified by this property is always applied. This is the default behavior.<br>**1** = the color specified by this property is applied only if the COLOR property was not used on the Entry-Field.<br>For example, if you only want disabled fields that don't have their own COLOR property to be set to background blue and foreground white, you can set either<br>*iscobol.gui.disabled_field_color=72;1*<br>or<br>*iscobol.gui.disabled_field_color=1,16;1*<br>or<br>*iscobol.gui.disabled_field_color=-128,-16777215;1* |
| iscobol.gui.disabled_placeholder_color | This property specifies the color of the placeholder text in disabled Entry-Field and Combo-Box controls. Negative values are considered RGB. For example, to set placeholder text to low intensity red color, you can set either<br>*iscobol.gui.disabled_placeholder_color=4*<br>or<br>*iscobol.gui.disabled_placeholder_color=-8388608* |
| iscobol.gui.ef.ext_message (boolean) | **True** = An error message is shown when the user puts an alphabetic value into a numeric entry-field.<br>**False** = A beep is played when the user puts an alphabetic value into a numeric entry-field. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.gui.ef_lineseparator | This property specifies the line separator character (or characters) used when returning the value of a MULTILINE entry-field. <br><br>For example, if you wish to force the Windows line separator set *iscobol.gui.ef_lineseparator=\r\n* in the configuration file or use "*SET ENVIRONMENT "gui.ef_lineseparator" TO x"0d0a"* in the COBOL program. <br><br>The default value is the line feed character. |
| iscobol.gui.implied_decimal (boolean) | **True** = If no decimal separator is input by the user, a decimal separator is automatically applied by the Runtime according to the picture of the data-item bound to the input field.<br>**False** = No decimal separator is automatically applied. <br><br>This property affects both graphical entry fields and character based input fields. <br><br>The default value is False. <br><br>*iscobol.gui.entryfield.implied_decimal* is still supported for backward compatibility. |
| iscobol.gui.entryfield.notify_change_delay * <span id="gui_entryfield_notify_change_delay"></span> | This property specifies how many milliseconds the runtime has to wait before firing a NTF-CHANGED event when the user changes the content of an Entry-Field. <br><br>The Entry-Field property [Notify-Change-Delay](../../../User-Interface/Controls-Reference/ENTRY-FIELD/Properties/Notify-Change-Delay) has priority over this setting. <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.gui.entryfield.read_only_color | This property specifies the color for read-only Entry-Field. It can be set to a single value in order to specify a combined color or to two distinct values, separated by comma, in order to specify background-color and foreground-color. Negative values are considered RGB. For example, if you want that read-only fields are background blue and foreground white, you can set either<br>*iscobol.gui.entryfield.read_only_color=72*<br>or<br>*iscobol.gui.entryfield.read_only_color=1,16*<br>or<br>*iscobol.gui.entryfield.read_only_color=-128,-16777215* <br><br>It is possible to append “;1” or “;0” to specify if this setting should override the COLOR property of the control:<br>**0** = the color specified by this property is always applied. This is the default behavior.<br>**1** = the color specified by this property is applied only if the COLOR property was not used on the Entry-Field.<br>For example, if you only want read-only fields that don't have their own COLOR property to be set to background blue and foreground white, you can set either<br>*iscobol.gui.entryfield.read_only_color=72;1*<br>or<br>*iscobol.gui.entryfield.read_only_color=64,16;1*<br>or<br>*iscobol.gui.entryfield.read_only_color=-255,-16777215;1* |
| iscobol.gui.entryfield.read_only_cursor_arrow (boolean) | **True** = Moving the mouse over a read-only field, the mouse shape remains an arrow<br>**False** = Moving the mouse over a read-only field, the mouse shape changes to a vertical bar <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.gui.entryfield.spell_checking_delay | This property specifies the delay in milliseconds for the tool-tip shown by the spell checking feature of the Entry-Field control. <br><br>The default value is 500. |
| iscobol.gui.events_list <span id="gui_events_list"></span>  | This property specifies the value of the [Event-List](../../../Appendices/Graphical-Control-List#event_list) property for all controls for which the [Event-List](../../../Appendices/Graphical-Control-List#event_list) property is not specified. <br><br>The value of this property is the list of numeric values of event constants as defined in the [isgui.def](../../../Appendices/Copybooks/isgui.def) copybook. Multiple values must be separated by comma. <br><br>In a WebDirect environment this property may be overridden by [iscobol.gui.Control.event](#gui_controlevent). |
| iscobol.gui.exclude_events (boolean) | **True** = All controls for which the [Exclude-Event-List](../../../Appendices/Graphical-Control-List) property is not specified assume EXCLUDE-EVENT-LIST=1<br>**False** = All controls for which the [Exclude-Event-List](../../../Appendices/Graphical-Control-List#exclude_event_list) property is not specified assume EXCLUDE-EVENT-LIST=0 <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.gui.fields_unboxed (boolean) | **True** = Entry field controls that do not have boxed style or 3-D style set use no-box style by default.<br>**False** = Entry fields controls that do not have boxed style or 3-D style set use box style by default. <br><br>The default value is True. <br><br>(iscobol.fields_unboxed is supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.gui.flatlaf.conf * | This property specifies the directory where FlatLaf customizations can be found.<br>Customizations must be provided through properties files whose names matches with the chosen FlatLaf theme.<br>For example, if you wish to run your programs with a dark theme and rounded borders on the Entry-Fields, running the program with the [--flatlaf FlatDarkLaf](../../../SDK-Users-Guide/Compiler-and-Runtime/Runtime-Framework/Runtime-Options#--flatlaf) option is not enough; you can customize the FlatDarkLaf theme as follows:<br>1. create a file named *FlatDarkLaf.properties*<br>2. add the entry *TextComponent.arc=999* to that file<br>3. place the file in the directory specified by the iscobol.gui.flatlaf.conf property <br><br>Customizations can be used also to alter the layout of the menu bar. When using FlatLaf the menu bar is embedded in the window title bar. If you prefer to have a standard menu bar below the window title bar, follow the above steps and add the following entry to the properties file: *TitlePane.menuBarEmbedded=false*. <br><br>For more information on the possible entries that you can use in the properties files, refer to the FlatLaf documentation at https://www.formdev.com/flatlaf/components. <br><br>In a thin client environment, the property must be set on the client side and point to a client-side file path. <br><br>By default, the working directory is used. |
| iscobol.gui.input_predisplay (boolean) * | **True** = During Accept the runtime has to refresh the values of the variables defined in SCREEN SECTION. <br>**False** = During Accept the runtime doesn’t refresh the values of the variables defined in SCREEN SECTION. <br><br>The default value is False. <br><br>(iscobol.gui.from_fields_refreshed_in_accept is supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.gui.grid.extended_finish_reason (boolean) * | **True** = Additional [Finish-Reason](../../../User-Interface/Controls-Reference/GRID/Properties/Finish-Reason) values are returned by the MSG-FINISH-ENTRY event in Grid.**False** = Standard [Finish-Reason](../../../User-Interface/Controls-Reference/GRID/Properties/Finish-Reason) values are returned by the MSG-FINISH-ENTRY event in Grid. The default value is False. |
| iscobol.gui.grid.lm_on_columns (boolean) * | **True** = Grid columns of grids with the [Adjustable-Columns](../../../User-Interface/Controls-Reference/GRID/Styles/Adjustable-Columns) style are automatically resized when the window is resized and a layout manager is involved.<br>**False** = Grid columns are not affected by layout managers. <br><br>The Grid property [Lm-On-Columns](../../../User-Interface/Controls-Reference/GRID/Properties/Lm-On-Columns) has priority over this setting. <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.gui.grid.no_cell_drag (boolean) * <span id="gui_grid_no_cell_drag"></span> | **True** = Grids don’t fire drag events.<br>**False** = Grids fire drag events. <br><br>The Grid style [No-Cell-Drag](../../../User-Interface/Controls-Reference/GRID/Styles/No-Cell-Drag) has priority over this setting. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.gui.grid.terminate_finish_entry (boolean) | **True** = Setting EVENT-ACTION to EVENT-ACTION-TERMINATE in a Grid's MSG-FINISH-ENTRY event triggered by function key terminates the ACCEPT with a CRT STATUS value of 96.<br>**False** = Setting EVENT-ACTION to EVENT-ACTION-TERMINATE in a Grid's MSG-FINISH-ENTRY event triggered by function key terminates the ACCEPT with the CRT STATUS value associated to the function key. <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.gui.hints_off | This property sets the amount of time, in hundreds of second, that elapses before the hints are removed from the screen. <br><br>(iscobol.hints_off is supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.gui.hints_on | This property sets the amount of time, in hundreds of second, that elapses before the hints are displayed. <br><br>(iscobol.hints_on is supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.gui.kbd_case | This property defines how characters input by the user are converted into GUI screen fields. Possible values are:<br>**Upper** = All characters are converted uppercase<br>**Lower** = All characters are converted to lowercase<br>**Both** = All characters are entered as typed by user <br><br>The default value is Both. <br><br>Note - This setting may be overridden with Screen Section styles like Entry-Field’s UPPER and LOWER or Grid’s DATA-TYPES. |
| iscobol.gui.keyboard_buffering | The isCOBOL Framework bufferizes keyboard events in order to avoid losing input digits between different Accepts on different windows. This keyboard buffering can be configured by setting this property to one of the following values: <br><br>**-1** = no limit on buffering<br>**0** = disable keyboard buffering<br>**>0** = specifies the number of keyboard events that must be bufferized <br><br>The default value is -1. |
| iscobol.gui.icon (boolean) * <span id="gui_icon"></span> | **True** = The isCOBOL logo is displayed on the windows of the program.<br>**False** = The Java logo is displayed on the windows of the program. <br><br>This property is considered only if [iscobol.gui.icon_file](#gui_iconfile) is not set or points to an invalid file.<br>This property affects only windows for which the [Icon](../../../User-Interface/Controls-Reference/WINDOW/Properties/Icon) property was not specified. <br><br>The default value is True. <br><br>(iscobol.icon is supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.gui.icon_file <span id="gui_iconfile"></span> | This property specifies an image file to be used as the default icon for windows.<br>The following image formats are accepted: BMP, JPG, GIF, ICO and PNG. <br><br>This property affects only windows for which the Icon property was not specified. <br><br>If this property is not set or the file can’t be loaded, then the windows icon is controlled by the property [iscobol.gui.icon (boolean) *](#gui_icon). |
| iscobol.gui.icons_scaling (boolean) | **True** = Check-Box and Radio-Button default icons are resized along with the font under the [LM-ZOOM](../../../User-Interface/Working-With-UI-and-Controls/Creating-a-control/Layout-managers/LM-ZOOM) layout manager.<br>**False** = Check-Box and Radio-Button default icons always have a fixed size. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.gui.ignore_invalid_handle <span id="gui_ignore_invalid_handle"></span> | This property affects the way the Runtime Framework behaves when an INQUIRE or MODIFY statement affects an invalid control handle.<br>Possible values: <br><br>**-1** = No error is returned, but the exception is written to the log if [iscobol.tracelevel](#tracelevel) is set to a value greater than 0.<br>**0** = An "Invalid handle" error message is shown and the program terminates.<br>**1** = No error is returned. <br><br>The default value is 0. <br><br>(iscobol.ignore_invalid_handle is supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.gui.javabean.catch_exception (boolean) | **True** = Exceptions generated by Java-Beans are caught by the runtime system. The exception stack is printed on the system error, but the program continues.<br>**False** = Exceptions generated by Java-Beans cause the termination of the run unit and they're are reported to the user like any other exception. <br><br>Note - To catch exceptions produced by the Java-Bean initialization, it’s good practice to call the [WFLUSH-REFRESH](../../../Appendices/Library-Routines/W$FLUSH/WFLUSH-REFRESH) function just after the display of the Java-Bean control (or the display of the screen-group that contains the Java-Bean control). <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.gui.javabean.serialization_check (boolean) | **True** = Force serialization even if not necessary, i.e. in a stand-alone environment.<br>**False** = Serialize only in thin client environment. <br><br>Setting this property to True allows you to find out if a Java-Bean is suitable for a thin client environment while you're developing on your local PC. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.gui.justify_num_fields (boolean) | **True** = Numeric and edited fields are automatically right-aligned.<br>**False** = Numeric and edited fields are left-aligned, unless otherwise specified. <br><br>The default value is False. <br><br>(iscobol.justify_num_fields is supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.gui.label.rtrim (boolean) | **True** = Spaces on the right side of the title label are trimmed away.<br>**False** = Spaces to the right of the title label are not removed. <br><br>Default value is True. |
| iscobol.gui.layout_manager | When this property is set, the RESIZABLE style is assumed for all windows and all windows use the layout manager specified by the property, unless different settings were used in the DISPLAY WINDOW statement.<br>Specifying a different layout manager or NOT RESIZABLE in the DISPLAY WINDOW statement invalidates the feature for that specific window. <br><br>Possible values are:<br><br>• lm-scale<br>• lm-zoom |
| iscobol.gui.layout_manager.max_font_zoom | Specifies the higher limit for the font increase performed by LM-ZOOM.<br>The value is expressed in percentage having 100 as the original font dimension. <br><br>For example, having an original font size of 12 and setting this property to 150, when the user increases the window’s height, LM-ZOOM will increase the font size accordingly until it reaches the size of 24. After it, the font size will not be increased anymore. |
| iscobol.gui.layout_manager.min_font_zoom | Specifies the lower limit for the font reduction performed by LM-ZOOM.<br>The value is expressed in percentage having 100 as the original font dimension. <br><br>For example, having an original font size of 12 and setting this property to 50, when the user reduces the window’s height, LM-ZOOM will reduce the font size accordingly until it reaches the size of 6. After it, the font size will not be reduced anymore. |
| iscobol.gui.light_gray_is_transparent (boolean) | **True** = The RGB color 0xC0C0C0 in bitmaps is considered transparent color.<br>**False** = The RGB color 0xC0C0C0 in bitmaps is preserved and rendered as gray. <br><br>This propery affects all the images loaded by the [W$BITMAP](../../../Appendices/Library-Routines/W$BITMAP/W$BITMAP) routine in both the graphical user interface and the print jobs. <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.gui.lightweightpopup (boolean) | **True** = lists dropped by menu bar and combo-boxes as well as tool-tips are in the background and can be covered by a web-browser control on the screen.<br>**False** = lists dropped by menu bar and combo-boxes as well as tool-tips are in the foreground and cannot be covered by any control. <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.gui.list.lm_on_columns (boolean)* | **True** = ListBox columns are automatically resized when the window is resized and a layout manager is involved.<br>**False** = ListBox columns are not affected by layout managers. <br><br>The ListBox property [Lm-On-Columns](../../../User-Interface/Controls-Reference/LIST-BOX/Properties/Lm-On-Columns) has priority over this setting. <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.gui.matching_text_color | This property specifies the colors used to highlight the matching text in Entry-Field proposals and records filtered by Grid, List-Box and Tree-View search panels. <br><br>It can be set to a single value to specify a combined color or to two distinct values separated by a comma to specify background-color and foreground-color. Negative values are considered RGB. For example, to set matching text to background blue and foreground white you can set either<br>*iscobol.gui.matching_text_color=72*<br>or<br>*iscobol.gui.matching_text_color=1,16*<br>or<br*>iscobol.gui.matching_text_color=-128,-16777215* <br><br>In Grid, List-Box and Tree-View it’s possible to activate an alternative search approach: instead of filtering the data, leave the data in the control and highlight the portion of text that matches the text searched by the user. The user can then navigate in the control content to review all the items when there’s matching text. In this case, two colors are involved: the color of all the matching texts and the color of the matching text currently selected by the user. To configure both colors, use the semicolon separator. For example, to set all the matching text background blue and foreground white but the currently selected matching text background red and foreground white you can set either:<br>*iscobol.gui.matching_text_color=72;168*<br>or<br>*iscobol.gui.matching_text_color=1,16;4,16*<br>or<br>*iscobol.gui.matching_text_color=-128,-16777215;-128,-8388608* |
| iscobol.gui.max_text_in_bytes (boolean) | **True** = the text typed by the user in entry-fields and grids is measured in bytes, i.e. a double byte character counts as two.<br>**False** = the text typed by the user in entry-fields and grids is measured in characters, i.e. a double byte character counts as one. <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.gui.menu.altkey_default (boolean)* | **True** = menu bar items with no subitems require two steps in order to be selected by keyboard: press Alt+keyletter to activate the item and press Enter to select it (Java Swing behavior).<br>**False** = menu bar items with no subitems are selected by keyboard by just pressing Alt+keyletter. <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.gui.messagebox.bcolor | This property specifies the background that must be used by message boxes where neither the BACKGROUND-COLOR clause nor the COLOR clause were specified in the DISPLAY MESSAGE BOX statement. <br><br>Negative values are considered RGB. For example, if you want the background color to be blue, you can set either<br>*iscobol.gui.messagebox.bcolor=1*<br>or<br>*iscobol.gui.messagebox.bcolor=-128* <br><br>The default value is LAF dependent. |
| iscobol.gui.messagebox.centered (boolean) | **True** = The CENTERED clause is assumed for the DISPLAY MESSAGE BOX statement.<br>**False** = The CENTERED clause is not assumed for the DISPLAY MESSAGE BOX statement. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.gui.messagebox.custom_prog | This property specifies the name of a COBOL program that will be automatically called whenever a DISPLAY MESSAGE BOX statement is used. <br><br>The name can be followed by comma and a letter to specify the program location. When ‘C’ is used, the program is searched for in the client machine, otherwise it’s searched for in the server machine. <br><br>The program name and the next letter are case insensitive. <br><br>Examples:<br>Equivalent settings to load the program from the server’s Classpath or code-prefix:<br>iscobol.gui.messagebox.custom_prog=MYMSG<br>iscobol.gui.messagebox.custom_prog=MYMSG,S<br>Settings to load the program from the client’s Classpath or code-prefix:<br>iscobol.gui.messagebox.custom_prog=MYMSG,C <br><br>See [Custom message box implementation](../../../Language-Reference/Procedure-Division-Statements/DISPLAY#custom-message-box-implementation) for more details. |
| iscobol.gui.messagebox.fcolor | This property specifies the foreground that must be used by message boxes where neither the FOREGROUND-COLOR clause nor the COLOR clause were specified in the DISPLAY MESSAGE BOX statement. <br><br>Negative values are considered RGB. For example, if you want the foreground color to be blue, you can set either<br>*iscobol.gui.messagebox.fcolor=1*<br>or<br>*iscobol.gui.messagebox.fcolor=-128* <br><br>The default value is LAF dependent. |
| iscobol.gui.messagebox.font | This property specifies the font that must be used by message boxes where the FONT clause was not specified in the DISPLAY MESSAGE BOX statement. <br><br>The value format is: FontName-FontStyle-FontDim <br><br>*FontName* is the name of the font.<br>*FontStyle* is the style of the font such as bold, italic or bolditalic.<br>*FontDim* is the dimension of the font. <br><br>Example: *iscobol.gui.messagebox.font=Arial-Bold-08* <br><br>The default value is LAF dependent. |
| iscobol.gui.messagebox.native_style (boolean) | **True** = Message boxes use icons and fonts from the current Look & Feel.<br>**False** = Message boxes use standardized icons and fonts regardless of the selected Look & Feel. <br><br>Default value is True. |
| iscobol.gui.native_name <span id="gui_native_name"></span> | **True** = the Screen Section name of controls is shown in the message box produced by pressing Alt+Pause.<br>**False** = the internal name of controls is shown in the message box produced by pressing Alt+Pause. <br><br>Setting this property to true makes isCOBOL export the COBOL name in the corresponding Java control. This may help when using JVM monitor tools. <br><br>Default value is False. |
| iscobol.gui.native_style (boolean) * | **True** = a LAF dependent border is applied to all controls with a BOXED style (e.g. EntryFields, ComboBoxes, Grids...). It doesn’t work on controls with the 3-D style.<br>**False** = a standard black border is applied to all controls with a BOXED style unless the [Border-Color](../../../Appendices/Graphical-Control-List#border_color) property is set for them. <br><br>The native style is more evident on ComboBoxes with the DROP-LIST style. <br><br>Default value is True. |
| iscobol.gui.nested_embedded_proc_check (boolean)* <span id="gui_nested_embedded_proc_check"></span> | **True** = raise an exception for nested embedded procedures.<br>**False** = don’t raise any exception for nested embedded procedures. <br><br>When the property is set to true, the “[Nested Accept in embedded procedure](../../../Appendices/Runtime-Errors).” exception might be raised. <br><br>Default value is False. |
| iscobol.gui.ntf_resized_delay | This property specifies the timeout in milliseconds before the runtime sends a NTF-RESIZED event to the program during window resizing. A value of 0 means that the event must be sent as soon as the resize occurs. A value greater than 0 will make the runtime wait. If another NTF-RESIZED event occurs before the timeout expires, the timer is restarted. When the timeout expires, one NTF-RESIZED event is sent to the program.<br>Setting this property reduces the number of events generated during window resizing and improves performance. A reasonable value is 100. <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.gui.placeholder_color | This property specifies the placeholder text color for Entry-Field and Combo-Box. Negative values are considered RGB. For example, if you want the placeholder text to be blue, you can set either<br>*iscobol.gui.placeholder_color=1*<br>or<br>*iscobol.gui.placeholder_color=-128* <br><br>By default, the disabled field foreground color specified by the LAF is used as placeholder text color. |
| iscobol.gui.push_activated_by_enter (boolean) | **True** = Push-Buttons can always be activated by pressing Enter when they have the focus.<br>**False** = Push-Buttons can be activated by pressing Enter when they have the focus only if the [iscobol.key.enter](#key_enter) setting includes "termination=13". <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.gui.click_override_focus_change (boolean) | **True** = the click on GUI controls is always intercepted even if the focus was forced on an input field by setting Screen-Control items in an After Procedure.<br>**False** = the click on GUI controls is not intercepted if the focus was forced on an input field by setting Screen-Control items in an After Procedure. <br><br>The default value is True. <br><br>(iscobol.gui.push_override_focus_change is still supported for backward compatibility but it affects only the click on Push-Button controls) |
| iscobol.gui.quit_mode | This property configures the behavior of the close button on the system menu of initial and standard windows. Independent and floating windows are not affected by this property. Possible values are: <br><br>**-2** = Clicking on the close button has no effect.<br>**0** = Clicking on the close button closes the window and the program terminates.<br>**>0** = Clicking on the close button raises an exception on the current ACCEPT. The CRT STATUS variable is set to the value of quit_mode. <br><br>The default value is 0. <br><br>(iscobol.quit_mode is supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.gui.rollover_border_color | This property specifies the border color of boxed Entry-Fields when the mouse hovers over the field.<br>The property accepts numeric values of standard and RGB colors. <br>See [Color management](../../../User-Interface/Working-With-UI-and-Controls/Creating-a-control/Color-management/Color-management) for the list of possible values.<br>For example, if you want a bright blue border when the mouse hovers over an Entry-Field, set *iscobol.gui.rollover_border_color=9*, or, if you prefer to use RGB values, set *iscobol.gui.rollover_border_color=-255* |
| iscobol.gui.rollover_border_width | This property specifies the border width in pixels of boxed Entry-Fields when the mouse hovers over the field.<br>This property should be set to a list of 4 numeric values separated by space that identify the top border, the left border, the bottom border and the right border widths respectively.<br>For example, in order to make the top and bottom border bigger when the mouse hovers over an Entry-Field, set *iscobol.gui.rollover_border_width=5 15 1*. |
