| iscobol.gui.screen_col_plus_base | This property controls the column adjustments when relative values are used by the program for the COLUMN property in the Screen Section.<br>When the value is -1, "COLUMN + 0" and "COLUMN + 1" produce adjacent items. <br><br>When the value is 0, "COLUMN + 0" produces adjacent items, and "COLUMN + 1" puts a space between items.<br>The default value is -1. |
| iscobol.gui.screen_col_zero (boolean) | This property is evaluated each time a statement like "DISPLAY foo LINE *line-number* COL 0" occurs: <br><br>**True** = *stuff* is displayed after the last item on line line-number.<br>**False** = *stuff* is displayed at line line-number column 1. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.gui.scrn_size_cols | This property sets the SIZE of the default initial window in character-based environments (it doesn’t affect graphical windows). <br><br>The default value is 80. |
| iscobol.gui.scrn_size_rows | This property sets the LINES of the default initial window in character-based environments (it doesn’t affect graphical windows). <br><br>The default value is 25. |
| iscobol.gui.search_delay | This property specifies when the control should start filtering data while the user interacts with the search panel. It affects all controls with a search panel: Grid, List-Box and Tree-View. <br><br>Possible values are: <br><br>**0** - wait for the user to press Enter or click the Find button before start searching.<br>**>0** - wait this number of milliseconds before start searching automatically. If the user presses Enter or clicks the Find button before this timeout expires, start searching. <br><br>The default value is 500. <br><br>(iscobol.gui.grid.find_delay is still supported for backward compatibility, but it affects only Grid and List-Box) |
| iscobol.gui.search_panel_settings | This property configures the appearance of the search panel in Grid, List-Box and Tree-View. The property value is a sequence of 4 values that configure different aspects of the bar. <br><br>position 1: filter of items:     <br>0: filter disabled, filter button not visible     <br>1: filter enabled, filter button not visible     <br>2: filter disabled, filter button visible     <br>3: filter enabled, filter button visible<br><br>position 2: navigation buttons:     <br>0: navigation buttons not visible     <br>1: navigation buttons visible<br><br>position 3: clean button:     <br>0: clean button not visible     <br>1: clean button visible<br><br>position 4: case sensitive     <br>0: case sensitive disabled, case sensitive button not visible     <br>1: case sensitive enabled, case sensitive button not visible     <br>2: case sensitive disabled, case sensitive button visible     <br>3: case sensitive enabled, case sensitive button visible <br><br>The default value is '1012' that means:<br>• filter enabled<br>• filter button not visible<br>• navigation buttons not visible<br>• clean button visible<br>• case sensitive not enabled<br>• case sensitive button visible |
| iscobol.gui.secure_char | This property specifies the character used to mask the content of Entry-Fields and Grid cells marked as secure. <br><br>The default value is "*". |
| iscobol.gui.secure_num_chars | This property specifies how many masked characters are displayed in Entry-Fields and Grid cells marked as secure.<br>If set to 0, the number of masked characters matches the length of the value.<br>Set this variable to any number greater than 0 to enhance security by preventing users from knowing the actual length. <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.gui.show_zeroes (boolean) * | **True** = leading zeroes are shown when displaying numeric data<br>**False** = leading zeroes are not shown when displaying numeric data <br><br>This property affects all the output devices: console, character-based terminals and graphical windows. <br><br>The default value is False |
| iscobol.gui.temporary_controls (boolean) * | **True** = all graphical controls are TEMPORARY by default<br>**False** = all graphical controls are PERMANENT by default <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.gui.tool_bar.native (boolean) * | **True** = tool bars are implemented using the JToolBar class; in this way the [Moveable](../../../User-Interface/Controls-Reference/TOOL-BAR/Styles/Moveable) style is supported, but buttons are always flat under the Windows look and feel.<br>**False** = tool bars are implemented using the JPanel class; in this way you can have 3-D buttons under the Windows look and feel, but the [Moveable](../../../User-Interface/Controls-Reference/TOOL-BAR/Styles/Moveable) style has no effect. <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.gui.treeview.selection_delay * | This property sets the timeout in milliseconds for the TrreeView to start searching for an item while the user is typing. The user can select TreeView items by typing part of their name with the keyboard. The TreeView bufferizes the inputed digits and, when this timeout expires, it starts searching for the first matching item.<br>A value of -1 makes the TreeView inherit this setting from the current LAF. <br><br>The default value is -1. |
| iscobol.gui.waitcursordelay | In thin client environment, in case of slow server to client answers, the mouse pointer changes from default-cursor to wait-cursor. This property sets the timeout in milliseconds before the cursor is changed. A value of 0 disables the feature. <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.gui.web_browser.home | This property specifies the URL loaded when the GO-HOME Property of the WEB-BROWSER Control is set to a non-zero value. <br><br>The default value is https://www.veryant.com. <br><br>(iscobol.web_browser.home is supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.gui.web_browser.search | This property specifies the URL loaded when the GO-SEARCH Property of the WEB-BROWSER Control is set to a non-zero value. <br><br>The default value is https://www.google.com. <br><br>(iscobol.web_browser.search is supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.gui.webbrowser.class | This property specifies the class that provides the web-browser feature. Possible values are:<br>• com.iscobol.browser.SWTWebBrowser<br>• com.iscobol.browser.fx.JFXWebBrowser<br>• com.iscobol.browser.jx.JXWebBrowser <br><br>The default value is com.iscobol.browser.SWTWebBrowser. |
| iscobol.gui.webbrowser.no_msg_before_navigate (boolean) | **True** = [No-Msg-Before-Navigate](../../../User-Interface/Controls-Reference/WEB-BROWSER/Styles/No-Msg-Before-Navigate) style is implicitly defined for all Web-Browser controls.<br>**False** = [No-Msg-Before-Navigate](../../../User-Interface/Controls-Reference/WEB-BROWSER/Styles/No-Msg-Before-Navigate) style is not implicitly defined for all Web-Browser controls. <br><br>The default value is False |
| iscobol.gui.window.auto_resize.fixed_dim (boolean) | **True** = a window with the AUTO-RESIZE style can be resized only to a smaller dimension; it cannot be increased<br>**False** = a window with the AUTO-RESIZE style can resized freely <br><br>The default value is True |
| iscobol.gui.window.hook * | This property specifies the name of a class that will be automatically invoked before and after each DISPLAY WINDOW. The class must implement the *com.iscobol.rts.WindowCreateHandler* interface and can extend and customize the window layout.<br>See [WindowCreateHandler Interface (com.iscobol.rts.WindowCreateHandler)](../../../Appendices/Internal-Objects/WindowCreateHandler-Interface) for more information. |
| iscobol.gui.window_title | This property specifies the title of INITIAL/STANDARD windows displayed without the TITLE phrase. <br>Without this setting, the name of the program is used as title of INITIAL/STANDARD windows displayed without the TITLE phrase. |
| iscobol.gui.windows_darkening * | This property specifies the opacity of the parent window of modal floating windows and message boxes. <br><br>A value of 0 means default opacity. <br><br>A positive value ranging from 1 to 100 specifies the level of darkening with 1 representing an imperceptible darkening and 100 representing the black color. <br><br>A negative value ranging from -1 to -100 specifies the level of transparency with -1 representing an imperceptible transparency and -100 representing the full transparency. <br><br>The opacity is applied to windows when they’re blocked by a modal dialog.<br>Having [iscobol.gui.windows_modality (boolean) *](#gui_windows_modality) set to true (default), a window is blocked when a descendant modal dialog is opened.<br>Having [iscobol.gui.windows_modality (boolean) *](#gui_windows_modality) set to false, a window is blocked when any modal dialog that is not its ancestor is opened. <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.gui.windows_modality (boolean) * <span id="gui_windows_modality"></span> | This property takes effect in scenarios where there are multiple initial windows. This is typical in applications that call multiple main programs using the CALL RUN statement. This property affects both Floating windows and message boxes. This property affects also windows created in compatibility with Cobol-WOW. <br><br>**True** = modal windows block all windows in the same hierarchy<br>**False** = modal windows block all windows in the runtime session <br><br>The default value is True |
| iscobol.gui.windows_uncropped (booloean) | This property affects the position where child windows and message boxes are displayed. It can happen that, due the parent window position and the child window dimensions, the child window is cropped by the screen edges. By setting this property, you can instruct the runtime to alter the child window position to avoid the cropping. <br><br>**True** = the runtime ensures that the bottom right border of the child window is not cropped by the screen edges. It moves the window if necessary.<br>**False** = the runtime displays the child window in the position requested by the program, even if the window bottom right border is over the screen edges. <br><br>The default value is True |
| iscobol.guifactory.class * | This property specifies the factory class that is used for GUI. Valid values are:<br>• com.iscobol.gui.client.swing.GuiFactoryImpl<br>• com.iscobol.gui.client.zk.GuiFactoryImpl<br>• com.iscobol.gui.client.charva.GuiFactoryImpl <br><br>The default value is com.iscobol.gui.client.swing.GuiFactoryImpl |
| iscobol.message.cancel | This property specifies the title of the "Cancel" button in the window invoked by the DISPLAY MESSAGE BOX Statement. <br><br>The default value is "&Cancel". |
| iscobol.message.no | This property specifies the title of the "No" button in the window invoked by the DISPLAY MESSAGE BOX Statement. <br><br>The default value is "&No" |
| iscobol.message.ok | This property specifies the title of the "OK" button in the window invoked by the DISPLAY MESSAGE BOX Statement. <br><br>The default value is "&OK". |
| iscobol.message.yes | This property specifies the title of the "Yes" button in the window invoked by the DISPLAY MESSAGE BOX Statement. <br><br>The default value is "&Yes". |
| iscobol.win3_grid | This property specifies a value defining the color of a grid displayed on all windows. This property has meaning only in a development environment. Its purpose is to help the programmer align controls in the window.<br>Possible values are:<br>**-1** = no color (feature disabled)<br>**1** = black<br>**2** = blue<br>**3** = green<br>**4** = cyan<br>**5** = red<br>**6** = magenta<br>**7** = yellow <br><br>Any other value will specify unpredictable colors. <br><br>The default value is -1. |

##### Character Based Interface

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.terminal.alpha_autoclear (boolean) | **True** = clear the area as soon as the user inputs a digit or pastes text from the clipboard.<br>**False** = don't clear the area where the ACCEPT is performed. Keep the value on video and allow the user to overwrite the single digits. <br><br>This affects an ACCEPT with the UPDATE clause on alphanumeric fields. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.terminal.antialiasing (boolean) | **True** = antialiasing is applied to fonts on video.<br>**False** = fonts on video are shown as they are. <br><br>This property affects only character-based displays. Graphical controls always use antialiasing. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.terminal.autowrap (boolean) | **True** = DISPLAY statements longer than one line will wrap around on a character-based display.<br>**False** = DISPLAY statements longer than one line will be truncated on a character-based display. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.terminal.cl2end_fill_spaces (boolean) | **True** = The field is filled with spaces from the cursor position until the end. If the AUTO clause was used or the program is compiled with the -va option, this causes the cursor to be automatically moved to the next input field or the termination of the accept if no more fields are available.<br>**False** = The field is filled with spaces from the cursor position until the end, but the cursor position is unchanged. <br><br>The default value is False. <br><br>See [Keyboard Configuration](./Configuration-Properties#keyboard-configuration) in order to know how to assign the cl2end function to a key. |
| iscobol.terminal.cursor_blink | This property configures the cursor blink for character based ACCEPT. Possible values are: <br><br>**0** = no blink<br>**1** = blink every 600 milliseconds<br>**>1** = blink every n milliseconds, where n is the value of this property <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.terminal.cursor_color | This property configures the cursor color for character based ACCEPT. Possible values are: <br><br>**-1** = default color<br>**0** = black<br>**1** = blue<br>**2** = green<br>**3** = cyan<br>**4** = red<br>**5** = magenta<br>**6** = brown<br>**7** = white<br>**8** = dark gray<br>**9** = bright blue<br>**10** = bright green<br>**11** = bright cyan<br>**12** = bright red<br>**13** = bright magenta<br>**14** = yellow<br>**15** = bright white <br><br>The default value is -1. |
| iscobol.terminal.cursor_type | This property configures the cursor shape for character based ACCEPT. Possible values are: <br><br>**0** = invisible<br>**1** = underscore shape<br>**2** = block shape<br>**3** = underscore shape, becomes block while in insert mode<br>**4** = vertical bar <br><br>The default value is 2 |
| iscobol.terminal.data_range | This property allows you to filter characters accepted in a character-based screen. The value format is *minVal[,maxVal\]*, where minVal and maxVal are integer numbers representing the ASCII value of the character. If a character is not inside the range specified by the property, it is replaced with a space.<br>For example, in order to skip all characters below the space, set <br><br>*iscobol.terminal.data_range=32* <br><br>In order to skip all characters that are not upper case letters, set <br><br>*iscobol.terminal.data_range=65,90* |
| iscobol.terminal.drag_enabled (boolean) | **True** = the user can select an area in the window by dragging the mouse. On character-based screens the highlighted text is copied to the clipboard.<br>**False** = dragging the mouse in the window doesn’t produce a selection. <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.terminal.edited_formatted (boolean) | **True** = numeric edited fields are not cleared when the first valid character is imputed and the editing characters are automatically skipped, except for the B editing character. Only the fields whose edited picture contains characters different from '9', 'Z', '+', and '-' are affected.<br>**False** = numeric edited fields are cleared when the first valid character is imputed. The editing characters are removed as well during the cleaning. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.terminal.kbd_case | This property defines how characters input by the user are converted into character-based screen fields. Possible values are: <br><br>**Upper** = All characters are converted uppercase<br>**Lower** = All characters are converted to lowercase<br>**Both** = All characters are entered as typed by user <br><br>The default value is Both. <br><br>Note - This setting may be overridden with the settings of the "UPPER" and "LOWER" keywords on individual ACCEPT statements. <br><br>(iscobol.keyboard.kbd_case is still supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.terminal.lines_3d (boolean) | **True** = lines and boxes whose color is black as rendered with a 3D effect.<br>**False** = lines and boxes are rendered normally. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.terminal.no_autoclear (boolean) | **True** = don't clear the area where the ACCEPT is performed.<br>**False** = clear the area where the ACCEPT is performed. <br><br>If affects ACCEPT without the UPDATE clause. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.terminal.numeric_autoclear (boolean) | **True** = clear the area as soon as the user inputs a digit.<br>**False** = don't clear the area where the ACCEPT is performed. Keep the numeric value on video and allow to overwrite the single digits. <br><br>If affects ACCEPT with the UPDATE clause on numeric fields. <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.terminal.paste_key | This property specifies the keystroke for pasting text from the clipboard.<br>See [Keyboard Configuration](./Configuration-Properties#keyboard-configuration) for information about how to create keystrokes.<br>By default the paste action is bound to the right mouse click.<br>If you wish to bind the paste action to the CTRL-V keystroke, set: <br><br>*iscobol.terminal.paste_key=*v* |
| iscobol.terminal.screen_prompt | This property sets the character used for PROMPT for character-based ACCEPT. <br><br>Default value:_ |
| iscobol.terminal.screen_prompt_all | This property specifies where the PROMPT character must be shown. Possible values are: <br><br>**NO** - the PROMPT character is shown only in the active field, where the cursor is.<br>**YES** - the PROMPT character is shown in every field involved in the current ACCEPT.<br>**PROTECTED** - the PROMPT character is shown in every field involved in the current ACCEPT except for protected fields. <br><br>Default value: NO |
| iscobol.terminal.update_from_screen (boolean) | **True** = read content from screen during ACCEPT.<br>**False** = don’t read content from screen during ACCEPT. <br><br>The default value is False. |

#### Debugger Configuration

Debugger properties cannot be set by SET ENVIRONMENT within the program. They must appear in the external configuration.

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.debug.code_prefix <span id="debug_codeprefix"></span>  | The Debugger uses this property to locate the source code of a program. Set this property to the list of paths where source files can be found. Separate multiple paths by your system path separator. <br><br>This property is considered in three conditions:<br>- if the program was compiled with isCOBOL 2020 R1 or previous, or<br>- if [iscobol.debug.embedded_source (boolean)](#debug_embedded_source) is set to false, or- if the program was compiled in two steps (compile to java source using the [-jj](../../../SDK-Users-Guide/Compiler-and-Runtime/Compiler/Compiler-Options#java-options) option and then compile from java to class using the javac compiler) <br><br>(iscobol.debug_code_prefix is supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.debug.embedded_source (boolean) <span id="debug_embedded_source"></span> | **True** = The Debugger extracts the source code from the class file.<br>**False** = The Debugger ignores the source code in the class file and looks for the source code on disk. The source code is searched in the following places:<br><br>• the working directory that was used by the Compiler command<br>• the paths and libraries listed in the Classpath<br>• the paths listed by the [iscobol.debug.code_prefix](#debug_codeprefix) property <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.debug.port | This property specifies the port used by the Remote Debugger. <br><br>It can be used in the Framework configuration in order to tell on which port it should listen from connections. <br><br>It can be used in the Debugger configuration in order to tell on which port to connect instead of passing this information on the command line. <br><br>There is no point in using this property in the isCOBOL Server configuration for thin client debugging, as the isCOBOL Client automatically asks the isCOBOL Server to listen for Debugger connections on a specific port, according to the -debugport option (see [Format 6](../../../../is-cobol-AS/Usage-of-isCOBOL-Client/Usage-of-isCOBOL-Client#format-6) of isCOBOL Client usage). <br><br>The default value is 9999. |
| iscobol.debug.propfile | This property specifies the name of the file where the Debugger saves and loads settings. Absolute and relative file names are allowed. <br><br>By default settings are saved in a file named *isdebugger2.properties* under the user home directory. |

#### File Handling Configuration

The following configuration settings affect COBOL i-o statements. Library routines are not affected unless otherwise specified in the routine documentation.

(\*) The asterisk after the property name means that the property is static, it’s inquired only once by the Framework and then changing it using the SET ENVIRONMENT statement has no effect.

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.ctree.auto_adjust (boolean) | **True** = during OPEN I-O, c-tree automatically increases the maximum record size and adds new keys to match the file description used to open the file. The adjustment details are traced in the runtime log, if enabled.<br>**False** = during OPEN I-O, a file mismatch error is returned if the file description used to open the file doesn’t match the characteristics of the file. <br><br>The default value is False. <br><br>**Important notes:**<br>The increase of the record size changes fixed-length record files to variable-length record files.<br>On variable-length record files, only the maximum record size is increased, while the minimum record size remains unchanged.<br>The auto_adjust feature is supported only for new record areas and new keys added at the end of the file description. When adding a new key, the [-cko](../../../SDK-Users-Guide/Compiler-and-Runtime/Compiler/Compiler-Options#cko) compiler option conditions the position of the first segment of the new key as follows:<br>• if the option is not used, the new key must be the last declared in the FILE-CONTROL paragraph;<br>• if the option is used, the first segment of the new key must be at an offset greater than the offset of the first segment of any previous key.<br>Adding new record areas or new keys in the middle of the file description will cause either a file mismatch error or unexpected data in some fields. |
| iscobol.ctree.bound_library * | Specifies the name of the c-tree server main library. <br><br>The default value is "ctdbsapp". |
| iscobol.ctree.bound_server (boolean)* <span id="ctree_bound_server"></span> | **True** = c-tree works in stand-alone mode. This mode supports one client process only, so it is suggested that it is used only in an Application Server environment or single-user installations.<br>**False** = c-tree works in client/server mode. <br><br>The default value is False. <br><br>(iscobol.ctree.ace is supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.ctree.new_config (boolean)* <span id="ctree_new_config"></span> | **True** = The Runtime Framework searches for the c-tree configuration between Framework Properties. See [Configuring the client through Framework properties](../../../../c-treeRTG-for-iscobol/Configuring-the-client/Configuring-the-client-through-Framework-properties) for the list of available properties.<br>**False** = The Runtime Framework searches for the c-tree configuration in CTREE_CONF. <br><br>The default value is True |
| iscobol.dci_routines.use_connector (boolean)* | **True** = [DCI Routines](../../../Appendices/Library-Routines/DCI-Routines) always work on the DCI Connector environment regardless of the configuration settings.<br>**False** = [DCI Routines](../../../Appendices/Library-Routines/DCI-Routines) work on the standard DCI environment unless [iscobol.file.index](#file_index) is set to "dcic" in the configuration. <br><br>The default value is False. <br><br>Setting this property to True is useful when you have the DCI Conenctor (dcic) bound to single files (i.e. *iscobol.file.index.file1=dcic*, *iscobol.file.index.file2=dcic*, etcetera) instead of having it as general file handler (i.e. *iscobol.file.index=dcic*). |
| iscobol.extfh.keep_trailing_spaces (boolean) | **True** = Sequential files managed through EXTFH preserve trailing spaces at the end of the record.<br>**False** = Sequential files managed through EXTFH strip trailing spaces at the end of the record.<br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.extfh.intrinsic_file_manager (boolean) | **True** = "EXTFH input" does not use the "isCOBOL FileManager chooser" but rather uses the "Default isCOBOL File manager"<br>**False** = "EXTFH input" uses the "isCOBOL FileManager chooser", so it uses the file manager specified by iscobol.file properties (e.g. indexed files will be managed by th e handlers specified by [iscobol.file.index](#file_index) and [iscobol.file.index.FileName](#file_index_filename)). <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.extfh.libname | Specifies the name of the EXTFH library |
| iscobol.apply_code_path (boolean) | **True** = The code_prefix is also used for programs whose name begins with "/" or "\".<br>**False** = The code_prefix is used only for programs with a relative path name. <br><br>For example, having *iscobol.code_prefix=/myapp* and loading "/obj/SUB1", the class searched by the runtime depends on the apply_code_path setting. When the setting is True, the runtime will look for "/myapp/obj/SUB1". When the setting is False, the runtime will look for "/obj/SUB1". <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.file.apply_file_path (boolean) | **True** = The file_prefix is also used for files whose name begins with "/" or "\".<br>**False** = The file_prefix is used only for files with a relative path name. <br><br>For example, having *iscobol.file.prefix=/myapp* and opening "/data/FILE1", the file searched by the runtime depends on the apply_file_path setting. When the setting is True, the runtime will look for "/myapp/data/FILE1". When the setting is False, the runtime will look for "/data/FILE1". <br><br>The default value is False. <br><br>(iscobol.apply_file_path is supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.file.binary_file_prefix | This property lists the paths in which to search unencoded sequential files (files whose names start with + characters). <br><br>When a data file is opened for input or i-o, the Framework looks for the data file in every path listed by this property until it finds the data file. If the data file is not found, an error is returned.<br>When the data file is opened for output, the Framework looks for the data file in every path listed by this property until it finds the data file, then it initializes it. If the data file is not found, then the Framework tries to create it in the first path listed by this property. <br><br>Paths must be separated by the a line feed character or by the current operating system path separator.<br>If one of the paths starts with "isf://", then paths must be separated by the a line feed character.<br>Within configuration files, the line feed character is "\n". In COBOL programs, the line feed character is x"0a". <br><br>If unset, the paths set in iscobol.file.prefix are used. <br><br>(iscobol.binary_file_prefix is supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.file.case | **U / u** = Data file names are converted to upper case. Conversion occurs before applying file prefix and file suffix.<br>*L / l* = Data file names are converted to lower case. Conversion occurs before applying file prefix and file suffix.<br>**(Other)**  Data file names are not changed. <br><br>The default value is empty, so no conversion happens. |
| iscobol.file.close_on_exit (boolean) | **True** = All open files are automatically closed when the program exits.<br>**False** = Open files are left open when the program exits. <br><br>The default value is False <br><br>This property is overriden by the [-coe](../../../SDK-Users-Guide/Compiler-and-Runtime/Compiler/Compiler-Options#coe) compiler option. For example, if a program is compiled with -coe, files will be closed even if it sets iscobol.file.close_on_exit=false with a SET ENVIRONMENT statement before exiting. <br><br>(iscobol.close_on_exit is supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.file.connector.program | This property specifies an alternate name for the c-tree File Connector executable. <br><br>The default value is "fscsc". |
| iscobol.file.connector.program.dcic | This property specifies an alternate name for the DCI File Connector executable. <br><br>The default value is "dcic". |
| iscobol.file.connector.program.mfc | This property specifies an alternate name for the Micro Focus File Connector executable. <br><br>The default value is "mfc". |
| iscobol.file.connector.program.rmc | This property specifies an alternate name for the RM/COBOL File Connector executable. <br><br>The default value is "rmc". |
| iscobol.file.connector.program.vfc | This property specifies an alternate name for the Vision File Connector executable. <br><br>The default value is "vfc". |
| iscobol.file.encryption.key * <span id="file_encryption_key"></span> | This property specifies the encryption key that JIsam will use to deal with encrypted indexed files. See [Encryption](../../../SDK-Users-Guide/Files-Management/Indexed-Files/JISAM/Encryption) for more details. <br><br>The encryption key must be 1 to 16 bytes in size and can’t be spaces. <br><br>The property is checked before opening the file. <br><br>In Application Server and File Server environments this property should be set in the server configuration and it affects all the clients. |
| iscobol.file.env_naming (boolean) <span id="file_env_naming"></span> | **True** = The Runtime Framework searches for the name of the file among the environment variables. If found, its value is used in place of the file name: <br><br>SELECT LOGICAL FILE ASSIGN TO "PHYSICAL-FILE" <br><br>**False** = The Runtime Framework searches for the name of the file among the environment variables only if it is preceded by "-E": <br><br>SELECT LOGICAL-FILE ASSIGN TO "-E PHYSICAL-FILE" <br><br>The default value is False. <br><br>Rules for the correct format of the property name:if you set the environment variable in isCOBOL properties, be sure to use lower case, as regardless of what case is used in the COBOL program, all property names must be lower-case. If you set the variable in system variables, instead, use upper case or set [iscobol.file.env_toupper (boolean)](#file_env_toupper) to false. If you set the variable via SET ENVIRONMENT, the runtime will take care of normalizing it, so either lower or upper case can be used.<br>If the file name contains hyphens, replace them with underscoreif the file name includes a path, use the path also in the property name<br>Some examples:<br>+-------------------+---------------------------+<br>\| physical-file     \| property name             \|<br>+-------------------+---------------------------+<br>\| FILE1             \| iscobol.file1             \|<br>\| FILE-1            \| iscobol.file_1            \|<br>\| FILE-1.ext        \| iscobol.file_1.ext        \|<br>\| folder/FILE-1.ext \| iscobol.folder/file_1.ext \|<br>+-------------------+---------------------------+ <br><br>**Note** - if you alter the physical file name under the *easydb* file handler you also need to map the new name with the proper EDBI routine through the [iscobol.easydb.mapping](#easydb_mapping) configuration setting; for example, after setting *iscobol.custfile=customers* set also *iscobol.easydb.mapping=customers=custfile*. <br><br>The *iscobol.file.env_naming* property can be used also to replace the file path in the program's SELECT statement with an environment variable. The path name must be prefaced by the '$' sign and must use the correct system’s file separator. <br><br>Example for Windows: <br><br>SELECT FILE1 ASSIGN TO "$STATION\FILE1" <br><br>Example for Unix: <br><br>SELECT FILE1 ASSIGN TO "$STATION/FILE1" <br><br>**Note** - Only one environment variable is considered when this property is set to true. If your program's SELECT statement contains a "$", the file path will be replaced with the environment variable, but the filename won't be replaced even if there is a valid variable for it. |
| iscobol.file.env_naming_prefix | When this property is used in conjunction with *iscobol.file.env_naming=True*, a prefix is stripped from the file mapping properties. <br><br>For example, if you set: <br><br>iscobol.file.env_naming_prefix=dd_<br>iscobol.file.dd_customers=customer_file<br>iscobol.file.dd_cities=cities_file <br><br>You obtain the same effect as setting: <br><br>iscobol.file.customers=customer_file<br>iscobol.file.cities=cities_file <br><br>(iscobol.file.env_naming.prefix is supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.file.env_toupper (boolean) <span id="file_env_toupper"></span> | **True** = The runtime will search for environment variables that map a file name in upper case.<br>**False** = The runtime will search for environment variables that map a file name with the same case used in the program. <br><br>This property has effect only when [iscobol.file.env_naming (boolean)](#file_env_naming) is set to true and affects only the file names mapping. Any other environment variable or configuration property is not affected. <br><br>For example, having SELECT FILE-1 ASSIGN TO "MyEnv" or SELECT FILE-1 ASSIGN TO "$MyEnv/file1", on a Linux/Unix system the framework will look for an environment variable named MYENV when this property is true, and for an environment variable named MyEnv when this property is false. <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.file.errors_ok <span id="file_errors_ok"></span> | Configure how i-o errors are treated when there’s no section in DECLARATIVES for them. <br><br>**0** = if an i-o error occurs, the run unit terminates with an exception. <br><br>**1** = if an i-o error occurs, the program continues anyway. <br><br>**2** = if an i-o error occurs, the program continues only if a file-status is defined for the file; if no file status is defined for the file, the run unit terminates with an exception. <br><br>**3** = if an error occurs and a file-status is defined for the file, the program continues; if a file-status is not defined for the file, the behavior depends on the error code: the program continues if the error code is less than 30 (i.e. record not found or duplicate key), but the run unit terminates with an exception if the error code is 30 or higher (i.e. file not found or resource locked). This value works only in conjunction with the [-cm](../../../SDK-Users-Guide/Compiler-and-Runtime/Compiler/Compiler-Options#cm) compiler option, it has no effect on programs compiled without that option. <br><br>The default value is 0. <br><br>(iscobol.errors_ok is supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.file.extend_creates (boolean) | **True** = All files opened in EXTEND mode are treated as if they were declared OPTIONAL. If the file does not exist, it is created.<br>**False** = Standard rules are applied. Opening non-existing files when in EXTEND mode causes an error. <br><br>The default value is False. <br><br>(iscobol.extend_creates is supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.file.extend_lock (boolean) | **True** = OPEN EXTEND is treated as OPEN EXTEND WITH LOCK.<br>**False** = OPEN EXTEND doesn’t exclusively lock the file. <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.file.extra_keys_ok (boolean) | **True** = Does not return errors when opening an indexed file that contains more keys than are described by the program.<br>**False** = Returns a mismatch error when opening an indexed file that contains more keys than are described by the program. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.file.indd <span id="file_indd"></span> | This property specifies a custom class for the handling of files associated to [iscobol.compiler.indd](#compiler_indd).<br>The class must implement the com.iscobol.io.InddHandler interface.<br>Refer to the javadoc installed with isCOBOL for details. |
| iscobol.file.index <span id="file_index"></span> | This property specifies the default file system. <br><br>Refer to the [Indexed file handlers](../../../SDK-Users-Guide/Files-Management/File-Handlers#indexed-file-handlers) table for the list of possible values. You can use either the Class-Name or the Alias (if available) to specify the file handler. <br><br>The default value is "jisam". |
| iscobol.file.index.autolock_allowed (boolean) * | This property affects the lock behavior in the same run unit when locks are managed by the *InternalLockManager* class in a thin client or file server environment. It affects locks between a caller program and a called program as well as locks on multiple records acquired by a single program. <br><br>**True** = More locks on the same record can be acquired in the same run unit.<br>**False** = Only one lock on the same record can be acquired in the same run unit. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.file.index.check_all_keys (boolean) <span id="file_index_check_all_keys"></span> | **True** = check keys structure on OPEN and return error on mismatch.<br>**False** = don’t check keys structure on OPEN. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.file.index.data_suffix * | This property specifies the default extension to be applied to the data file of a JIsam or c-tree archive.<br>The separator dot must be explicitly specified. <br><br>By default, ".dat" is used. |
| iscobol.file.index.*FileName* <span id="file_index_filename"></span> | This property specifies the file system to be used with *FileName*. It overrides the setting of [iscobol.file.index](#file_index). <br>*FileName* must match the physical file name declared in the COBOL program with hyphens replaced by underscores and must be lower-case regardless of the case used in the program. Here are some examples:<br>for “F-CUST” set *iscobol.file.index.f_cust=...*<br>for “f_cust” set *iscobol.file.index.f_cust=...*<br>for “F_cust.dat” set *iscobol.file.index.f_cust.dat=...* <br><br>**Note**: The names of the properties that are dynamically set inside the program using SET ENVIRONMENT are normalized by the runtime and therefore the above rules can be ignored. <br><br>Refer to the [Indexed file handlers](../../../SDK-Users-Guide/Files-Management/File-Handlers#indexed-file-handlers) table for the list of possible values. You can use either the Class-Name or the Alias (if available) to specify the file handler. <br><br>When the ASSIGN TO EXTERNAL clause is explicitly used or implied by the [-cax](../../../SDK-Users-Guide/Compiler-and-Runtime/Compiler/Compiler-Options#cax) compiler option, *FileName* must refer to the name of the environment variable and not to its value. For example, having SELECT FILE1 ASSIGN TO EXTERNAL "ENV1" in the source and having ENV1=/tmp/file1 in the environment, set *iscobol.file.index.env1* not *iscobol.file.index.file1*. <br><br>The default value is the one specified by [iscobol.file.index](#file_index). |
| iscobol.file.index.index_suffix * | This property specifies the default extension to be applied to the index file of a JIsam or c-tree archive.<br>The separator dot must be explicitly specified.<br>By default, ".idx" is used. |
| iscobol.file.index.lock_read_anyhow (boolean) * | **True** = The record data is returned to the program even if the record is locked.<br>**False** = The record data is not returned to the program if a lock condition occurs. <br><br>The default value is False. <br><br>This property affects JIsam and c-tree file systems. |
| iscobol.file.index.lock_wait (boolean) * | **True** = Wait for the record to become unlocked if a lock condition occurs.<br>**False** = Return the lock condition to the program. <br><br>The default value is False. <br><br>This feature is fully supported by c-tree RTG.<br>It is supported also by JIsam but, in a thin client or file server environment, it works only if [iscobol.file.lock_manager *](#file_lockmanager) is set to "com.iscobol.as.locking.InternalLockManager". |
| iscobol.file.index.open_hook * | This property specifies the name of a COBOL program that will be automatically called before opening an indexed file.<br>The program receives the following parameters:<br>77 file-path pic x(300).<br>77 file-class pic x(300).<br>The parameters hold the current pathname and file handler class.<br>The COBOL program can change these parameters altering the runtime behavior. <br><br>Sample programs for this feature are installed under the *sample/extend-and-customize/file-open-hook* directory of the isCOBOL SDK. |
| iscobol.file.index.read_lock_test (boolean) * | **True** = A READ WITH NO LOCK returns a lock condition if the record is locked.<br>**False** = A READ WITH NO LOCK reads the record even if it’s locked. <br><br>The default value is False. <br><br>This property affects JIsam and c-tree file systems. |
| iscobol.file.index.strip_extension (boolean) | **True** = The file extension is removed from the file name before opening the file.<br>**False** = The file extension is not removed from the file name before opening the file. <br><br>The default value is False. <br><br>This property doesn’t affect default extensions applied by the file handler, but it affects only the extension specified by the COBOL program, if any. |
| iscobol.file.index.version | This property returns the version of the file handler specified by the [iscobol.file.index](#file_index) property. |
| iscobol.file.indexed_file_prefix | This property lists the paths in which to search for indexed data files. <br><br>When a data file is opened for input or i-o, the Framework looks for the data file in every path listed by this property until it finds the data file. If the data file is not found, an error is returned.<br>When the data file is opened for output, the Framework looks for the data file in every path listed by this property until it finds the data file, then it initializes it. If the data file is not found, then the Framework tries to create it in the first path listed by this property. <br><br>Paths must be separated by the a line feed character or by the current operating system path separator.<br>If one of the paths starts with "isf://", then paths must be separated by the a line feed character.<br>Within configuration files, the line feed character is "\n". In COBOL programs, the line feed character is x"0a". <br><br>(iscobol.indexed_file_prefix is supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.file.input | This property specifies an alternative class with which to handle input streams (input streams are read-only sequential files ). Contact your local distributor for technical specifications. <br><br>For example, to let isCOBOL work with an EXTFH provider, this property can be set to: com.iscobol.extfh.ExtfhInput |
| iscobol.file.input_nolock (boolean)* | **True** = READ WITH LOCK, either explicit or implicit, doesn’t lock the record if the file is open in INPUT mode.<br>**False** = READ WITH LOCK, either explicit or implicit, locks the record even if the file is open in INPUT mode. <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.file.io_creates (boolean) | **True** = All files opened in I-O mode are treated as if they were declared OPTIONAL. If the file does not exist, it is created.<br>**False** = Standard rules are applied. Opening non-existing files in I-O mode causes an error. <br><br>The default value is False. <br><br>(iscobol.io_creates is supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.file.linesequential <span id="file_linesequential"></span> | This property specifies an alternative class with which to handle line sequential files. <br><br>Refer to the [Line Sequential file handlers](../../../SDK-Users-Guide/Files-Management/File-Handlers#line-sequential-file-handlers) table for the list of possible values. You can use either the Class-Name or the Alias (if available) to specify the file handler. <br><br>The default value is "lseq8bit". |
| iscobol.file.linesequential.*FileName* | This property specifies the file system to be used with *FileName*. It overrides the setting of [iscobol.file.linesequential](#file_linesequential). <br><br>*FileName* must match the physical file name declared in the COBOL program with hyphens replaced by underscores and must be lower-case regardless of the case used in the program. Here are some examples:<br>for “F-CUST” set iscobol.file.linesequential.f_cust=...<br>for “f_cust” set iscobol.file.linesequential.f_cust=...<br>for “F_cust.txt” set iscobol.file.linesequential.f_cust.txt=... <br><br>Note: The names of the properties that are dynamically set inside the program using SET ENVIRONMENT are normalized by the runtime and therefore the above rules can be ignored. <br><br>Refer to the [Line Sequential file handlers](../../../SDK-Users-Guide/Files-Management/File-Handlers#line-sequential-file-handlers) table for the list of possible values. You can use either the Class-Name or the Alias (if available) to specify the file handler. <br><br>When the ASSIGN TO EXTERNAL clause is explicitly used or implied by the [-cax](../../../SDK-Users-Guide/Compiler-and-Runtime/Compiler/Compiler-Options#cax) compiler option, *FileName* must refer to the name of the environment variable and not to its value. For example, having SELECT FILE1 ASSIGN TO EXTERNAL "ENV1" in the source and having ENV1=/tmp/file1 in the environment, set *iscobol.file.linesequential.env1* not *iscobol.file.linesequential.file1*. <br><br>The default value is the one specified by [iscobol.file.linesequential](#file_linesequential). |
| iscobol.file.linesequential_N (boolean) | **True** = Use the com.iscobol.io.DynamicLSeqMF_N class as the default for line sequential files.<br>**False** = Use the com.iscobol.io.DynamicLSeq8bit class as the default for line sequential files. <br><br>The default value is False <br><br>(This property is deprecated and is provided for backward compatibility only. Use iscobol.file.linesequential=lseqmf_n instead.) |
| iscobol.file.lock_manager * <span id="file_lockmanager"></span> | This property specifies an alternate class with which to handle locks in the Application Server (Thin Client) and File Server environments. Possible values are: <br><br>**com.iscobol.as.locking.BaseLockManager**<br>The Application Server tracks active locks on indexed files so they can be monitored by the server administration panel and by calling the A$LIST_LOCKS routine. However, the physical lock management is performed by the active file handler. <br><br>**com.iscobol.as.locking.InternalLockManager**<br>Application Server and File Server manage locks on indexed files itself without demanding the lock request to the active file handler. In Application Server environment active locks are listed by the server administration panel and can be inquired by calling the A$LIST_LOCKS routine. <br><br>If this property is omitted, then locks are managed by the active file handler and are not traced by either the server administration panel nor by the A$LIST_LOCKS routine. <br><br>For more information about lock managers, see [Internal lock management](../../../../is-cobol-AS/Internal-lock-management). <br><br>Note - Lock managers don’t work in the Application Server if multitasking is enabled, e.g. if [iscobol.as.multitasking](#as_multitasking) is set to a value greater than 0 in the isCOBOL Server configuration. When multitasking is enabled, lock managers work only in the File Server. |
| iscobol.file.min_rec_size | When trailing spaces are stripped, this property specifies the minimum size of a line. <br><br>The default value is 1. <br><br>Trailing spaces are always stripped in print files, unless the NO CONVERSION clause is used on WRITE.<br>Trailing spaces are stripped in disk sequential files when [iscobol.file.strip_trailing_spaces (boolean)](#file_strip_trailing_spaces) is set to *True*. <br><br>(iscobol.min_rec_size is supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.file.open_check (boolean)* | **True** = If a relative or sequential file is opened by a process even without using any kind of lock, no other process can lock it in exclusive mode.<br>**False** = If a relative or sequential file is opened by a process without any locking, another process can lock it in exclusive mode.<br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.file.outdd <span id="file_outdd"></span> | This property specifies a custom class for the handling of files associated to [iscobol.compiler.outdd](#compiler_outdd).<br>The class must implement the com.iscobol.io.OutddHandler interface.<br>Refer to the javadoc installed with isCOBOL for details. |
| iscobol.file.output | This property specifies an alternative class with which to handle output streams (output streams are write-only sequential files, like print files). Contact your local distributor for technical specifications. <br><br>For example, to let isCOBOL work with an EXTFH provider, this property can be set to: com.iscobol.extfh.ExtfhOutput |
| iscobol.file.output_lock (boolean) | **True** = OPEN OUTPUT is treated as OPEN OUTPUT WITH LOCK.<br>**False** = OPEN OUTPUT doesn’t exclusively lock the file. <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.file.page_eject_on_close (boolean) <span id="file_page_eject_on_close"></span> | **True** = Print files print a page advance record when the file is closed, unless the close contains the NO REWIND phrase.<br>**False** = Print files don’t print a page advance record when the file is closed.<br><br>The default value is False.<br><br>This property doesn’t affect print files assigned to the spooler. |
| iscobol.file.prefix <span id="file_prefix"></span> | This property lists the paths in which to search for data files. <br><br>When a data file is opened for input or i-o, the Framework looks for the data file in every path listed by this property until it finds the data file. If the data file is not found, an error is returned.<br>When the data file is opened for output, the Framework looks for the data file in every path listed by this property until it finds the data file, then it initializes it. If the data file is not found, then the Framework tries to create it in the first path listed by this property. <br><br>Paths must be separated by the a line feed character or by the current operating system path separator.<br>If one of the paths starts with "isf://", then paths must be separated by the a line feed character.<br>Within configuration files, the line feed character is "\n". In COBOL programs, the line feed character is x"0a". <br><br>(iscobol.file_prefix is supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.file.prefix_separator <span id="file_prefix_separator"></span> | This property sets the character used by isCOBOL to define the full path name (joining the value of the *file.prefix* setting and the file name specified in the program) according to the platform. This property was introduced specifically for client-server file systems, such as c-tree.<br><br>For example, if the c-tree server works on Linux while clients are on Windows, and this variable has not been set, isCOBOL inserts a backslash separator, resulting in an error. <br><br>The default value is "\" for Windows and "/" for UNIX/Linux. <br><br>(iscobol.file_prefix.separator is supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.file.relative <span id="file_relative"></span> | This property specifies an alternative class with which to handle relative files. <br><br>Refer to the [Relative file handlers](../../../SDK-Users-Guide/Files-Management/File-Handlers#relative-file-handlers) table for the list of possible values. You can use either the Class-Name or the Alias (if available) to specify the file handler. <br><br>The default value is "relative". |
| iscobol.file.relative.*FileName* | This property specifies the file system to be used with FileName. It overrides the setting of [iscobol.file.relative](#file_relative). <br><br>*FileName* must match the physical file name declared in the COBOL program with hyphens replaced by underscores and must be lower-case regardless of the case used in the program. Here are some examples:<br>for “F-CUST” set iscobol.file.relative.f_cust=...<br>for “f_cust” set iscobol.file.relative.f_cust=...<br>for “F_cust.dat” set iscobol.file.relative.f_cust.dat=... <br><br>Note: The names of the properties that are dynamically set inside the program using SET ENVIRONMENT are normalized by the runtime and therefore the above rules can be ignored. <br><br>Refer to the [Relative file handlers](../../../SDK-Users-Guide/Files-Management/File-Handlers#relative-file-handlers) table for the list of possible values. You can use either the Class-Name or the Alias (if available) to specify the file handler. <br><br>When the ASSIGN TO EXTERNAL clause is explicitly used or implied by the [-cax](../../../SDK-Users-Guide/Compiler-and-Runtime/Compiler/Compiler-Options#cax) compiler option, *FileName* must refer to the name of the environment variable and not to its value. For example, having SELECT FILE1 ASSIGN TO EXTERNAL "ENV1" in the source and having ENV1=/tmp/file1 in the environment, set *iscobol.file.relative.env1* not *iscobol.file.relative.file1*. <br><br>The default value is the one specified by [iscobol.file.relative](#file_relative). |
| iscobol.file.relative_file_prefix | This property lists the paths in which to search for relative data files. <br><br>When a data file is opened for input or i-o, the Framework looks for the data file in every path listed by this property until it finds the data file. If the data file is not found, an error is returned.<br>When the data file is opened for output, the Framework looks for the data file in every path listed by this property until it finds the data file, then it initializes it. If the data file is not found, then the Framework tries to create it in the first path listed by this property. <br><br>Paths must be separated by the a line feed character or by the current operating system path separator.<br>If one of the paths starts with "isf://", then paths must be separated by the a line feed character.<br>Within configuration files, the line feed character is "\n". In COBOL programs, the line feed character is x"0a". <br><br>If unset, the paths set in iscobol.file.prefix are used. <br><br>(iscobol.relative_file_prefix is supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.file.remote.host * | This property specifies the host name where the isCOBOL File Server is listening. <br><br>The default value is ‘localhost’. |
| iscobol.file.remote.port * | This property specifies the port where the isCOBOL File Server is listening. <br><br>The default value is 10997. |
| iscobol.file.remove_name_spaces (boolean) | **True** = spaces are removed from physical file names before looking for the files on disk (e.g. "C:\t m p \ file1" is treated as "C:\tmp\file1").<br>**False** = spaces are not removed from physical file names before looking for the files on disk. <br><br>The default value is False. <br><br>**Note** - spaces are removed only from the name used by the COBOL program, not from file.prefix and current directory. |
| iscobol.file.sequential <span id="file_sequential"></span> | This property specifies an alternative class with which to handle sequential files. <br><br>Refer to the [Binary Sequential file handlers](../../../SDK-Users-Guide/Files-Management/File-Handlers#binary-sequential-file-handlers) table for the list of possible values. You can use either the Class-Name or the Alias (if available) to specify the file handler. <br><br>The default value for files with fixed length record is "sequential".<br>The default value for files with variable length record is "varseq". |
| iscobol.file.sequential.*FileName* | This property specifies the file system to be used with *FileName*. It overrides the setting of [iscobol.file.sequential](#file_sequential). <br><br>*FileName* must match the physical file name declared in the COBOL program with hyphens replaced by underscores and must be lower-case regardless of the case used in the program. Here are some examples:<br>for “F-CUST” set iscobol.file.sequential.f_cust=...<br>for “f_cust” set iscobol.file.sequential.f_cust=...<br>for “F_cust.txt” set iscobol.file.sequential.f_cust.txt=... <br><br>Note: The names of the properties that are dynamically set inside the program using SET ENVIRONMENT are normalized by the runtime and therefore the above rules can be ignored. <br><br>Refer to the [Binary Sequential file handlers](../../../SDK-Users-Guide/Files-Management/File-Handlers#binary-sequential-file-handlers) table for the list of possible values. You can use either the Class-Name or the Alias (if available) to specify the file handler. <br><br>When the ASSIGN TO EXTERNAL clause is explicitly used or implied by the [-cax](../../../SDK-Users-Guide/Compiler-and-Runtime/Compiler/Compiler-Options#cax) compiler option, *FileName* must refer to the name of the environment variable and not to its value. For example, having SELECT FILE1 ASSIGN TO EXTERNAL "ENV1" in the source and having ENV1=/tmp/file1 in the environment, set *iscobol.file.sequential.env1* not *iscobol.file.sequential.file1*. <br><br>The default value is the one specified by [iscobol.file.sequential](#file_sequential). |
| iscobol.file.sequential_file_prefix | This property lists the paths in which to search for sequential data files. <br><br>When a data file is opened for input or i-o, the Framework looks for the data file in every path listed by this property until it finds the data file. If the data file is not found, an error is returned.<br>When the data file is opened for output, the Framework looks for the data file in every path listed by this property until it finds the data file, then it initializes it. If the data file is not found, then the Framework tries to create it in the first path listed by this property. <br><br>Paths must be separated by the a line feed character or by the current operating system path separator.<br>If one of the paths starts with "isf://", then paths must be separated by the a line feed character.<br>Within configuration files, the line feed character is "\n". In COBOL programs, the line feed character is x"0a". <br><br>If unset, the paths set in iscobol.file.prefix are used. <br><br>(iscobol.sequential_file_prefix is supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.file.status * <span id="file_status"></span> | This property specifies which file status codes to use. Set it to one of the following values: <br><br>• com.iscobol.io.FileStatusDefault (for 2002 file status codes)<br>• com.iscobol.io.FileStatus85<br>• com.iscobol.io.FileStatus74<br>• com.iscobol.io.FileStatusDG<br>• com.iscobol.io.FileStatusVax<br>• com.iscobol.io.FileStatusIBM<br>• com.iscobol.io.FileStatusMF<br>• com.iscobol.io.FileStatusMS <br><br>For example, for RM/COBOL-85 (ANSI 85) codes set <br><br>iscobol.file.status=com.iscobol.io.FileStatus85 <br><br>It's possible to create a custom set of file status codes. The class must implement the "com.iscobol.io.FileStatus" interface; contact your local distributor for technical specifications. |
| iscobol.file.strip_trailing_spaces (boolean) <span id="file_strip_trailing_spaces"></span> | **True** = Trailing spaces are automatically removed before writing a line sequential file. When reading, the destination item is automatically filled with spaces before the line is read.<br>**False** = Trailing spaces are not removed before writing a line sequential file. When reading, the destination item is not cleared before the line is read. <br><br>The default value is False. <br><br>This property affects line sequential files on disk. In print files trailing spaces are stripped by default, unless the NO CONVERSION clause is used on WRITE. <br><br>(iscobol.strip_trailing_spaces is supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.file.suffix | This property automatically appends a suffix to all data file names. The dot that separates the file name and the file extension is automatically added by the runtime. <br><br>Example: opening a file whose physical name is "arc" having *iscobol.file.suffix=dat* will open "arc.dat". <br><br>(iscobol.file_suffix is supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.file.xextfh | This property allows you to choose the file manger when EXTFH is not involved. Valid values are:<br>**2** = Use extended EXTFH2 32 bit<br>**3** = Use extended EXTFH3 64 bit |
| iscobol.jisam.autolock_allowed (boolean) | This property affects the lock behavior in the same run unit when using JIsam as file handler. It affects locks between a caller program and a called program as well as locks on multiple records acquired by a single program. <br><br>**True** = More locks on the same record can be acquired in the same run unit.<br>**False** = Only one lock on the same record can be acquired in the same run unit. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.jisam.version | Specifies the version of JIsam files created by OPEN OUTPUT. Valid values are 1 and 2. You should set this property to 1 if you plan to share the JIsam files with programs that run with an old version of isCOBOL that doesn’t support JIsam version 2. <br><br>The default value is 2. |
| iscobol.sort | This property specifies an alternative class with which to handle sort files. Contact your local distributor for technical specifications.<br>For example, to let isCOBOL work with an external sort module, this property can be set to: <br>com.iscobol.extfh.ExtsmSort |
| iscobol.sort.dir | This property specifies the path of the directory where temporary sort files are stored. |
| iscobol.sort.maxfiles | This property specifies the maximum amount of files used for sorting. <br><br>The default value is 16. |
| iscobol.sort.memsize <span id="sort_memsize"></span> | This property specifies the maximum amount of memory used for sorting. The value is expressed in bytes. <br><br>The default value is 1048576. |
| iscobol.sqlserver.convention <span id="sqlserver_convention"></span> | This property specifies the sign convention adopted by the COBOL program that creates the c-tree file. Possible values are:<br>**A** = Acucobol-GT convention<br>**D** = Data General convention<br>**I** = IBM convention<br>**M** = Micro Focus convention<br>**N** = NCR COBOL convention<br>**R** = Realia COBOL convention<br>**V** = VAX COBOL convention <br><br>The default value is A. |
| iscobol.sqlserver.database | This property specifies the name of the c-tree SQL database that the Framework must connect to. This setting is used when iscobol.sqlserver.iss is set to true. |
| iscobol.sqlserver.dirlevel <span id="sqlserver_dirlevel"></span> | This property specifies how many parts of the file path are to be used to build the table name on c-tree SQL when iscobol.sqlserver.iss is set to true. <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.sqlserver.grant | This property specifies the permissions for the linked table.<br>It controls how other users will be able to interact with the table. <br><br>Possible values are: <br><br>**0** = no permission, access denied to other users<br>**1** = public permission with full access to other users<br>**2** = public permission with read-only access to other users <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.sqlserver.iss (boolean) <span id="sqlserver_iss"></span> | **True** = The c-tree indexed file is automatically linked into c-tree SQL during the OPEN OUTPUT.<br>**False** = The c-tree indexed file is created as a standard c-tree file during the OPEN OUTPUT. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.sqlserver.iss.mapping.*filename* <span id="sqlserver_iss_mapping_filename"></span> | This property creates a mapping between one or more physical file names and an iss file. *filename* is the name of the physical file, wildcards are supported (e.g. customers*). <br>The value for this property is the basename (no extension) of the iss file to be used for all file names that match the pattern specified by *filename*. |
| iscobol.sqlserver.iss.replacement_rules <span id="sqlserver_iss_replacement_rules"></span> | This property configures the replacements performed by the runtime on the physical file name before linking it as a table when *iscobol.sqlserver.iss* is set to true.<br>It affects the name of the iss dictionary that will be searched by the runtime as well as the name of the table in the c-tree SQL database. <br><br>By default '.' and '-' become '_'.<br>Before this conversion takes place, you can strip '.', '-' and file extension from the file name by setting this property to the combination between one or more of the following values: <br><br>**0** = don’t omit any character<br>**1** = '.' is omitted<br>**2** = '-' is omitted<br>**4** = the file extension (from the last '.' to the end of the name) is omitted <br><br>For example, given a file whose name is MY-ARC.01.DAT, the runtime will create the table on the c-tree SQL database with the following criteria: <br><br>+-------------------+--------------------+<br>\| replacement rules \|     table name     \|<br>+-------------------+--------------------+<br>\|                0  \| MY_ARC_01_DAT      \|<br>\|                1  \| MY_ARC01DAT        \|<br>\|                2  \| MYARC_01_DAT       \|<br>\|                3  \| MYARC01DAT         \|<br>\|                4  \| MY_ARC_01          \|<br>\|                5  \| MY_ARC01           \|<br>\|                6  \| MYARC_01           \|<br>\|                7  \| MYARC01            \|<br>+-------------------+--------------------+ <br><br>The property affects also the name of the ISS dictionary used by the runtime to manage the file, unless differently configured via the [iscobol.sqlserver.iss.mapping.filename](#sqlserver_iss_mapping_filename) setting. For example, given a file whose name is MY-ARC.01.DAT, the runtime will look for the ISS dictionary with the following criteria: <br><br>+-------------------+--------------------+<br>\| replacement rules \|   ISS dictionary   \|<br>+-------------------+--------------------+<br>\|                0  \| my_arc_01_dat.iss  \|<br>\|                1  \| my_arc01dat.iss    \|<br>\|                2  \| myarc_01_dat.iss   \|<br>\|                3  \| myarc01dat.iss     \|<br>\|                4  \| my_arc_01.iss      \|<br>\|                5  \| my_arc01.iss       \|<br>\|                6  \| myarc_01.iss       \|<br>\|                7  \| myarc01.iss        \|<br>+-------------------+--------------------+ <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.sqlserver.isspath | This property specifies the path where iss files are located. This setting is used when iscobol.sqlserver.iss is set to true. |
| iscobol.sqlserver.owner | This property specifies the owner of the SQL tables. This setting is used when iscobol.sqlserver.iss is set to true. |
| iscobol.sqlserver.password | This property specifies the password for connecting to c-tree SQL. <br><br>This setting is used when *iscobol.sqlserver.iss* is set to true in the isCOBOL configuration and COMPATIBILITY SQLIMPORT_ADMIN_PASSWORD is present in the c-tree Server configuration (ctsrvr.cfg). <br><br>Note that the use of COMPATIBILITY SQLIMPORT_ADMIN_PASSWORD is discouraged for security reasons. |
| iscobol.sqlserver.prefix | This property specifies a prefix to be put before the name of the SQL tables. This setting is used when iscobol.sqlserver.iss is set to true. |
| iscobol.visionj.v_apply_signature | **True** = apply the custom comment "Created by Vision4J" in the header of new files.<br>**False** = don’t apply the custom comment "Created by Vision4J" in the header of new files. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.visionj.v_buffers_per_file * | This property sets the number of indexed block buffers to allocate per file. These buffers are used to improve the performance of indexed files. Each buffer is 512 bytes plus some overhead.<br>Increasing the number of buffers can improve file performance. Decreasing the number conserves memory.<br>The value can range from zero (no buffering) to 2097152. <br><br>The default value is 64. |
| iscobol.visionj.v_compress_factor | This property is used to define the compression factor that is applied to Vision files. The compression factor is applied when a file is created with the WITH COMPRESSION phrase in the ASSIGN clause of the file's SELECT and the COMPRESSION CONTROL VALUE phrase is either omitted or specifies a value of 1. If the COMPRESSION CONTROL VALUE phrase specifies a value other than one, that value is used and the value of this property is ignored. <br><br>This property can be set to any value within the range 0 to 100. The value 0 specifies no compression. Values from 2 to 100 are treated as a percentage that specifies how much of the space saved by file compression is removed from the compressed records. A value of 1, the default, is a special case that causes the standard default compression factor of 70 to be applied. Note that a file's compression factor is set when the file is created and cannot later be changed except by recreating or rebuilding the file. |
| iscobol.visionj.v_force_open | **True** = open broken files that would normally cause a "file broken" error.<br>**False** = return a "file broken" error when opening a broken file. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.visionj.v_index_block_percent | This property determines the ratio of index block to data blocks that are created for a Vision file, which can help trim file size when disk space is tight.<br>When set to a value smaller than 100, fewer index blocks are created than data blocks. When set to a value greater than 100, more index blocks are created than data blocks.<br>The value range is 1 to 1000. <br><br>The default value is 100. |
| iscobol.visionj.v_internal_locks | **True** = return a record or file locked condition for a record or file that was previously locked by the same run unit.<br>**False** = don’t return a record or file locked condition for a record or file that was previously locked by the same run unit. <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.visionj.v_lock_method | This property selects which locking method Vision will use to control simultaneous access to indexed files.<br>**0** = Vision locks the first byte of the file for every access to the file (both reads and updates). This ensures that the process is not interfered with by another process.<br>**1** = Vision locks the first byte of the file for all operations except random READs or READ NEXTs. These two operations proceed without the lock. Instead, they perform some additional reads of the file, to ensure that they get consistent results. If they get inconsistent results, they are retried, this time locking the first byte as other operations do. <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.visionj.v_locks_per_file | This property determines the maximum number of record locks that can be held on a file by a single process. This value affects only the files that are maintaining multiple record locks.<br>The maximum value is 32767.<br>Setting this variable to its maximum value can waste resources and is not recommended. <br><br>The default setting is 10. |
| iscobol.visionj.v_mark_read_corrupt | **True** = mark a file as broken if it encounters a corruption during a read or start operation.<br>**False** = don’t mark a file as broken if it encounters a corruption during a read or start operation. <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.visionj.v_max_files * | This property sets the maximum number of files that can be opened by the current run-time session.<br>Keeping this value small conserves memory. Many operating systems limit the number of files that can be opened by a single process, so you may have to make some adjustments there too.<br>The maximum value is 32767. <br><br>The default value is 64. |
| iscobol.visionj.v_open_strict (boolean) | **True** = receive an error status when opening a file marked as broken for INPUT.<br>**False** = allow OPEN INPUT on broken files. <br><br>The default value is False. <br><br>Note - When this property is set to True, make sure you do not also have the V_FORCE_OPEN variable set to True because the settings conflict. |
| iscobol.visionj.v_read_ahead (boolean) * | **True** = turns on Vision's read-ahead logic.<br>**False** = turns off Vision's read-ahead logic. <br><br>Turning off the Vision's read-ahead logic may improve performance in cases where highly random file processing is being used. <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.visionj.v_seg_size | This property sets the maximum size of a Vision file segment. Vision Version 5 allows for very large logical files by creating additional physical files (called file segments) as needed.<br>The default value is 2 GB - 1 KB -1 (which is 2,147,482,623 or hexadecimal 0x7FFFFBFF).<br>The default is also the maximum value allowed. For best performance, set this value as high as possible, to minimize the number of files created. |
| iscobol.visionj.v_strip_dot_extension (boolean) | **True** = strip any trailing dot extension (such as .dat) from the logical name of a data file when generating file names for index and data segments (except for the first data segment).<br>**False** = prevent file extensions frombeing removed. <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.visionj.v_version | This property specifies the version number of new Vision data files that are created by Vision4J. It accepts values from 3 to 6. <br><br>Version 5 and later files are generated in a dual file format, with data records stored in one segment and overhead key information stored in another. The value 4 produces Version 4 files, which also use the dual file format, but do not support the larger maximum record size and larger block sizes available in Version 5 and later. The value 3 produces Version 3 files, in which dataand keys are stored in a single file. <br><br>The default value is 6, which produces Vision files in the latest format (Version 6). |

#### DatabaseBridge and JDBC-ESQL Configuration

(\*) The asterisk after the property name means that the property is static, it’s inquired only once by the Framework and then changing it using the SET ENVIRONMENT statement has no effect.

##### Common JDBC/ESQL Configuration

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.esql.db2.row_data_as_bytes_threshold * | This property is considered only when connected to a IBM DB2 database and it specifies the minimum length of CHAR and VARCHAR fields that must be managed as byte arrays instead of strings. If set to 0, all CHAR and VARCHAR fields are managed as strings. <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.esql.default_param_type | This property specifies the default type of parameters passed to stored procedures. If affects stored procedures called via [CALL](../../../Language-Reference/Embedded-SQL-Statements/CALL) statement as well as stored procedures called in PL/SQL blocks via [EXECUTE](../../../Language-Reference/Embedded-SQL-Statements/EXECUTE) statement.<br>The default parameter type specified by this property is overridden by the IN, OUT and INOUT clauses in the [CALL](../../../Language-Reference/Embedded-SQL-Statements/CALL) statement as well as by storeproc.property and by the $SQL HOSTVAR directive. <br><br>Possible values are:<br>• IN<br>• OUT<br>• INOUT <br><br>The default value is INOUT. |
| iscobol.esql.error.negative (boolean) <span id="esql_error_negative"></span> | **True** = SQLCODE values different from 0 and 100 are returned as negative values.<br>**False** = SQLCODE values are always positive. <br><br>The default value is False. <br><br>Warnings are not affected by this property. See [iscobol.esql.warnings (boolean)](#esql_warnings) for information about how to intercept warnings. <br><br>SQLCODE values can also be changed via the [iscobol.esql.sqlcode.<value\>=<new-value\> *](#esql_sqlcode_value_newvalue) property. |
| iscobol.esql.indicator_trunc_on_call (boolean) | **True** = set the indicator variable to the length of the stored procedure’s output parameter value when this value doesn’t fit the host variable.<br>**False** = set the indicator variable to 0 when the stored procedure’s output parameter value doesn’t fit the host variable. <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.esql.picx_handling | This property specifies the Java data type behind alphanumeric host variables with picture X(n). <br><br>Possible values are:<br>**0** - alphanumeric host variables are always treated as Java strings<br>**1** - alphanumeric host variables are always treated as Java byte arrays<br>**2** - alphanumeric host variables are treated as Java strings or Java byte arrays depending on column type on the database (e.g. Java strings are used for CHAR and VARCHAR columns while Java byte arrays are used for BINARY and VARBINARY columns) <br><br>The default value is 0. <br><br>**Note** - setting this property to 2 has an impact on the performance as the runtime must check the database metadata to retrieve the necessary information. For better performance it is preferable to mark the host variables that must be treated as binary data using the [VAR](../../../Language-Reference/Embedded-SQL-Statements/VAR) statement, as described at [Storing binary data in Host Variables](../../../Language-Reference/Embedded-SQL-ESQL/Host-variables/Storing-binary-data-in-Host-Variables); variables that are not marked will be treated as strings by default. |
| iscobol.esql.prepare_handler * <span id="esql_prepare_handler"></span> | This property allows a custom class to be specified for the handling of dynamic ESQL queries. This class is used by the [EXECUTE](../../../Language-Reference/Embedded-SQL-Statements/EXECUTE) statement to get the final version of the query to execute. <br><br>The class must implement the com.iscobol.rts.EsqlPrepareHandler interface. Consult the javadoc installed with isCOBOL for specifics. |
| iscobol.esql.sqlca_handler * | This property allows a custom class to be specified for the filling of the SQLCA structure. This class is invoked after each query to get the values for SQLCODE, SQLERRMC and SQLSTATE of the SQLCA structure. <br><br>The class must implement the com.iscobol.rts.EsqlSqlcaHandler interface. Consult the javadoc installed with isCOBOL for specifics. |
| iscobol.esql.sqlcode.<value\>=<new-value\> * <span id="esql_sqlcode_value_newvalue"></span> | This property allows you to remap SQLCODE values to custom values. For example, in order to obtain SQLCODE=1403 instead of SQLCODE=100 when no record is found, set <br><br>*iscobol.esql.sqlcode.100=1403* <br><br>It doesn’t allow to remap SQLCODE values produced by [iscobol.esql.value_sqlcode_on_no_data](#esql_value_sqlcode_on_no_data), [iscobol.esql.value_sqlcode_on_null](#esql_value_sqlcode_on_null) and [iscobol.esql.value_too_many_rows](#esql_value_too_many_rows) as well as the value 1405 returned by programs compiled with [-csqn](../../../SDK-Users-Guide/Compiler-and-Runtime/Compiler/Compiler-Options#csqn) option. <br><br>This property is evaluated after [iscobol.esql.error.negative (boolean)](#esql_error_negative).
