If *error.negative* is true and you wish to remap a SQLCODE value, then you need to remap the negative value.<br>The following pair of settings    <br><br>*iscobol.esql.error.negative=false*   <br>*iscobol.esql.sqlcode.1847=9999* <br><br>produce the same effect of    <br><br>*iscobol.esql.error.negative=true*   <br>*iscobol.esql.sqlcode.-1847=9999* |
| iscobol.esql.value_sqlcode_on_no_data <span id="esql_value_sqlcode_on_no_data"></span> | This property specifies the value of SQLCODE when a query doesn’t affect any row.<br>The following SQL statements are affected<br>• DELETE<br>• INSERT INTO SELECT<br>• UPDATE <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.esql.value_sqlcode_on_null <span id="esql_value_sqlcode_on_null"></span> | This property specifies the value of SQLCODE when an host variable is set to null. This feature is activated by the [-csqn](../../../SDK-Users-Guide/Compiler-and-Runtime/Compiler/Compiler-Options#csqn) compiler option. If the option is omitted, the program sets SQLCODE to zero when an host variable is set to null. <br><br>The default value is 1405. |
| iscobol.esql.value_too_many_rows <span id="esql_value_too_many_rows"></span> | This property specifies the value of SQLCODE when an EXEC SQL SELECT INTO statement returns more than one result. Destination host variables are however set with the values of the first result. <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.esql.warnings (boolean) <span id="esql_warnings"></span> | **True** = warnings are returned through SQLCA if the ESQL statement produces them.<br>**False** = warnings are never returned through SQLCA. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.jdbc.allocate_type | This property specifies the data type of items allocated through the EXEC SQL ALLOCATE statement. It can be set to one of the constant values of java.sql.Types (https://docs.oracle.com/javase/8/docs/api/java/sql/Types.html). <br><br>The default value is -16 |
| iscobol.jdbc.auto_connect (boolean) | **True** = The runtime issues a [CONNECT](../../../Language-Reference/Embedded-SQL-Statements/CONNECT) statement automatically if no connection is available for the Embedded SQL.<br>**False** = The runtime doesn’t try to automatically connect if no connection is available for the Embedded SQL. <br><br>The CONNECT issued by the runtime has no parameters, it’s just EXEC SQL CONNECT END-EXEC, so the necessary parameters must have been set in the configuration (e.g. [iscobol.jdbc.url](#jdbc_url)). <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.jdbc.autocommit (boolean) <span id="jdbc_autocommit"></span> | **True** = The JDBC driver will automatically commit INSERT, UDATE and DELETE statements.<br>**False** = INSERT, UPDATE and DELETE statements are not automatically committed. The COMMIT or ROLLBACK must be done manually in your program. <br><br>The autocommit mode is set at the [CONNECT](../../../Language-Reference/Embedded-SQL-Statements/CONNECT) statement. Changing this property after the connection to the database has been established has no effect. <br><br>All statements issued after the last COMMIT or ROLLBACK in the runtime session will be committed or discarded on STOP RUN according to the [iscobol.jdbc.on_stop_run](#jdbc_on_stop_run) setting.<br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.jdbc.connection.login.timeout | This property specifies the timeout in seconds before returning a connection refused error to the [CONNECT](../../../Language-Reference/Embedded-SQL-Statements/CONNECT) statement. If this property is not set, the connection timeout depends on database settings. |
| iscobol.jdbc.cursor.concurrency * <span id="jdbc_cursor_concurrency"></span> | Set the internal Cursor type for ESQL. <br><br>Valid values are:<br>**1007** = Cursors are read-only<br>**1008** = Cursors are updatable<br>**1009** = Cursors are lockable (only for MS SQL Server) <br><br>Default is 1007 |
| iscobol.jdbc.cursor.type * | This property determines the default cursor type used by the JDBC driver. <br><br>**1** = FORWARD_ONLY allows the cursor to move forward, but not backward, through the data.<br>**2** = SCROLL_INSENSITIVE allows the cursor to move forward and backward through the data. Changes made while the cursor is open are ignored. It provides a static view of the underlying data to which the cursor refers.<br>**3** = SCROLL_SENSITIVE allows the cursor to move forward and backwards through the data. Changes made while the cursor is open are immediately available. It provides a dynamic view of the underlying data to which the cursor refers. <br><br>The default value is 1. |
| iscobol.jdbc.datasource <span id="jdbc_datasource"></span> | This property allows a custom class to be specified for the JDBC connection. This class is used by the [CONNECT](../../../Language-Reference/Embedded-SQL-Statements/CONNECT) statement to get the connection. <br><br>The class must implement the com.iscobol.rts.MyDataSource interface. Consult the javadoc installed with isCOBOL for specifics. |
| iscobol.jdbc.dateformat * | This property specifies the format in which PIC x and PIC 9 receive the value of DATE fields from database tables.<br>yyyy = year<br>MM = month<br>dd = day. <br><br>All supported values are listed in the Java Documentation:<br>https://docs.oracle.com/javase/8/docs/api/java/text/SimpleDateFormat.html <br><br>**Note** - This property has no effect when using SQLJ. Refer to the documentation of the SQLJ implementation in your database to find how to specify the date format. <br><br>The default value is yyyy-MM-dd. |
| iscobol.jdbc.driver <span id="jdbc_driver"></span> | This property specifies the name of the JDBC driver to be used by the ESQL module. |
| iscobol.jdbc.driver.*ConnectionName* | Please consult the DatabaseBridge documentation, chapter [Working with multiple connections](../../../../is-cobol-db-bridge/Working-with-multiple-connections), for details on this property. |
| iscobol.jdbc.fetch_size | This property gives the JDBC driver a hint as to the number of rows that should be fetched from the database when more rows are returned at the opening of a ESQL cursor. If the value specified is 0, then the hint is ignored. <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.jdbc.kept_spaces | This property specifies the manner in which trailing spaces in ESQL host variables are handled. <br><br>**-1** = Trailing spaces are maintained.<br>**0** = Trailing spaces are removed.<br>**1** = Trailing spaces are removed and the first character of the string is always maintained, regardless of whether it is a space or not.<br>**2** = Trailing spaces and low-values are removed. <br><br>Trailing spaces are handled before the host variable is passed to the ESQL processor. <br><br>This property affects only host variables. Constant strings (including the value of the [The EDBI-WHERE-CONSTRAINT external variable](../../../../is-cobol-db-bridge/Runtime-Options-and-Configuration/The-EDBI-WHERE-CONSTRAINT-external-variable)) are not affected. <br><br>This property doesn't affect host variables marked as binary or raw with the VAR statement. <br><br>**Note** - you can verify if a host variable value was trimmed or not in the runtime log. Add 256 to the value of [iscobol.tracelevel](#tracelevel) to trace the SQL activity and obtian this kind of information. <br><br>The default value is 1. |
| iscobol.jdbc.on_stop_run <span id="jdbc_on_stop_run"></span> | This property specifies the behavior of the JDBC driver when the run unit terminates.<br>**commit** = Executes a COMMIT statement, then a DISCONNECT statement.<br>**rollback** = Executes a ROLLBACK statement, then a DISCONNECT statement. Note that the ROLLBACK will have effect only if you’re not working in autocommit mode, e.g. [iscobol.jdbc.autocommit (boolean)](#jdbc_autocommit) must be set to false.<br>**none** = Executes only a DISCONNECT statement. A COMMIT or ROLLBACK is performed depending on the database defaults. <br><br>The default value is none. |
| iscobol.jdbc.options | This property sets a list of options to be passed to the JDBC driver. Every option consists of a name and a value, separated by the equals (=) symbol. Options are separated by commas. For example: <br><br>*iscobol.jdbc.options=Option1=Value1,Option2=Value2* <br><br>Options must be set before connecting. <br><br>**Note** - Due to JDBC rules, options are evaluated only if user and password are not specified in the [CONNECT](../../../Language-Reference/Embedded-SQL-Statements/CONNECT) statement.<br>If you wish to specify a user and password in the [CONNECT](../../../Language-Reference/Embedded-SQL-Statements/CONNECT) statement, then you should use a custom [CONNECT](../../../Language-Reference/Embedded-SQL-Statements/CONNECT) implementation in order to set JDBC options. See [iscobol.jdbc.datasource](#jdbc_datasource) for details. |
| iscobol.jdbc.password <span id="jdbc_password"></span> | This property specifies the password to log in the database. <br><br>You should set this property only if the password is not specified neither in [iscobol.jdbc.url](#jdbc_url) nor by the [CONNECT](../../../Language-Reference/Embedded-SQL-Statements/CONNECT) statement. |
| iscobol.jdbc.thread_connection (boolean) | **True** = Each thread created by PERFORM THREAD or CALL THREAD uses a separate database connection. The thread code is responsible to create the connection. When working with the DatabaseBridge, EDBI subroutines take care of managing the multiple thread connections.<br>**False** = All threads created by PERFORM THREAD or CALL THREAD share the same database connection. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.jdbc.timeformat * | This property specifies the format in which PIC X and PIC 9 receive the value of TIME fields from database tables.<br>All supported values are listed in the Java Documentation:<br>https://docs.oracle.com/javase/8/docs/api/java/text/SimpleDateFormat.html <br><br>**Note** - This property has no effect when using SQLJ. Refer to the documentation of the SQLJ implementation in your database to find how to specify the time format. <br><br>Default value is HH:mm:ss.SSS. |
| iscobol.jdbc.timestampformat * | This property specifies the format in which PIC X and PIC 9 receive the value of TIMESTAMP fields from database tables. <br><br>All supported values are listed in the Java Documentation:<br>https://docs.oracle.com/javase/8/docs/api/java/time/format/DateTimeFormatter.html <br><br>**Note** - This property has no effect when using SQLJ. Refer to the documentation of the SQLJ implementation in your database to find how to specify the timestamp format. <br><br>Default value is yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss.SSS. |
| iscobol.jdbc.transaction_isolation | This property specifies the transaction isolation level.<br>Possible values are:<br>**1**: TRANSACTION_READ_UNCOMMITTED<br>**2**: TRANSACTION_READ_COMMITTED<br>**4**: TRANSACTION_REPEATABLE_READ<br>**8**: TRANSACTION_SERIALIZABLE <br><br>The default isolation level depends on the database. |
| iscobol.jdbc.url <span id="jdbc_url"></span> | This property specifies the prefix of the JDBC URL used by the ESQL module. Possible values depend on the JDBC driver being used. <br><br>The URL could include user and password, or you can specify user and password separately by setting [iscobol.jdbc.user](#jdbc_user) and [iscobol.jdbc.password](#jdbc_password). <br><br>For example, the following setting:<br>iscobol.jdbc.url=jdbc:oracle:thin:scott/tiger@myhost:1521:orcl<br>is equivalent to:<br>iscobol.jdbc.url=jdbc:oracle:thin:@myhost:1521:orcl<br>iscobol.jdbc.user=scott<br>iscobol.jdbc.password=tiger |
| iscobol.jdbc.url.*ConnectionName* | Please consult the DatabaseBridge documentation, chapter [Working with multiple connections](../../../../is-cobol-db-bridge/Working-with-multiple-connections), for details on this property. |
| iscobol.jdbc.user <span id="jdbc_user"></span> | This property specifies the user to log in the database. <br><br>You should set this property only if the user is not specified neither in [iscobol.jdbc.url](#jdbc_url) nor by the [CONNECT](../../../Language-Reference/Embedded-SQL-Statements/CONNECT) statement. |

##### DatabaseBridge (EasyDB) Runtime Configuration

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.easydb.commit_count <span id="easydb_commit_count"></span> | Specifies the COMMIT COUNT for EDBI routines. <br><br>When this property is set to a value greater than 0, a COMMIT is issued after this number of operations. WRITE, REWRITE, and DELETE are counted; READ, START, and READ NEXT are not. <br><br>The default value is 0. <br><br>[iscobol.jdbc.autocommit (boolean)](#jdbc_autocommit) must be set to false in the configuration, otherwise all the operations are automatically committed. <br><br>**Note** - routines generated by the EDBIIS command consider this setting only if the [-cc](../../../../is-cobol-db-bridge/DatabaseBridge-generator-edbiis#cc) option was used. |
| iscobol.easydb.commit_count.*ConnectionName* <span id="easydb_commit_count_connectionname"></span> | Specifies the COMMIT COUNT for EDBI routines. It affects only the connection identified by *ConnectionName*. <br><br>When this property is set to a value greater than 0, a COMMIT is issued after this number of operations. WRITE, REWRITE, and DELETE are counted; READ, START, and READ NEXT are not. <br><br>The default value is 0. [iscobol.jdbc.autocommit (boolean)](#jdbc_autocommit) must be set to false in the configuration, otherwise all the operations are automatically committed. <br><br>**Note** - routines generated by the EDBIIS command consider this setting only if the [-cc](../../../../is-cobol-db-bridge/DatabaseBridge-generator-edbiis#cc) option was used. |
| iscobol.easydb.connection_name.*FileName* | Please consult DatabaseBridge documentation, chapter [Working with multiple connections](../../../../is-cobol-db-bridge/Working-with-multiple-connections), for details on this property. |
| iscobol.easydb.date_cutoff | This property uses a two-digit value and establishes the two-digit year that will be interpreted by the program as being in the 20th Century and the two-digit year that will be interpreted by the program as being in the 21st Century.<br>For example, consider setting: <br><br>*iscobol.easydb.date_cutoff=30* <br><br>In this case, 2000 will be added to the two-digit year that are smaller than "30" (or whatever value you give to this variable), and will therefore make them part of the 21st Century. 1900 will be added to the two-digit year that are larger than "30", making them part of the 20th Century. A COBOL date like 99/10/10 will be translated into 1999/10/10. A COBOL date like 00/02/12 will be translated into 2000/02/12. <br><br>The default value is 20. <br><br>(iscobol.easydb_date_cutoff is supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.easydb.dirlevel <span id="easydb_dirlevel"></span> | This property gives you a method of mapping filenames with directories to the EDBI routine. It determines how many levels of the directory to use as part of the table name. If you set this property to 0, it means no level behavior (default). If you set it to a positive value, it determines how many directory names to keep. <br><br>For example assuming that a COBOL program opens a file like"/usr/temp/users/invoice", if *easydb.dirlevel*=0, the EDBI routine will reference a table named "invoice"; if *easydb.dirlevel*=1, the EDBI routine will reference a table named "usersinvoice"; if *easydb.dirlevel*=2, the EDBI routine will reference a table named "tempusersinvoice"; and so on... <br><br>When this property is set to a value greater than zero, it generates a mismatch between the name of the table and the name of the EDBI routine, so it’s necessary to set [iscobol.easydb.mapping](#easydb_mapping) as well. With reference to the above example, you should set *iscobol.easydb.mapping=*invoice=invoice*. <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.easydb.dirlevel_to_column <span id="easydb_dirlevel_to_column"></span> | This property determines how many levels of the file path should be stored as value of the DTC column. <br><br>For example assuming that a COBOL program opens a file like"/usr/temp/users/invoice", if *easydb.dirlevel_to_column*=0, the EDBI routine will leave the DTC column empty; if *easydb.dirlevel_to_column*=1, the EDBI routine will store the value "users" in the DTC column; if *easydb.dirlevel_to_column*=2, the EDBI routine will store the value "temp/users" in the DTC column; and so on... <br><br>This property is considered only by EDBI routines generated with [iscobol.compiler.easydb.dirlevel_to_column (boolean)](#easydb_dirlevel_to_column) = true. <br><br>The feature is currently available only on the Oracle database. <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.easydb.inv_date | This property is used to establish an invalid date (such as 2000/02/31) in order to avoid problems that can occur when an incorrect date format has been written to the database.<br>The date must be specified as a eight digits string that is the concatenation between year, month and day.<br>For example, to use January 1, 2000 as valid date to replace invalid dates, set: <br><br>*iscobol.easydb.inv_date=20000101* <br><br>The default replacement for invalid dates is December 30, 9999. <br><br>During record insertion, if the COBOL field contains an invalid date, the date specified by this property is written to the database field. After record read, if the date value matches with the value of this property, the COBOL field is set to zero. <br><br>(iscobol.easydb_inv_date is supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.easydb.julian_base_date | This property specifies the base date to resolve julian dates. The value must be specified in the format YYYYMMDD.<br>For example, to use the January 1, 2000 as base date to resolve julian dates, set: <br><br>*iscobol.easydb.julian_base_date=20000101* <br><br>The default julian base date is Januray 1, 1600. <br><br>This property can be set to dates that are greater than the 1st January 1600. If you need a lower date as base date (E.g. 00010101) then you can’t use the automatic handling of Julian dates, but you need to write your own date conversion routines. See [iscobol.compiler.easydb.julian_routines=<cbdb\>;<dbcb\>](#compiler_easydb_julian_routines); for more information. <br><br>(iscobol.easydb_julian_base_date is supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.easydb.limit_dropdown <span id="easydb_limit_dropdown"></span> | This property limits the number of queries performed for a START operation.<br><br>Possible values are:<br>**0** - Perform all the queries in the drop down.<br>**1** - Only for START with SIZE clause, stop after all records matching the START columns have been exhausted.<br>**2** - Only for START without SIZE clause, stop after all records matching the START columns have been exhausted.<br>**3** - Regardless of the kind of START, stop after all records matching the START columns have been exhausted. <br><br>The default value is 0. <br><br>See [iscobol.easydb.limit_dropdown](../../../../is-cobol-db-bridge/Runtime-Options-and-Configuration/iscobol.easydb.limit_dropdown) for more information. |
| iscobol.easydb.mapping <span id="easydb_mapping"></span> | This property allows you to associate certain filenames with particular EDBI routines. This enables you to use one EDBI routine for many different files with the same data structure. Multiple mappings must be separated by space. For example, setting: <br><br>*iscobol.easydb.mapping=invoice2008=invoice* or *invoice2009=invoice* <br><br>or the equivalent <br><br>*iscobol.easydb.mapping=invoice2008=invoice* or *invoice2009=invoice* <br><br>means that the Dynamic Filesystem Interface will redirect I/O done from any COBOL program that uses "invoice2008" and "invoice2009" as a physical file basename to EDBI-invoice.<br>You can also use the wildcard "\*" character to simplify file name associations, where "\*" matches any number of characters. Note that "\*" is the only wildcard supported.<br>For example, <br><br>*iscobol.easydb.mapping=invoice\*=invoice* <br><br>will match all of the above.<br>The parsing of iscobol.easydb.mapping value is interrupted at the first match found. You should pay attention to the order mappings are listed in order to avoid an unexpected mapping within similar file names. For example, suppose that, in addition to the invoice files shown above you have a different file named "invoices" and you want it to be managed by the routine EDBI-file1. The following setting is wrong: <br><br>*iscobol.easydb.mapping=invoice*=invoice or *invoices=file1* <br><br>Because "invoice*" would be valid also for the file name "invoices" and so that file would be associated to the EDBI-invoice routine as well. The correct setting in this case is: <br><br>*iscobol.easydb.mapping=invoices=file1* or *invoice*=invoice* <br><br>Dots in the file name are translated to underscores by the DatabaseBridge. If you need to map a file whose name has an extension, you need to be aware of this rule. For example, if you want that "file1.db" is managed by EDBI-file1, set: <br><br>*iscobol.easydb.mapping=file1_db=file1* <br><br>If no matches are found in *iscobol.easydb.mapping*, the Dynamic Filesystem Interface will look for matches in the deprecated *iscobol.filename.mapping* configuration property. This property is still supported for backward compatibility. |
| iscobol.easydb.max_date | This property is used to establish a high-value date in order to avoid problems in cases where invalid dates have been incorrectly written to the database. The date must be specified as a eight digits string that is the concatenation between year, month and day.<br>For example, to use December 31, 2099 as valid date to replace dates set to high-values, set: <br><br>*iscobol.easydb.max_date=20991231* <br><br>The default max date is December 31, 9999. <br><br>During record insertion, if the COBOL field contains high-value, the date specified by this property is written to the database field. After record read, if the date value matches with the value of this property, the COBOL field is set to high-value. <br><br>(iscobol.easydb_max_date is supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.easydb.min_date | This property is used to establish a low-value (0 or space) date in order to avoid problems that can occur when invalid dates have been incorrectly written to the database. The date must be specified as a eight digits string that is the concatenation between year, month and day.<br>For example, to use January 1, 2000 as valid date to replace dates set to low-values, set: <br><br>*iscobol.easydb.min_date=20000101* <br><br>The default value is database dependent:<br>• on Informix, the default min date is December 31, 1899;<br>• on Microsfot SQL Server, the default min date is January 1, 1753;<br>• on MySQL, the default min date is January 1, 1001;<br>• on other databases, the default min date is January 1, year 1. <br><br>During record insertion, if the COBOL field contains low-value, zero or spaces, the date specified by this property is written to the database field. After record read, if the date value matches with the value of this property, the COBOL field is set to zero. <br><br>(iscobol.easydb_min_date is supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.easydb.mysql_row_limit <span id="easydb_mysql_row_limit"></span> | This property specifies the weight of the cursors used to read data from MySQL. By default the MySQL JDBC driver loads in memory all the records of a table when the COBOL program performs a Start. This behavior might consume resources and affect performance when working on huge tables. If [iscobol.compiler.easydb.light_cursors](##compiler_easydb_light_cursors) was set to a value grater than 0 at compile time, then EDBI subroutines read data using a paging logic in order to work around the driver behavior. <br><br>The default value is 100. <br><br>**Note** - routines generated by the EDBIIS command consider this setting only if the [-dmld](../../../../is-cobol-db-bridge/DatabaseBridge-generator-edbiis#dmld) or [-dmlu](../../../../is-cobol-db-bridge/DatabaseBridge-generator-edbiis#dmlu) options were used. |
| iscobol.easydb.postgres_row_limit <span id="easydb_postgres_row_limit"></span> | This property specifies the weight of the cursors used to read data from PostgreSQL. By default the PostgreSQL JDBC driver loads in memory all the records of a table when the COBOL program performs a Start. This behavior might consume resources and affect performance when working on huge tables. If [iscobol.compiler.easydb.light_cursors](##compiler_easydb_light_cursors) was set to a value grater than 0 at compile time, then EDBI subroutines read data using a paging logic in order to work around the driver behavior. <br><br>The default value is 100. <br><br>**Note** - routines generated by the EDBIIS command consider this setting only if the [-dpld](../../../../is-cobol-db-bridge/DatabaseBridge-generator-edbiis#dpld) or [-dplu](../../../../is-cobol-db-bridge/DatabaseBridge-generator-edbiis#dplu) options were used. |
| iscobol.easydb.prefix | This property specifies the prefix that the runtime should put before the EDBI routine name before calling it. <br><br>For example, setting this property to "ora", when the program opens FILE1, the runtime will call ORAEDBI_FILE1. <br><br>By default, no prefix is used. |
| iscobol.easydb.prefix.*ConnectionName* | Please consult the DatabaseBridge documentation, chapter [Working with multiple connections](../../../../is-cobol-db-bridge/Working-with-multiple-connections), for details on this property. |
| iscobol.easydb.replacement_rules | This property configures the replacements performed by the EDBI routines on the physical file basename before using it as database table name. <br><br>By default '.' and '-' become '_'.Before this conversion takes place, you can strip '.', '-' and file extension from the file name by setting this property to the combination between one or more of the following values: <br><br>**0** = don’t omit any character<br>**1** = '.' is omitted<br>**2** = '-' is omitted<br>**4** = the file extension (from the last '.' to the end of the name) is omitted <br><br>For example, given a file whose name is MY-ARC.01.DAT, the runtime will look for the table on the database with the following criteria: <br><br>+-------------------+--------------------+<br>\| replacement rules \|     table name     \|<br>+-------------------+--------------------+<br>\|                0  \| MY_ARC_01_DAT      \|<br>\|                1  \| MY_ARC01DAT        \|<br>\|                2  \| MYARC_01_DAT       \|<br>\|                3  \| MYARC01DAT         \|<br>\|                4  \| MY_ARC_01          \|<br>\|                5  \| MY_ARC01           \|<br>\|                6  \| MYARC_01           \|<br>\|                7  \| MYARC01           \|<br>+-------------------+--------------------+ <br><br>The property affects also the name of the EDBI routine used by the runtime to manage the file, unless differently configured via the [iscobol.easydb.mapping](#easydb_mapping) setting. For example, given a file whose name is MY-ARC.01.DAT, the runtime will look for the EDBI routine with the following criteria: <br><br>+-------------------+--------------------+<br>\| replacement_rules \|    EDBI routine    \|<br>+-------------------+--------------------+<br>\|                0  \| EDBI_MY_ARC_01_DAT \|<br>\|                1  \| EDBI_MY_ARC01DAT   \|<br>\|                2  \| EDBI_MYARC_01_DAT  \|<br>\|                3  \| EDBI_MYARC01DAT    \|<br>\|                4  \| EDBI_MY_ARC_01     \|<br>\|                5  \| EDBI_MY_ARC01      \|<br>\|                6  \| EDBI_MYARC_01      \|<br>\|                7  \| EDBI_MYARC01       \|<br>+-------------------+--------------------+ <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.easydb.sqlserver_row_limit <span id="easydb_sqlserver_row_limit"></span> | This property specifies the weight of the cursors used to read data from Microsoft SQL Server. By default the Microsoft SQL Server JDBC driver loads in memory all the records of a table when the COBOL program performs a Start. This behavior might consume resources and affect performance when working on huge tables. If [iscobol.compiler.easydb.light_cursors](##compiler_easydb_light_cursors) was set to a value grater than 0 at compile time, then EDBI subroutines read data using a paging logic in order to work around the driver behavior. <br><br>The default value is 100. <br><br>**Note** - routines generated by the EDBIIS command consider this setting only if the [-dsld](../../../../is-cobol-db-bridge/DatabaseBridge-generator-edbiis#dsld) or [-dslu](../../../../is-cobol-db-bridge/DatabaseBridge-generator-edbiis#dslu) options were used. |
| iscobol.easydb.start_on_specific_table (boolean) <span id="easydb_start_on_specific_table"></span> | **True** = When a START is performed on a multi-record FD, only the table related to the current record type is used.<br>**False** = When a START is performed on a multi-record FD, all the tables related to the FD are used. <br><br>The default value is False. <br><br>**Note** - routines generated by the EDBIIS command consider this setting only if the [-esst](../../../../is-cobol-db-bridge/DatabaseBridge-generator-edbiis#esst) option was used. |
| iscobol.easydb.wait_for_lock (boolean) <span id="easydb_wait_for_lock"></span> | **True** = EDBI routines wait for the lock to be released. The EDBI routine must have been generated with *easydb.oracle.wait_for_locks=1*.<br>**False** = EDBI routines return the lock condition. <br><br>The default value is False. <br><br>This feature is currently provided only for Oracle. <br><br>**Note** - routines generated by the EDBIIS command consider this setting only if the [-owfl](../../../../is-cobol-db-bridge/DatabaseBridge-generator-edbiis#owfl) option was used. |
| iscobol.edbi.notnum.tracefile <span id="edbi_notnum_tracefile"></span>| This property specifies the name of the trace file generated by EDBI subroutines for cases of *not numeric data in numeric field*. <br><br>The default value is TRACENUM. <br><br>**Note** - routines generated by the EDBIIS command consider this setting only if the [-t](../../../../is-cobol-db-bridge/DatabaseBridge-generator-edbiis#t) option was used. |

#### isCOBOL Server (thin client) Configuration

isCOBOL Server properties listed below cannot be set by SET ENVIRONMENT within the program. They must appear in the configuration passed to the isCOBOL Server at startup.

(\*) The asterisk after the property name means that the property is read every time a Client connects. Other properties instead are read only at server startup.

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.as.alias.*AliasName* * | This property defines an alias.<br>Aliases are evaluated when [iscobol.as.use_aliases (boolean)](#as_use_aliases) is set to true.<br>For more information see [Working with Aliases](../../../../is-cobol-AS/Working-with-Aliases). |
| iscobol.as.appserver (boolean) | **True** = Start the Class Server along with isCOBOL Server.<br>**False** = Don’t start the Class Server along with isCOBOL Server. <br><br>This setting is only considered if at least one of these properties is set to true: [iscobol.as.fileserver (boolean)](#as_fileserver), [iscobol.as.httpserver (boolean)](#as_httpserver), [iscobol.as.ide (boolean)](#as_ide) or [iscobol.as.turborun (boolean)](#as_turborun). If all these properties are false, then the Class Server is always started. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.as.authentication * | This property defines how the users are authenticated in Application Server environment. <br><br>**0** = No password required.<br>**1** = A password is required only for admin functions.<br>**2** = A password is always required. <br><br>The default value is 1. |
| iscobol.as.check_alive_interval <span id="as_check_alive_interval"></span> | This property activates a "check alive" communication between the isCOBOL Server and the connected Clients in Thin Client environment. If the communication fails, then the Server sends a kill signal to the Client. The response of the Client to the kill signal may be affected by the [iscobol.as.stop_thread *](#as_stop_thread) setting. <br><br>The property must be set to two numeric values separated by space. The first value specifies the interval in seconds between a ping and another. The second value is the response timeout in seconds.<br>Optionally, you can specify other two numeric values that specify how many times the isCOBOL Server should retry to communicate with a Client before considering it dead and the delay in seconds between these retries. <br><br>For example, in order to perform a check each 5 minutes with a timeout of a minute, set: <br><br>*iscobol.as.check_alive_interval=300 60* <br><br>In order to retry up to 5 times in 5 minutes before considering a Client dead, set: <br><br*>iscobol.as.check_alive_interval=300 60 5 60* <br><br>In order to have a trace of the checks that failed, set [iscobol.as.logging (boolean)](#as_logging) and [iscobol.as.logging.exception (boolean)](#as_logging_exception) to true. <br><br>By default the check alive feature is disabled. |
| iscobol.as.clientupdate.propfile | This property specifies the name of a custom *swupdater.properties* file to be used instead of the default *swupdater.properties*.<br>While the default *swupdater.properties* includes just the *iscobol*, *iscobolJars* and *iscobolNative* packages, this custom file can contain any package.<br>This is useful to instruct the client to download additional items (e.g. custom items like programs that will be called via CALL CLIENT) in addition to the isCOBOL libraries. |
| iscobol.as.clientupdate.site | This property specifies the URL of an HTTP server where Clients can connect to look for updates.<br>The HTTP server could be:<br>• a external HTTP Server like IIS or Apache<br>• a separate isCOBOL Server started with -hs option<br>• the current isCOBOL Server started with both -as and -hs options <br><br>The URL is in the form *http://servername:port*. <br><br>The isUpdater utility, automatically invoked by the isCOBOL Client, will append "swupdater.properties" to the URL in order to download that configuration file.<br>For example, setting *iscobol.as.clientupdate.site=http://192.168.0.1:10996* will cause isUpdater to download the file "http://192.168.0.1:10996/swupdater.properties" via HTTP GET method. |
| iscobol.as.clientupdate.version | This property specifies the isCOBOL build required client side. If the Client is running a build whose number is less than the value of this property, then the Client will automatically update itself before starting any COBOL program. <br><br>By default, the value of this property matches with the build of the runtime library installed in the isCOBOL Server. |
| iscobol.as.debugport_range | This property specifies the range of ports that can be used to connect remote debuggers.<br>The property is evaluated only when [iscobol.as.multitasking](#as_multitasking) is set either to 1 or 2. The debugger will use the first available port in this range, unless a specific port was specified through the -debugport option on the Client’s command line.<br>The value can be expressed in two ways:<br>• a list of port numbers separated by comma, e.g. "9991,9992,9993.9994",<br>• the minimum port number and the maximum port number separated by hyphen, e.g. "9991-9994". <br>The default value is 9999-10099. |
| iscobol.as.digest | This property specifies the algorithm used by isCOBOL Server to encrypt passwords. The same algorithm is used by the [A$GET_DIGEST](../../../Appendices/Library-Routines/A$GET_DIGEST) library routine.<br>Possible values are: <br><br>**LEGACY** = Use the same hash method used by isCOBOL 2012 R2 and previous releases<br>**MD5** = Use MD5<br>*SHA-1* = Use SHA-1 <br><br>The file *password.properties* generated with a specific algorithm is not compatible with an isCOBOL Server that uses a different algorithm. <br><br>The default value is SHA-1. |
| iscobol.as.fileserver (boolean) <span id="as_fileserver"></span> | **True** = Start File Server services along with isCOBOL Server.<br>**False** = Don’t start File Server services along with isCOBOL Server. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.as.fileserver.port | This property specifies the port used by the File Server. <br><br>The default value is 10997. |
| iscobol.as.hook | This property specifies the name of the hook program that is be automatically executed by the Application Server each time a client connects. <br><br>See [Hook program](../../../../is-cobol-AS/Hook-program) for more information. |
| iscobol.as.httpserver (boolean) <span id="as_httpserver"></span> | **True** = Start the HTTP Server feature.<br>**False** = Don’t start the HTTP Server feature. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.as.httpserver.port | This property specifies the port used by the HTTP Server. <br><br>The default value is 10996. |
| iscobol.as.httpserver.root | This property specifies the base directory of the HTTP Server. <br><br>By default, the isCOBOL Server working directory is used. |
| iscobol.as.ide (boolean) <span id="as_ide"></span> | **True** = Allow remote IDEs to compile and run programs.<br>**False** = Don’t serve remote IDEs. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.as.info.arguments | This property returns the arguments of the client command-line. |
| iscobol.as.info.entering | This property returns the client status to the hook program. There are two possible values: <br><br>**1** = Program starting<br>**0** = Program exiting |
| iscobol.as.info.host | This property returns the hostname of the current client. |
| iscobol.as.info.program | This property returns the program name of the client command-line. |
| iscobol.as.info.userid | This property returns the user ID used by the client to connect. |
| iscobol.as.info.username | This property returns the user name used by the client to connect. |
| iscobol.as.logfile | This property specifies the path of the log file for the Application Server activities. |
| iscobol.as.logging (boolean) <span id="as_logging"></span> | **True** = Application Server activities are traced and logged.<br>**False** = Tracing is disabled. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.as.logging.exception (boolean) <span id="as_logging_exception"></span> | **True** = Print exceptions caused by remote method invocation (RMI).<br>**False** = Hide exceptions caused by remote method invocation (RMI). <br><br>The default value is False. <br><br>This property is evaluated only when [iscobol.as.logging (boolean)](#as_logging) is set to true. |
| iscobol.as.max_connections | This property sets the maximum number of concurrent connections allowed by the isCOBOL Server. <br><br>Valid range: 1 ~ 2147483647. <br><br>The default value is 512. |
| iscobol.as.multitasking <span id="as_multitasking"></span> | This property specifies if the isCOBOL Server should create a new thread in the current JVM or a new separate process when a client connects.<br>Possible values are: <br><br>**0** = Create a new thread for every client.<br>**1** = Create a separate process for every client.<br>**2** = Create a new thread for clients launched without -d option and create a separate process for clients launched with -d option. <br><br>Programs loaded from [iscobol.remote.code_prefix](#remote_code_prefix), File Server clients, isCOBOL utilities and the isCOBOL Server Administration Panel are not affected by this property, they always run as internal threads. <br><br>Setting *iscobol.as.multitasking=2* is useful for production environments if you need to debug without blocking other users. Multiple clients launched with -d option must use different debug ports (see -debugport option in [Format 6](../../../../is-cobol-AS/Usage-of-isCOBOL-Client/Usage-of-isCOBOL-Client#format-6) of isCOBOL Client usage). When *iscobol.as.multitasking=0*, if a client starts in debug mode, all the other clients can’t work until the debug session terminates. <br><br>Clients running in a separate task due to this property are isolated from other clients and therefore<br>• they can’t get the list of connected users by calling [A$LIST_USERS](../../../Appendices/Library-Routines/A$LIST_USERS/A$LIST_USERS),<br>• they can’t get the list of active locks by calling [A$LIST_LOCKS](../../../Appendices/Library-Routines/A$LIST_LOCKS/A$LIST_LOCKS),<br>• they can’t share custom information by calling [A$USERINFO](../../../Appendices/Library-Routines/A$USERINFO/A$USERINFO),<br>• they can’t send messages to other clients by calling [A$SEND_MESSAGE](../../../Appendices/Library-Routines/A$SEND_MESSAGE). <br><br>Note - Lock managers activated by [iscobol.file.lock_manager *](#file_lockmanager) don’t work in the Application Server if multitasking is enabled. When multitasking is enabled, lock managers work only in the File Server. <br><br>The default value is 2. |
| iscobol.as.panel.custom_font | This property specifies the font used by the isCOBOL Server Administration Panel.It accepts a pair of font name and font size separated by dash. For example, in order to use Arial size 16, set: <br><br>*iscobol.as.panel.custom_font=Arial-16* <br><br>By default the isCOBOL Server Administration Panel uses the label font set in the current Look & Feel. |
| iscobol.as.panel.refresh_timeout * | This property sets the timeout in seconds for the automatic refresh of the isCOBOL Server Administration Panel lists. The automatic refresh must be activated by the user by clicking on the “Auto refresh” check box in the tool-bar. The value -1 disables the automatic refresh feature. <br><br>The default value is 30. |
| iscobol.as.password_file | This property specifies an alternate name and path for the *password.properties* file.<br>Example: <br><br>*iscobol.as.password_file=/etc/as_pwd.txt* <br><br>The default value is "password.properties". |
| iscobol.as.stop_thread * <span id="as_stop_thread"></span> | This property affects the killing of Client connections performed by the isCOBOL Server. A connection may be killed in three conditions:<br>• if the Client is terminated via the command iscclient -kill.<br>• if the Client is terminated via the isCOBOL Server’s administration panel<br>• if the Client is terminated by the "check alive" feature of the isCOBOL Server (see [iscobol.as.check_alive_interval](#as_check_alive_interval)) <br><br>When set to a value of zero or greater it specifies the number of seconds to wait for the Client to terminate by cleaning up anything it needs to do before dying. When the time expires, if the Client is still alive, then it is terminated regardless of its status. Setting this property to zero is not good practice as it will always forcibly terminate the Clients. Reasonable values are 1 or greater. <br><br>When set to -1, no timeout is used and the Server waits for the Client to terminate in a clean way, even if it means waiting endlessly. <br><br>The default value is -1. <br><br>**Note** - this feature is no longer supported since Java 20. |
| iscobol.as.turborun (boolean) <span id="as_turborun"></span> | **True** = Start TurboRun along with isCOBOL Server.<br>**False** = Don’t start TurboRun along with isCOBOL Server. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.as.turborun.chdir (boolean) | **True** = Relative paths are resolved on the trun executable working directory.<br>**False** = Relative paths are resolved on the isCOBOL Server working directory. <br><br>The default value is False. <br><br>**Note** - it affects only COBOL routines. External Java and C routines are not affected. |
| iscobol.as.turborun.port | This property specifies the port used by TurboRun. <br><br>The default value is 10995. |
| iscobol.as.use_aliases (boolean) <span id="as_use_aliases"></span> | **True** = The name of the program specified in the Client command line is searched among aliases defined in the server configuration file.<br>**False** = The name of the program specified in the Client command line is actually the program to start. <br><br>The default value is False. <br><br>For more information see [Working with Aliases](../../../../is-cobol-AS/Working-with-Aliases). |
| iscobol.net.ssl.key_store | If this properties contains a path, then isCOBOL Server and LoadBalancer will consider that path as a JKS keystore containing a certificate and it will accept only SSL connections: the certificate must contain private and public key and must be suitable for a server. <br><br>See [TLS/SSL support](../../../../is-cobol-AS/TLS-SSL-support) for details. |
| iscobol.net.ssl.key_store_password | This property specifies the password of the keystore when requested. <br><br>See [TLS/SSL support](../../../../is-cobol-AS/TLS-SSL-support) for details. |

The following properties are considered by both isCOBOL Server and Client

| | |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.hostname | This property specifies the host name of the machine that is running the server in an Application Server environment. <br><br>In the Client configuration it is possible to specify multiple values separated by comma. It’s good practice to have the same number of values in iscobol.port. The client will attempt to connect to the fist available hostname and port pair. Hostnames and ports are paired from the first in the list to the last, such as hostname1:port1, hostname2:port2 and so on.<br>If the numbers of specified hostnames and ports do not match, the last in the shorter list will be used for creating all remaining pairs. <br><br>The default value is 127.0.0.1. |
| iscobol.port | This property specifies the port used by the Application Server. In the Client configuration it is possible to specify multiple values separated by comma. It’s good practice to have the same number of values in iscobol.hostname. The client will attempt to connect to the fist available hostname and port pair. Hostnames and ports are paired from the first in the list to the last, such as hostname1:port1, hostname2:port2 and so on.If the numbers of specified hostnames and ports do not match, the last in the shorter list will be used for creating all remaining pairs. The default value is 10999. |
| iscobol.runtime.cs.version | This property returns the version number of the client/server. |
| | |

The following properties are to be used client side only:

| | |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.net.ssl.trust_store | If this property contains a path, then isCOBOL Client and the HTTPClient class will use an SSL connection, getting information about the sever certificate from the JKS keystore indicated by the path: a special value of '*' directs the framework to use the system Java keystore. <br><br>See [TLS/SSL support](../../../../is-cobol-AS/TLS-SSL-support) for details. |
| iscobol.net.ssl.trust_store_password | This property specifies the password of the keystore when requested. <br><br>See [TLS/SSL support](../../../../is-cobol-AS/TLS-SSL-support) for details. |
| iscobol.remote_conf | This property specifies a remote configuration file to be used in an Application Server environment. The settings of this configuration file are appended to the settings in the isCOBOL Server’s configuration, if any, and the resulting configuration is used in the runtime session running within the isCOBOL Server. <br><br>This property affects both the isCOBOL Client in a thin client environment and the isCOBOL Framework calling remote programs. <br><br>This property must be set client side but it must point to a file located in the server machine where isCOBOL Server is running. |
| iscobol.timeout | This property specifies the connection timeout in seconds. <br><br>The default value is system dependent. |
| iscobol.user.name | This property allows you to set the user name to log in to the isCOBOL Server’s services: Application Server, File Server and Remote Calls. <br><br>The property also specifies the value returned in the USER-ID data item of the SYSTEM-INFORMATION group item. |
| iscobol.user.password | This property allows you to set the user password to log in to the isCOBOL Server’s services: Application Server, File Server and Remote Calls. <br><br>(iscobol.user.passwd is supported for backward compatibility) |
| | |

#### LoadBalancer Configuration

LoadBalancer properties can be set exclusively in the configuration file passed as parameter on the iscblancer command-line.

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.balancer.hostname | This property specifies the host name of the machine that is running the LoadBalancer. <br><br>The default value is 127.0.0.1. |
| iscobol.balancer.logfile | This property specifies the path of the log file for the LoadBalancer activities. |
| iscobol.balancer.logging (boolean) | **True** = LoadBalancer activities are traced and logged.<br>**False** = Tracing is disabled. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.balancer.port | This property specifies the port used by the LoadBalancer. <br><br>The default value is 10999. |
| iscobol.balancer.update.interval | This property specifies the number of seconds between the check-alive operations performed by the LoadBalancer. <br><br>The default value is 60. |
| iscobol.balancer.update.timeout | This property specifies the connection timeout for the check-alive operations performed by the LoadBalancer. <br><br>The default value is 60. |

#### Print Configuration

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.print.default_font | This property specifies the default font to be used while printing text for which no font was specified.<br>The isCOBOL Framework allows you to specify the font for the whole print job, for specific text or for page columns via the [WIN$PRINTER](../../../Appendices/Library-Routines/WIN$PRINTER/WIN$PRINTER) routine and the [P$ Routines](../../../Appendices/Library-Routines/P$-Routines/P$-Routines) routines.<br>When these routines are not involved, an undefined font is used.<br>This property allows you to specify which font should be used instead in these cases. <br><br>The value format is: *FontName-FontStyle-FontDim* <br><br>*FontName* is the name of the font, i.e. Consolas.<br>*FontStyle* is the style of the font such as bold, italic or bolditalic.<br>*FontDim* is the dimension of the font. <br><br>If *FontName* cannot be found, then *FontStyle* and *FontDim* are applied to the undefined font loaded by the runtime. |
| iscobol.print.memory (boolean) <span id="print_memory"></span> | **True** = Print jobs are stored in memory. As a result, the process is faster but more memory is occupied.<br>**False** = Print jobs are stored on the hard disk. One or more temporary files will be created in the user’s Temp directory. <br><br>In a thin client environment this property must be set on the machine where the print job is generated, that by default is the client machine. If you moved the print job to the server side via [WINPRINT-SET-PRINTER-AS](../../../Appendices/Library-Routines/WIN$PRINTER/WINPRINT-SET-PRINTER-AS), then set this property in the server side configuration. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.print.pdf_async (boolean) | **True** = The close of the print file is performed in a separate thread, allowing the program to continue with the rest of the operations. The STOP RUN statement doesn’t terminate the run unit until the print thread is terminated.<br>**False** = The close of the print file is performed in the current thread, so the program waits before continuing with the rest of the operations. <br><br>This setting affects print files assigned to "-P PDF". <br><br>Setting this property to true is not suggested if the program needs to automatically open the PDF file when the print job is completed. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.print.preview.icon | This property specifies a custom icon for the print preview dialog shown when you close a file whose physical name is “-P PREVIEW”. It must point to a file of type BMP, JPG, GIF, ICO and PNG. |
| iscobol.print.preview.title | This property specifies the title of the print preview dialog shown when you close a file whose physical name is “-P PREVIEW”. The default title is "Print Preview". |
| iscobol.print.spooler_async (boolean) | **True** = The close of the print file is performed in a separate thread, allowing the program to continue with the rest of the operations. The STOP RUN statement doesn’t terminate the run unit until the print thread is terminated.<br>**False** = The close of the print file is performed in the current thread, so the program waits before continuing with the rest of the operations. <br><br>This setting affects print files assigned to the system spooler (e.g. print files assigned to "-P SPOOLER"). <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.printer.channels | This configuration variable is used to define and print to printer channels C01-C12. Specify the line numbers for each channel. Null entries are ignored. Those channels that have line number zero, function-names S01-S052, CSP, or are undefined, are set to line 1.<br>You can specify only a single line number for each channel. <br><br>Example: <br><br>*iscobol.printer.channels=1:3::3* <br><br>In this example C01 equals 1, C02 and C04 equal 3, while C03 equals 1 because it’s undefined. If a print statement specifies channel C03, the line is printed at line 1.<br>Any WRITE BEFORE/AFTER PAGE statements cause positioning to be at line 1. Each line advance increases the line number by one. A request to skip to a line number less than or equal to the current line causes a new page to begin. The appropriate number of line feeds are then generated. |
| iscobol.printer.dialog.always (boolean) | **True** = The *Choose Printer* dialog is shown each time the program opens a print file on a pipe. If the user doesn’t choose a printer, then the OPEN fails.<br>**False** = The *Choose Printer* dialog is never automatically shown by the runtime. <br><br>The default value is False. |

The following properties specify the default attributes for PDF prints. They affect PDF files created by writing to a file assigned to "-P PDF" or by using the *Save As* function in the print preview dialog.

Note that these values are read on the side where the printing takes place, e.g. on the client side if the PDF is written by the client, on the server side otherwise.

The print attributes can be changed at runtime by calling the [WINPRINT-SET-ATTRIBUTE](../../../Appendices/Library-Routines/WIN$PRINTER/WINPRINT-SET-ATTRIBUTE/WINPRINT-SET-ATTRIBUTE) function.

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.print.attribute.author | This property specifies the author of the PDF document. It can be any text. |
| iscobol.print.attribute.encryption <span id="print_attribute_encryption"></span> | This property allows you to activate encryption. It takes a numeric bitwise value where each bit sets a specific feature. If this value is set to 0 then no encryption takes place. <br><br>You can use the following constants, defined in [isprint.def](../../../Appendices/Copybooks/isprint.def), to activate the desired feature: <br><br>78 pdfcrypt-no value 0.<br>78 pdfcrypt-std-40 value 1.<br>78 pdfcrypt-std-128 value 2.<br>78 pdfcrypt-aes-128 value 3.<br>78 pdfcrypt-no-metadata value x#08.<br>78 pdfcrypt-embedded-files-only value x#10. <br><br>78 pdfcrypt-allow-printing value x#0100.<br>78 pdfcrypt-allow-modify-content value x#0200.<br>78 pdfcrypt-allow-copy value x#0400.<br>78 pdfcrypt-allow-modify-annotations value x#0800.<br>78 pdfcrypt-allow-fill-in value x#1000.<br>78 pdfcrypt-allow-screenreaders value x#2000.<br>78 pdfcrypt-allow-assembly value x#4000.<br>78 pdfcrypt-allow-degraded-printing value x#8000.<br>78 pdfcrypt-all-permissions value x#FF00. <br><br>Permissions are applied only if combined with a valid encryption, otherwise *all-permissions* is assumed. <br><br>Usage example: <br><br>*iscobol.print.attribute.encryption=258* <br><br>The resulting PDF will be printable, but it will not be possible to add annotations or copy the text to clipboard, as 258 is the sum between pdfcrypt-std-128 (2) and pdf-crypt-allow-printing (256). <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.print.attribute.expires | This property specifies the custom property "Expires". It can be any text. |
| iscobol.print.attribute.font_default | This property affects the generation of PDF files (e.g. files whose physical name starts with "-P PDF")and it specifies the name of the font to be used if the fonts set in the print job cannot be found in the system.<br>Usage example: <br><br>*iscobol.print.attribute.font_default=arial* |
| iscobol.print.attribute.font_folder | This property specifies the folders where the fonts used in the PDF document are installed. You can specify multiple folders separated by pipe, e.g. "C:\\myCustomFonts\|C:\\WINDOWS\\Fonts". The fonts loaded from these folders are just referenced in the PDF. <br><br>See [Font embedding in PDF files](../../../Appendices/Library-Routines/WIN$PRINTER/WINPRINT-SET-ATTRIBUTE/Font-embdedding-in-PDF-files) for more information. |
| iscobol.print.attribute.font_folder_embed | This property specifies the folders where the fonts used in the PDF document are installed. You can specify multiple folders separated by pipe, e.g. "C:\\myCustomFonts\|C:\\WINDOWS\\Fonts". The fonts loaded from these folders are physically embedded in the PDF. <br><br>See [Font embedding in PDF files](../../../Appendices/Library-Routines/WIN$PRINTER/WINPRINT-SET-ATTRIBUTE/Font-embdedding-in-PDF-files) for more information. |
| iscobol.print.attribute.jpeg | This property specifies the compression applied to images in the PDF document. It can be 0 if you want to keep images unchanged or it can range from 1 to 100 to indicate the image quality, where 1 is the lowest quality and 100 is the highest quality.<br>When this attribute is set, all images are internally translated to jpeg; this will remove transparency, if any. <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.print.attribute.keywords | This property specifies the keywords of the PDF document. It can be any text. |
| iscobol.print.attribute.owner_password | This property specifies the password of the owner of the document. If this value is not set, then a random password is created. It works only along with [iscobol.print.attribute.encryption](#print_attribute_encryption). |
| iscobol.print.attribute.pdfa | This property allows you to create a PDF/A document following a specific standard.<br>Possible values are "PDF/A-1A" and "PDF/A-1B", case insensitive. <br><br>If this property is not set, then a standard PDF is created. |
| iscobol.print.attribute.producer | This property specifies the producer of the PDF document. It can be any text. |
| iscobol.print.attribute.subject | This property specifies the subject of the PDF document, it can be any text. |
| iscobol.print.attribute.title | This property specifies the title of the PDF document, it can be any text. |
| iscobol.print.attribute.user_password | This property specifies the password of the user of the document. If this value is not set, then a default password is used as specified in the PDF specifics. It works only along with [iscobol.print.attribute.encryption](#print_attribute_encryption). |

#### IDE Reports Export feature Configuration

##### Export to Excel feature

The following properties are evaluated only when a Report designed with the IDE is printed to XLS or XLSX file as described in [Exporting to Excel file](../../../../is-cobol-IDE/Reports/How-to-print-Reports). They’re not considered by the Grid and List-Box’s export features.

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.export.excel.cell_ignore_background (boolean) | **True** = Ignores the background color of the cell.<br>**False** = Replicates the background color of the cell. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.export.excel.cell_ignore_borders (boolean) | **True** = Ignores the border style of the cell.<br>**False** = Replicates the border style of the cell. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.export.excel.cell_locked (boolean) | **True** = Mark cells as locked.<br>**False** = Don’t mark cells as locked. <br><br>Locking cells has no effect until you protect the worksheet. This operation can be performed using external tools like Microsoft Excel. <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.export.excel.cell_numeric_format | Specifies the number format for numeric cells. Refer to https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/kb/264372 for a list of valid values. <br><br>There is no default. |
| iscobol.export.excel.cell_wrap_text (boolean) | **True** = Long text wraps in the cell.<br>**False** = Long text is truncated. <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.export.excel.collapse_row_span (boolean) | **True** = Collapse row span and avoid merging cells across rows. It implies iscobol.export.excel.remove_rows_space=true.<br>**False** = Don’t collapse row span and allow merging cells across rows. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.export.excel.detect_cell_type (boolean) | **True** = Preserve the type of the original Report field expressions and use it for the cell data type.<br>**False** = Don’t preserve the type of the original Report field expressions and don’t use it for the cell data type. <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.export.excel.force_page_breaks (boolean) | **True** = Create page breaks in the Excel sheet.<br>**False** = Don’t create page breaks in the Excel sheet. <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.export.excel.freeze_page_header (boolean) | **True** = The Report page header is shown only on the top of the spreadsheet and is freezed during scrolling. When the content of the header changes, a new sheet is created.<br>**False** = The Report page header is shown for each Report page exported in the spreadsheet. <br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.export.excel.ignore_images (boolean) | **True** = Ignore graphics and export only the text.<br>**False** = Export both text and graphics. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.export.excel.remove_columns_space (boolean) | **0** = Empty spaces that could appear between columns should not be removed.<br>**1** = Empty spaces that could appear between columns should be removed.<br>**2** = Empty spaces that could appear between columns should be removed. In the resulting output, useless columns are then removed as well. <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.export.excel.remove_rows_space (boolean) | **True** = Empty spaces that could appear between rows should be removed.<br>**False** = Empty spaces that could appear between rows should not be removed. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.export.excel.whitepage_background (boolean) | **True** = Force cell white background.<br>**False** = Don’t force cell white background. <br><br>The default value is True. |

##### Report print

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.report.font.default | This property has the same meaning and usage of [iscobol.font.default *](#font_default),<br>It is considered by the IDE during Report print and preview, but not during Report painting. The Report Designer uses [iscobol.font.default *](#font_default) to represent the default font. <br><br>If not set, then [iscobol.font.default *](#font_default) is used also during Report print and preview. |
| iscobol.report.font.fixed | This property has the same meaning and usage of [iscobol.font.fixed *](#font_fixed),<br>It is considered by the IDE during Report print and preview, but not during Report painting. The Report Designer uses [iscobol.font.fixed *](#font_fixed) to represent the fixed font. <br><br>If not set, then [iscobol.font.fixed *](#font_fixed) is used also during Report print and preview. |
| iscobol.report.font.large | This property has the same meaning and usage of [iscobol.font.large *](#font_large),<br>It is considered by the IDE during Report print and preview, but not during Report painting. The Report Designer uses [iscobol.font.large *](#font_large) to represent the large font. <br><br>If not set, then [iscobol.font.large *](#font_large) is used also during Report print and preview. |
| iscobol.report.font.medium | This property has the same meaning and usage of [iscobol.font.medium *](#font_medium),<br>It is considered by the IDE during Report print and preview, but not during Report painting. The Report Designer uses [iscobol.font.medium *](#font_medium) to represent the medium font. <br><br>If not set, then [iscobol.font.medium *](#font_medium) is used also during Report print and preview. |
| iscobol.report.font.small | This property has the same meaning and usage of [iscobol.font.small *](#font_small),<br>It is considered by the IDE during Report print and preview, but not during Report painting. The Report Designer uses [iscobol.font.small *](#font_small) to represent the small font. <br><br>If not set, then [iscobol.font.small *](#font_small) is used also during Report print and preview. |
| iscobol.report.font.traditional | This property has the same meaning and usage of [iscobol.font.traditional *](#font_traditional),<br>It is considered by the IDE during Report print and preview, but not during Report painting. The Report Designer uses [iscobol.font.traditional *](#font_traditional) to represent the traditional font. <br><br>If not set, then [iscobol.font.traditional *](#font_traditional) is used also during Report print and preview. |

#### Update Facility Configuration

##### Server Configuration (swupdater.properties)

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| swupdater.items_list[.<os\>[.<arch\>]].<packageName\>. | This property defines a list of items that will be included or excluded in the update process. You can specify file names or relative paths. Relative paths will be resolved according to the directory specified by [swupdater.lib[.<os\>[.<arch\>]].<packageName\>](#swupdater_lib). Wildcards are accepted. Multiple items must be separated by comma. When specifying a path, the “/” file separator must be used, even on Windows. On platforms that are not Windows, the file names and paths specified by this property are case sensitive. <br><br>Some examples: <br><br>• A value of “file1,file” includes file1 and file2 in the list.<br>• A value of “file1,mydir/\*” includes file1 and all the files under mydir in the list. <br><br>*os* is optional and can be any of the following values: win, linux, mac, solaris. If omitted, this property is considered for all clients regardless of their platform, otherwise the property is considered only for clients of a specific platform. <br><br>*arch* is optional and can be either 32 or 64. If omitted, this property is considered for all clients regardless of their bitness, otherwise the property is considered only for clients of a specific bitness. <br><br>Items list are supported only when [swupdater.lib[.<os\>[.<arch\>]].<packageName\>](#swupdater_lib) points to a directory. If it points to a ZIP file, items list can’t be used. <br><br>The inclusion and exclusion of items is controlled by the [swupdater.exclude_items_list[.<os\>[.<arch\>]].<packagename\>](#swupdater_exclude) property. |
| swupdater.exclude_items_list[.<os\>[.<arch\>]].<packagename\> <span id="swupdater_exclude"></span> | This property specifies how to treat the items listed by [swupdater.lib[.<os\>[.<arch\>]].<packageName\>](#swupdater_lib). Possible values are: <br><br>**-1** - ignore the items list and include all the files in the update process.<br>**0** - include only the files specified by the items list in the update process and exclude all the others.<br>**1** - include all the files except the ones specified by the items list in the update process. <br><br>*os* is optional and can be any of the following values: win, linux, mac, solaris. If omitted, this property is considered for all clients regardless of their platform, otherwise the property is considered only for clients of a specific platform. <br><br>*arch* is optional and can be either 32 or 64. If omitted, this property is considered for all clients regardless of their bitness, otherwise the property is considered only for clients of a specific bitness. <br><br>The default value is -1. |
| swupdater.version.<packageName\> | This property specifies the release of the package available on the server. |
| swupdater.lib[.<os\>[.<arch\>]].<packageName\> <span id="swupdater_lib"></span> | This property specifies the ZIP file or the directory containing the new version of the package. This entry can contain an absolute URL or a relative URL. <br><br>*os* is optional and can be any of the following values: win, linux, mac, solaris. If omitted, this property is considered for all clients regardless of their platform, otherwise the property is considered only for clients of a specific platform. <br><br>*arch* is optional and can be either 32 or 64. If omitted, this property is considered for all clients regardless of their bitness, otherwise the property is considered only for clients of a specific bitness. <br><br>(swupdater.zipfile[.<os\>[.<arch\>]].<packageName\> is supported for backward compatibility) |

##### Client Configuration (isupdater.properties)

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| swupdater.site <span id="swupdater_site"></span> | This property specifies the HTTP URL to search for the updates. <br><br>The HTTP server could be either an external HTTP Server like IIS or Apache or an isCOBOL Server started with -hs option. <br><br>isUpdater will append "swupdater.properties" to this URL in order to download the "swupdater.properties" configuration file.<br>For example, setting *swupdater.site=http://192.168.0.1:10996* will cause isUpdater to download the file "http://192.168.0.1:10996/swupdater.properties" via HTTP GET method. |
| swupdater.logfile | This property specifies the pathname of the log file where isUpdater traces its activity when logging is enabled. <br><br>[isUpdater environment variables](./Configuration-Properties#isupdater-environment-variables) can be used in the value of this property. |
| swupdater.logging (boolean) | **True** = Trace the isUpdater activity into the log file defined by swupdater.logfile.<br>**False** = isUpdater activity is not traced. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| swupdater.jvm_options | This property specifies the Java options to be used with the new JVM instantiated by isUpdater to run the program specified by the value of [swupdater.mainclass](#swupdater_mainclass). Multiple options must be separated by a space. For example, if you want the new JVM to use up to 500 MB of heap memory and allocate 256 MB for the metaspace, set: <br><br>*swupdater.jvm_options=-Xmx500m -XX:MetaspaceSize=256m* <br><br>Note - the creation of a new JVM is conditioned by the [swupdater.new_jvm_always (boolean)](#swupdater_new_jvm_always) configuration property. |
| swupdater.mainclass <span id="swupdater_mainclass"></span> | This property specifies the class to run after the update checking. If this entry is missing then the isupdater tool terminates at the end of the update process. |
| swupdater.net.ssl.trust_store | If this property contains a path, then isUpdater will use an SSL connection, getting information about the sever certificate from the JKS keystore indicated by the path: a special value of '*' directs isUpdater to use the system Java keystore. <br><br>[isUpdater environment variables](./Configuration-Properties#isupdater-environment-variables) can be used in the value of this property. |
| swupdater.net.ssl.trust_store_password | This property specifies the password of the keystore when requested. |
| swupdater.new_jvm_always (boolean) <span id="swupdater_new_jvm_always"></span> | **True** = The program set by [swupdater.mainclass](#swupdater_mainclass) is always executed in a new JVM.<br>**False** = The program set by [swupdater.mainclass](#swupdater_mainclass) is executed in a new JVM only if an update occurred. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| swupdater.http.ignore_certificates (boolean) | **True** = If the handshaking fails due to invalid certificates, continue and connect anyway.<br>**False** = If the handshaking fails due to invalid certificates, stop.<br><br>The default value is False.<br><br>Note - this property should be set to true only for test purposes. It’s not good practice to ignore handshaking errors. |
| swupdater.version.<packageName\> | This property specifies the release of the package <packageName\> currently installed. This is free text, both letters and numbers are allowed. The version can be expressed in dotted form as usually software versions are expressed. If the expession between dots is a decimal number then it will be evaluated in such way, otherwise it will be evluated as alphabetical expression, for example: "10.4" is less than "10.10" and "10.4a" is greater than "10.10a".<br>The isupdater tool will download new files only if the version specified by this property is less than the version available on the server. |
| swupdater.directory.<packageName\> | This property specifies the directory in which the new software will be downloaded. If this entry is missing, then files are downloaded in the isupdater working directory. <br><br>[isUpdater environment variables](./Configuration-Properties#isupdater-environment-variables) can be used in the value of this property. |
| swupdater.directory.clean.<packageName\> (boolean) | **True** = Clean the content of the client directory before downloading files from the server. Some jar libraries may not be removed as they’re locked by the client JVM, in this case they are made zero bytes in size.<br>**False** = Just download the files from the server to the client directory. <br><br>**Note** - the client runtime must be version 2018 R1 or greater for this feature to work. Previous runtime versions don’t support it. <br><br>The default value is False. |

##### isUpdater environment variables

The following variables can be used in the isUpdater client configuration:

| Variable | Value |
| --- | --- |
| $\{iscobol.home} | The isCOBOL home directory. <br><br>This path is calculated by removing the last directory from the path of the *isupdater.jar* library. For example, if *isupdater.jar* was loaded from '/opt/isCOBOL/lib', then *iscobol.home* will be '/opt/isCOBOL'. |
| $\{java.home} | The Java home directory. |
| $\{user.home} | The user’s home directory. |
| $\{user.temp} | The user’s temp directory. |

For example, if you wish the log to be generated in the user’s home directory, set:

```cobol
swupdater.logfile=${user.home}/isupdater.log
```

isUpdater will internally translate "${user.home}/isupdater.log" to "C:\\Users\\UserName\\isupdater.log" when running on Windows and "/home/UserName/isupdater.log" when running on Linux/Unix.

#### Library Routines Configuration

##### C$COPY

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.ccopy.client_temp_as_base_dir (boolean) | **True** = In thin client, for client-side files, use the user’s TEMP folder as base directory for relative file paths.<br>**False** = In thin client, for client-side files, use the client’s working directory as base directory for relative file paths. <br><br>The default value is False. |

##### C$EASYOPEN

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.easyopen.method | This property specifies which method must be used by C$EASYOPEN to open the file. Possible values are:<br>**JDIC** = use the JDIC component<br>**JAVA** = use the java.awt.Desktop class<br>**START** = use the START command on Windows<br>**WINAPI** = call the ShellExecuteA Windows function. <br><br>The default value is JAVA. |

##### C$MYFILE

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.cmyfile.classname_only (boolean) | **True** = only the class name (without path) is returned by C$MYFILE.<br>**False** = a full file name (path and file name) is returned by C$MYFILE. <br><br>The default value is False. |

##### C$SOCKET

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.csocket.keepalive (boolean) | **True** = The attribute SO_KEEPALIVE is turned on for sockets managed by the C$SOCKET routine.<br>**False** = The attribute SO_KEEPALIVE is turned off for sockets managed by the C$SOCKET routine. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.csocket.maxbuffersize | Sets the SO_RCVBUF option value for sockets created by [C$SOCKET](../../../Appendices/Library-Routines/C$SOCKET/C$SOCKET). <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.csocket.reuseaddr (boolean) | **True** = The attribute SO_REUSEADDR is turned on for sockets managed by [C$SOCKET](../../../Appendices/Library-Routines/C$SOCKET/C$SOCKET) routine.<br>**False** = The attribute SO_REUSEADDR is turned off for sockets managed by [C$SOCKET](../../../Appendices/Library-Routines/C$SOCKET/C$SOCKET) routine. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.csocket.tcp_nodelay (boolean) | **True** = socket packets are sent immediately.<br>**False** = socket packets are delayed using the Nagle algorithm. <br><br>The default value is True. |

##### C$XML

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.cxml.indent_number | This property changes the layout of XML files generated by [C$XML](../../../Appendices/Library-Routines/C$XML/C$XML).<br>Possible values are: <br><br>**-1** = all elements in the same line, no line feeds and no indentation.<br>**0** = every element in a separate line, no indentation.<br>**>0** = every element in a separate line, with a given indentation. The value of the property is the number of spaces used for the indentation. <br><br>The default value is -1. |

##### KEISEN

| | |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.keisen.method | This property specifies the line drawing method for the KEISEN routines. Valid values are 1 and 2. <br><br>The default value is 1. |
| | |

##### SYSTEM

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.system.exec | This property specifies the name of a command to be executed when the SYSTEM routine is called. The parameter of the SYSTEM routine will be passed to the command. For example, setting *iscobol.system.exec=sh -c* and executing *CALL "SYSTEM" using "ls >out"* will cause the following command to be executed: *sh -c "ls >out"*. <br><br>If this property is set to the special value "c" (lower case), the system() C routine will be used by isCOBOL in place of the Java API each time the SYSTEM library routine is called.<br>In order to have the system() C routine available on Windows, the msvcrt library must be loaded in memory, therefore you should set also *iscobol.shared_library_list=msvcrt.dll* in the configuration. <br><br>Programs compiled with the -cp option require the parameter passed to SYSTEM to be null terminated. <br><br>Quotes may be used to isolate a command line parameter that includes spaces. When this property is set either to "c" or to the system command interpreter, quotes are stripped under Linux/Unix and preserved under Windows. |

#### Utilities Configuration

##### COBFILEIO

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.cobfileio.efd_path | Specifies the directory containing the EFD file |
| iscobol.cobfileio.output_path | Specifies the output directory where COBFILEIO will place the generated items |
| iscobol.cobfileio.package | Specifies the Java package name to be defined in the generated source code |
| iscobol.cobfileio.use_resource_file | If set to ‘1’ or ‘True’, this setting causes all the strings to be generated with the ‘-r’ prefix so that they will be loaded from resource files at run time. For a correct result, the following entries must be available in the resource file: <br><br>number_too_large=*Number Too Large.
