Field*<br>max_number=*Max Number*<br>current_number=*Current Number*<br>string_too_long=*String Too Long. Field*<br>max_length=*Max Length*<br>current_length=*Current Length*<br>wrong_scale=*Wrong Scale. Field*<br>max_scale=*Max Scale*<br>current_scale=*Current Scale* |

##### GIFE

| | |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.gife.custom_font | This property specifies the font used by GIFE’s dialogs.<br>It accepts a pair of font name and font size separated by dash. For example, in order to use Arial size 16, set: <br><br>*iscobol.gife.custom_font=Arial-16* <br><br>By default GIFE uses the label font set in the current Look & Feel. |
| iscobol.gife.efd_directory | This property specifies the default folder for EFD dictionaries.<br>In the "Open File" dialog, GIFE automatically fills the "EFD File Path" field if:<br>• an EFD dictionary with the same name of the file exists in the folder where the opened file is located, or<br>• an EFD dictionary with the same name of the file exists in the folder set by *iscobol.gife.efd_directory*<br>When passing filename and EFD dictionary name as command line parameter, the folder set by *iscobol.gife.efd_directory* is used to resolve relative EFD pathnames. |
| iscobol.gife.encrypt | This property is considered for JIsam files. <br><br>**True** = GIFE considers the file as encrypted. It uses the key specified by the property [iscobol.file.encryption.key *](#file_encryption_key) to decrypt the file content.<br>**False** = GIFE considers the file as plain. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.gife.open_mode_io (boolean) | **True** = GIFE opens the file for i/o as default<br>**False** = GIFE opens the file for input as default <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.gife.rel_rec_size=n | This property specifies the record size for the open of a relative file.<br>*n* can be any positive number. |
| iscobol.gife.num_convention=conv | This property specifies the numeric convention used to represent numeric fields when a EFD dictionary is provided.conv can be any of the following:<br>• -dca<br>• -dcb<br>• -dcd<br>• -dcdm<br>• -dci<br>• -dcii<br>• -dcm<br>• -dcmi<br>• -dcn<br>• -dcr<br>• -dcv <br><br>The default value is -dca. |
| | |

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.isl.custom_font | This property specifies the font used by ISL’s dialogs.<br>It accepts a pair of font name and font size separated by dash. For example, in order to use Arial size 16, set: <br><br>*iscobol.isl.custom_font=Arial-16* <br><br>By default ISL uses the label font set in the current Look & Feel. |

| iscobol.isl.java_options | This property specifies the options to be passed to the Java Runtime. If set, the corresponding field in the ISL GUI will be automatically filled with this value. |
| iscobol.isl.laf | This property specifies the LAF used by the launched program. If set, the corresponding field in the ISL GUI will be automatically filled with this value.<br>Possible values are: <br><br>• system<br>• --system<br>• metal<br>• --metal<br>• motif<br>• --motif<br>• GTK<br>• --GTK<br>• nimbus<br>• --nimbus |

| iscobol.isl.prog_arguments | This property specifies the arguments for the COBOL program. If set, the corresponding field in the ISL GUI will be automatically filled with this value. |

##### ISMIGRATE

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.ctree.library | Set this property to “ctreestd” to use the c-tree standalone library (ctreestd) instead of the client/server library (ctree) for data migration. The standalone library is faster than the client/server library in reading and writing c-tree data. However, the standalone library is suitable only for data migration as it lacks many features when compared with the client/server library (for example, file encryption is not supported). The c-tree standalone library is single-threaded so it must not be used in application server environments and applications that use multithreading. In general, the use of the c-tree standalone library in a COBOL application is not suggested. <br><br>The default value is "ctree". |
| iscobol.ismigrate.custom_font | This property specifies the font used by ISMIGRATE’s dialogs.<br>It accepts a pair of font name and font size separated by dash. For example, in order to use Arial size 16, set: <br><br>*iscobol.ismigrate.custom_font=Arial-16* <br><br>By default ISMIGRATE uses the label font set in the current Look & Feel. |
| iscobol.ismigrate_additional_bytes | This property increases the output record length by the specified number of bytes.<br>The new bytes appended to the record are initialized to space. |
| iscobol.ismigrate_hook <span id="ismigrate_hook"></span> | This property specifies the name of a custom program that ISMIGRATE will call for each record read from the input file before writing the record to the output file. The feature allows you to alter the record content during the migration process.<br>The hook program is called as a standard COBOL program so it must be available in the code-prefix or in the Classpath, depending on the current configuration.<br>The program receives the following Linkage parameters: <br><br>01 INPUT-FULLNAME PIC X ANY LENGTH.<br>01 INPUT-RECORD   PIC X ANY LENGTH. <br><br>As the parameter names say, the former receives the full path name of the input file while the latter receives the content of the record read.<br>The length of INPUT-RECORD shouldn’t be changed by the hook program otherwise an unexpected result may occur since ISMIGRATE uses the original record length. <br><br>If the hook program exits with a negative return code (e.g. GOBACK -1), then the record is skipped by ISMIGRATE. <br><br>The hook program is not cancelled after being called. |
| iscobol.ismigrate_ignore_write_errors (boolean) <span id="ismigrate_ignore_write_errors"></span> | **True** = Continue the data migration even if write errors occur.<br>**False** = Stop if a write error occurs. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.ismigrate_input_encrypt (boolean) <span id="ismigrate_input_encrypt"></span> | **True** = Consider the input files as encrypted.<br>**False** = Don’t consider the input files as encrypted. <br><br>If the input files are JIsam, use *iscobol.ismigrate_input_encryption_key* to specify the encryption key. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.ismigrate_input_encryption_key | This property specifies the encryption key to be used when reading JIsam files during the migration process.<br><br>It’s evaluated when [iscobol.ismigrate_input_encrypt (boolean)](#ismigrate_input_encrypt) is true. <br><br>It overrides [iscobol.file.encryption.key *](#file_encryption_key) if both properties are set. |
| iscobol.ismigrate_input_file_index | This property defines the source file system.<br>The possible values of [iscobol.file.index](#file_index) are suitable also for this property. |
| iscobol.ismigrate_input_jdbc_driver | This property specifies the jdbc driver for the input files when migrating between two databases using “easydb” as ismigrate_input_file_index. <br><br>If you're not migrating between two databases, but between a database and another file system, then set the standard [iscobol.jdbc.driver](#jdbc_driver). |
| iscobol.ismigrate_input_jdbc_url | This property specifies the jdbc url for the input files when migrating between two databases using “easydb” as ismigrate_input_file_index. <br><br>If you're not migrating between two databases, but between a database and another file system, then set the standard [iscobol.jdbc.url](#jdbc_url). |
| iscobol.ismigrate_logfile <span id="ismigrate_logfile"></span> | This property specifies the path name of the ismigrate log file. By default, a file named “ismigrate.log” is created in the working directory. |
| iscobol.ismigrate_logging | **0** = The ISMIGRATE activity doesn’t produce any log.<br>**1** = The ISMIGRATE configuration and activity are traced in the file pointed by [iscobol.ismigrate_logfile](#ismigrate_logfile).<br>**2** = The ISMIGRATE configuration and activity are traced in the file pointed by [iscobol.ismigrate_logfile](#ismigrate_logfile), and a dump file is generated for each migration that failed for one of these reasons:<br>• a write error occurred, and [iscobol.ismigrate_ignore_write_errors (boolean)](#ismigrate_ignore_write_errors) is set to true,<br>• the hook program specified by [iscobol.ismigrate_hook](#ismigrate_hook) returned a negative exit code,<br>• the verification performed due to [iscobol.ismigrate_verify_records (boolean)](#ismigrate_verify_records) set to true found one or more records that don’t match.<br>The dump files contain the hex content of the problematic records.<br>Note that offsets traced in the dump file start at 1, while offsets in the EFD and ISS dictionaries start at 0. Keep this in mind when searching for the problematic field in the dictionary based on the offset traced in the dump.<br>The dump files are generated in the same directory as the log file and have the same names as the input files that were not successfully migrated.<br>**256** = Same as 2, but with low-level activity tracing, including the result of each individual I/O operation. This may produce very large log files if the input data file contains many records, so use this value with caution. <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.ismigrate_no_alphabet (boolean) | **True** = Don’t pass the collating sequence to the output file handler. This is useful when migrating to file systems like Dci, which do not support collating sequences.<br>**False** = Pass the collating sequence to the output file handler. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.ismigrate_no_directories (boolean) | **True** = Consider the first parameter (*InputFile*) as the name of the source file, and the second parameter (*OutputDir*) as the name of the destination file. Useful to migrate one file at a time.<br>**False** = Consider the first parameter (*InputFile*) as a list of files, and the second parameter (*OutputDir*) as the name of the destination folder. Useful to migrate multiple files at a time. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.ismigrate_no_echo (boolean) | **True** = No output is printed on the system output. It might be useful in a scenario where you call ISMIGRATE from a COBOL program managing errors and record count via Linkage parameters without the need to have them printed on the console.<br>**False** = Some information is printed on the system output. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.ismigrate_no_make (boolean) | **True** = Don’t perform the build of the output file. This is useful when optimizing the migration of file systems like EasyDB, which create a table when opening a file.<br>**False** = Build the output file. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.ismigrate_no_output_directory (boolean) | **True** = Ignore the second parameter.<br>**False** = Consider the second parameter. <br><br>This setting is useful when migrating to databases unless you wish to include a portion of the file path in the destination table name (e.g. if you set [iscobol.easydb.dirlevel](#easydb_dirlevel) to value greater than zero). <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.ismigrate_open_extend (boolean) | **True** = If the output file already exists, ISMIGRATE opens it in EXTEND mode and appends to it.<br>**False** = If the output file already exists, ISMIGRATE resets (overwrites) it. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.ismigrate_output_encrypt (boolean) <span id="ismigrate_output_encrypt"></span> | **True** = The output files are created with the encrypted flag. Note that some file handlers ignore such flag.<br>**False** = The encryption flag is not used to create the output files. <br><br>If the output files are JIsam, use [iscobol.ismigrate_output_encryption_key](#ismigrate_output_encryption_key) to specify the encryption key. <br><br>The default value is False. <br><br>*iscobol.ismigrate_make_encrypt* is still supported for backward compatibility. |
| iscobol.ismigrate_output_encryption_key <span id="ismigrate_output_encryption_key"></span> | This property specifies the encryption key to be used when writing JIsam files during the migration process.<br>It’s evaluated when [iscobol.ismigrate_output_encrypt (boolean)](#ismigrate_output_encrypt) is true. <br><br>It overrides [iscobol.file.encryption.key *](#file_encryption_key) if both properties are set. |
| iscobol.ismigrate_output_file_index | This property defines the destination file system.<br>The possible values of [iscobol.file.index](#file_index) are suitable also for this property. |
| iscobol.ismigrate_output_jdbc_driver | This property specifies the jdbc driver for the output files when migrating between two databases using “easydb” as ismigrate_output_file_index. <br><br>If you're not migrating between two databases, but between a database and another file system, then set the standard [iscobol.jdbc.driver](#jdbc_driver). |
| iscobol.ismigrate_output_jdbc_url | This property specifies the jdbc url for the output files when migrating between two databases using “easydb” as ismigrate_output_file_index. <br><br>If you're not migrating between two databases, but between a database and another file system, then set the standard [iscobol.jdbc.url](#jdbc_url). |
| iscobol.ismigrate_remove_extension | This property removes the extension from the incoming data file name before processing it.<br>Because some data file systems automatically add a file extension to the filename (JIsam and c-tree for instance), you can use this property to remove an existing extension to avoid a file not found error. <br><br>For example, ISMIGRATE will ignore the .dat extension of a passed file name "filename.dat" if *iscobol.ismigrate_remove_extension=dat* is set. |
| iscobol.ismigrate_strip_extension (boolean) | **True** = Strip the extension from the file name passed to ISMIGRATE before naming the output file. Set this to avoid a double extension created when the file extension automatically added by the output file handler would be added to the input file extension.<br>**False** = Use the full file name passed to ISMIGRATE when naming the output file. <br><br>The default is False. |
| iscobol.ismigrate_verify_records (boolean) <span id="ismigrate_verify_records"></span> | **True** = Check if the records in the output file match with the records in the input file after the file has been migrated.<br>**False** = Don’t check if the records in the output file match with the records in the input file after the file has been migrated. <br><br>The default value is False. |

##### JDBC2FD

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Property | Meaning |
| iscobol.jdbc2fd.custom_font | This property specifies the font used by JDBC2FD’s dialogs.<br>It accepts a pair of font name and font size separated by dash. For example, in order to use Arial size 16, set: <br><br>*iscobol.jdbc2fd.custom_font=Arial-16* <br><br>By default JDBC2FD uses the label font set in the current Look & Feel. |
| iscobol.jdbc2fd.current_schema_only | **True** = Show only tables of the current schema.<br>**False** = Show all tables returned by the database metadata. <br><br>The default value is False. |

#### isCOBOL Code Coverage Configuration

The following property must be set when the runtime starts and are evaluated if the [-coverage](../Runtime-Framework/Runtime-Options#-coverage) option is used.

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.coverage.analysis.excludes | This property specifies a comma separated list of COBOL classes that must be excluded from the analysis. <br><br>Regular expressions can be used to specify a pattern, for example:<br>*iscobol.coverage.analysis.excludes=SAMPLE,GUI.\**<br>will exclude the SAMPLE class and all the COBOL classes whose name starts with "GUI". <br><br>By default, all COBOL classes are analyzed. |
| iscobol.coverage.analysis.includes | This property specifies a comma separated list of COBOL classes that must be included in the analysis. <br><br>Regular expressions can be used to specify a pattern, for example:<br>*iscobol.coverage.analysis.includes=SAMPLE,IO.\**<br>will include the SAMPLE class and all the COBOL classes whose name starts with "IO". <br><br>By default, all COBOL classes are analyzed. |
| iscobol.coverage.append | This property specifies if the coverage report must be merged with existing reports or not. <br>It affects only XML reports, so it’s considered only if [iscobol.coverage.xml](#coverage_xml) is set.<br>Possible values are:<br>**0** = if the XML file already exists, overwrite it<br>**1** = if the XML file already exists, merge the contents of the existing XML with the content of the current XML. <br><br>This property can also be set to a comma-separated list of existing XML reports, e.g. *iscobol.coverage.append=C:\\Reports\\report1.xml,C:\\Reports\\report2.xml* <br>In this case, the resulting XML will be created by merging all the XML files in the list with the new coverage report. <br><br>The default value is 0. |
| iscobol.coverage.classfiles | This property specifies the list of paths where the Coverage engine can find the class files used to build the report. Multiple paths must be separated by the line feed character or by the current operating system path separator. <br><br>By default the report is built using the classes loaded by the runtime. |
| iscobol.coverage.html <span id="coverage_html"></span> | This property specifies the directory where the Coverage engine generates the HTML report. If the directory doesn’t exist, it’s automatically created. <br><br>The default value is "./htmlReport". <br><br>The default folder "./htmlReport" is created only if [iscobol.coverage.xml](#coverage_xml) is not set. |
| iscobol.coverage.sessionname | This property specifies the name of the session. This name appears on top of the index.html file of the HTML report. <br><br>The default value is the name of the main program as specified on the command-line. |
| iscobol.coverage.sourcefiles | This property specifies the list of paths where the Coverage engine can find the COBOL sources. Multiple paths must be separated by the line feed character or by the current operating system path separator. <br><br>The default value is "." (the current directory). |
| iscobol.coverage.xml <span id="coverage_xml"></span> | This property specifies the pathname of a file that will host the coverage report in XML format. <br><br>If this property is not set, then the XML report is not generated. If this property is set but [iscobol.coverage.html](#coverage_html) is not set, then only the XML report is generated. |

#### Unit Test Configuration

The following property must be set when the runtime starts and are evaluated if the [-iut](../Runtime-Framework/Runtime-Options#-iut) option is used.

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.unit_test.html <span id="unit_test_html"></span> | This property specifies the directory where the Unit Test engine generates the HTML report. If the directory doesn’t exist, it’s automatically created. <br><br>The default value is "./htmlReport". <br><br>The default folder "./htmlReport" is created only if [iscobol.unit_test.xml](#unit_test_xml) is not set. <br><br>If the Unit Test is performed along with Code Coverage, then this property is ignored and the report is generated in the folder specified by [iscobol.coverage.html](#coverage_html). |
| iscobol.unit_test.list_file | This property specifies the pathname of one or more text files that includes the list of programs to be included in the test.<br>Multiple pathnames must be separated by \n character sequence or by the current operating system path separator.<br>Within these files, each program must be indicated on a separate line.<br>The runtime takes care of normalizing the program name, stripping the extension, making it upper-case and replacing dashes with underscores, same as it happens on the runtime command line.<br>If this property points to an invalid file, like a file that doesn’t exist, then an empty report is generated. |
| iscobol.unit_test.xml <span id="unit_test_xml"></span> | This property specifies the pathname of a file that will host the coverage report in XML format. <br><br>If this property is not set, then the XML report is not generated. If this property is set but [iscobol.unit_test.html](#unit_test_html) is not set, then only the XML report is generated. |

#### Profiler Configuration

The following properties must be set when the runtime starts and are evaluated if the [-profile](../Runtime-Framework/Runtime-Options#-profile) option is used.

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.profiler.elapse_time | This property specifies the amount of seconds that the profiler will operate. After this timeout expires, the profiler activity terminates and the profiler report is generated, regardless if the runtime session is still in progress or not. <br><br>The default value is 0, that means "no timeout": the profiler activity will stop along with the runtime session. |
| iscobol.profiler.enable (boolean) <span id="profiler_enable"></span> | **True** = Automatically start profiling as soon as the runtime session starts.<br>**False** = Start profiling only after the [CPROF-ENABLE](../../../Appendices/Library-Routines/C$PROFILER/CPROF-ENABLE) function has been called. <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.profiler.excludes | This property specifies the list of COBOL classes that must not be analyzed by the profiler. Multiple values must be separated by comma. <br><br>Regular expressions can be used to specify a pattern, for example:<br>*iscobol.profiler.excludes=SAMPLE,GUI.\**<br>will exclude the SAMPLE class and all the COBOL classes whose name starts with "GUI". <br><br>It’s possible to exclude specific program paragraphs by specifying their name after the program name with a double colon separator, for example:<br>*iscobol.profiler.excludes=IO_INDEXED*<br>will exclude the whole IO_INDEXED program, while<br>*iscobol.profiler.excludes=IO_INDEXED::DELETE_FILE1_TEST,IO_INDEXED::UPDATE_FILE1_TEST*<br>will exclude only the DELETE_FILE1_TEST and UPDATE_FILE1_TEST paragraphs of the IO_INDEXED program.<br>Excluding specific paragraphs is supported only for standard programs (programs that start with a PROGRAM-ID). <br><br>**Note** - the time spent by excluded classes and paragraphs is counted in the time spent by their parent in the stack. <br><br>By default, all COBOL classes and their paragraphs are profiled. |
| iscobol.profiler.html <span id="profiler_html"></span> | This property specifies the directory where the profiler generates the HTML report. If the directory doesn’t exist, it’s automatically created. <br><br>The default value is "./hprofHtmlReport". <br><br>The default folder "./hprofHtmlReport" is created only if [iscobol.profiler.xml](#profiler_xml) is not set. |
| iscobol.profiler.includes <span id="profiler_includes"></span> | This property specifies the list of COBOL classes that must be analyzed by the profiler. Multiple values must be separated by comma. <br><br>Regular expressions can be used to specify a pattern, for example:<br>*iscobol.profiler.excludes=SAMPLE,GUI.\**<br>will include the SAMPLE class and all the COBOL classes whose name starts with "GUI". <br><br>It’s possible to include specific program paragraphs by specifying their name after the program name with a double colon separator, for example:<br>*iscobol.profiler.includes=IO_INDEXED*<br>will include the whole IO_INDEXED program, while*iscobol.profiler.includes=IO_INDEXED::DELETE_FILE1_TEST,IO_INDEXED::UPDATE_FILE1_TEST*<br>will include only the DELETE_FILE1_TEST and UPDATE_FILE1_TEST paragraphs of the IO_INDEXED program.<br>Including specific paragraphs is supported only for standard programs (programs that start with a PROGRAM-ID). <br><br>By default, all COBOL classes and their paragraphs are profiled. |
| iscobol.profiler.xml <span id="profiler_xml"></span> | This property specifies the pathname of a file that will host the profiler report in XML format. <br><br>If this property is not set, then the XML report is not generated. If this property is set but [iscobol.profiler.html](#profiler_html) is not set, then only the XML report is generated. |

#### Internal Objects Configuration

##### JSONSTREAM

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.jsonstream.allow_backslash_escaping_any_character (boolean) <span id="jsonstream_allow_backslash_escaping_any_character"></span> | **True** = When the [JSONStream Class (com.iscobol.rts.JSONStream)](../../../Appendices/Internal-Objects/JSONStream-Class/JSONStream-Class) reads a JSON stream, backslash is allowed before any character.<br>**False** = When the [JSONStream Class (com.iscobol.rts.JSONStream)](../../../Appendices/Internal-Objects/JSONStream-Class/JSONStream-Class) reads a JSON stream, backslash is allowed only in escapes: \b, \f, \n, \r, \t, \" and \\. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.jsonstream.indent_number | This property specifies the number of columns for the indentation of items in JSON files generated by the [JSONStream Class (com.iscobol.rts.JSONStream)](../../../Appendices/Internal-Objects/JSONStream-Class/JSONStream-Class). When this property is set to a value of 0 or grater, each element is generated on a separate line, meaning your stream will include CRLF. <br><br>The default value is -1, which disables indentation and results in a single line stream without CRLF. |
| iscobol.jsonstream.initialize_on_read (boolean) | **True** = the group item that will host the data read from JSON is automatically initialized before reading data from JSON.<br>**False** = the group item that will host the data read from JSON is not automatically initialized before reading data from JSON. You can initialize it with an [INITIALIZE](../../../Language-Reference/Procedure-Division-Statements/INITIALIZE) statement. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.jsonstream.omit_empty_elements (boolean) <span id="jsonstream_omit_empty_elements"></span> | **True** = empty elements are not generated by the [JSONStream Class (com.iscobol.rts.JSONStream)](../../../Appendices/Internal-Objects/JSONStream-Class/JSONStream-Class)<br>**False** = empty elements are generated by the [JSONStream Class (com.iscobol.rts.JSONStream)](../../../Appendices/Internal-Objects/JSONStream-Class/JSONStream-Class) <br><br>An element is considered empty when it’s alphanumeric and it’s 0 bytes in size. This condition can occur if the underlying data item is an initialized ANY LENGTH item or if the value has been trimmed by [iscobol.jsonstream.rtrim (boolean)](#jsonstream_rtrim). <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.jsonstream.rtrim (boolean) <span id="jsonstream_rtrim"></span> | **True** = remove trailing spaces from JSON items value<br>**False** = keep trailing spaces in JSON items value <br><br>The default value is False. <br><br>This property is evaluated before [iscobol.jsonstream.omit_empty_elements (boolean)](#jsonstream_omit_empty_elements). |

##### XMLSTREAM

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.xmlstream.indent_number | This property specifies the number of columns for the indentation of items in XML files generated by the [XMLStream Class (com.iscobol.rts.XMLStream)](../../../Appendices/Internal-Objects/XMLStream-Class/XMLStream-Class). When this property is set to a value of 0 or greater, each XML element is generated on a separate line, meaning your XML stream will include CRLF. <br><br>The default value is -1, which disables indentation and results in a single line XML stream without CRLF. |
| iscobol.xmlstream.initialize_on_read (boolean) | **True** = the group item that will host the data read from XML is automatically initialized before reading data from XML.<br>**False** = the group item that will host the data read from XML is not automatically initialized before reading data from XML. You can initialize it with an [INITIALIZE](../../../Language-Reference/Procedure-Division-Statements/INITIALIZE) statement. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.xmlstream.omit_empty_elements (boolean) <span id="xmlstream_omit_empty_elements"></span> | **True** = empty elements are not generated by the [XMLStream Class (com.iscobol.rts.XMLStream)](../../../Appendices/Internal-Objects/XMLStream-Class/XMLStream-Class)<br>**False** = empty elements are generated by the [XMLStream Class (com.iscobol.rts.XMLStream)](../../../Appendices/Internal-Objects/XMLStream-Class/XMLStream-Class) <br><br>An element is considered empty when it’s alphanumeric and it’s 0 bytes in size. This condition can occur if the underlying data item is an initialized ANY LENGTH item or if the value has been trimmed by [iscobol.xmlstream.rtrim (boolean)](#xmlstream_rtrim). <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.xmlstream.resolve_references (boolean) | **True** = resolve href/id references in the read XML stream<br>**False** = don’t resolve href/id references in the read XML stream <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.xmlstream.rtrim (boolean) <span id="xmlstream_rtrim"></span> | **True** = remove trailing spaces from XML values<br>**False** = keep trailing spaces in XML values <br><br>The default value is False. <br><br>This property is evaluated before [iscobol.xmlstream.omit_empty_elements (boolean)](#xmlstream_omit_empty_elements). |

#### Keyboard Configuration

(\*) The asterisk after the property name means that the property is static, it’s inquired only once by the Framework and then changing it using the SET ENVIRONMENT statement has no effect.

| Property | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| iscobol.kbd_auto_return | When set to a non-zero value, this property specifies the termination value that is stored into crt status when ACCEPT terminates automatically due to a AUTO-TERMINATE clause. <br><br>The default value is 0. <br><br>(iscobol.keyboard.kbd_auto_return is still supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.key.accepted_control_characters | Specifies which control characters should be accepted and displayed by Entry-Field, Combo-Box and Grid as well as character-based input fields.<br>A control character or non-printing character (NPC) is a code point in a character set, that does not represent a written symbol. Control characters are used as signaling to cause effects other than the addition of a symbol to the text. <br><br>Set this property to the list of control characters using hex notation.<br>For example, in order to allow 0x1D and 0x1E to be inserted in an Entry-Field, Combo-Box or Grid, set the property as follows in the configuration file: <br><br>*iscobol.key.accepted_control_characters=\u001d\u001e* <br><br>The property can also be set dynamically by COBOL programs with this syntax: <br><br>*set environment "key.accepted_control_characters" to x"1d1e"* <br><br>Note - The control characters are usually discarded because it is assumed that they are already used for different actions than displaying a character in an input field. For example, the character 0x08 (backspace) deletes a character in the text component. Adding 0x08 to the list of accepted control characters would cause the backspace to display an odd symbol in the field instead of deleting a character. Use this property carefully in order to avoid side effects. |
| iscobol.key.default_shortcuts_enabled (boolean) * <span id="key_default_shortcuts_enabled"></span> | **True** = Copy/Cut/Paste/Undo/Redo (Ctrl+C/X/V/Z/Y) actions are enabled on all controls, so their exception codes can’t be caught by the program.<br>**False** = Copy/Cut/Paste/Undo/Redo (Ctrl+C/X/V/Z/Y) actions are disabled and their exception codes can be caught by the program. <br><br>When the property is set to False, the Copy/Cut/Paste/Undo/Redo (Ctrl+C/X/V/Z/Y) actions can be enabled using this code: <br><br>SET ENVIRONMENT "KEYSTROKE" TO "Exception=101 ^C".<br>SET ENVIRONMENT "KEYSTROKE" TO "Exception=102 ^X".<br>SET ENVIRONMENT "KEYSTROKE" TO "Exception=103 ^V".<br>SET ENVIRONMENT "KEYSTROKE" TO "Exception=104 ^Z".<br>SET EXCEPTION 101 TO copy-selection<br>SET EXCEPTION 102 TO cut-selection<br>SET EXCEPTION 103 TO paste-selection<br>SET EXCEPTION 104 TO UNDO <br><br>However, they affect only the Entry-Field and Combo-Box controls in this case. <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.key.*KeyName* | This property specifies the keyboard configuration. See below for further details. |
| iscobol.key.\*f1.system (boolean) | **True** = The keystroke CTRL+F1 is not intercepted by the isCOBOL Framework and affects the COBOL window directly.<br>**False** = The keystroke CTRL+F1 is intercepted by the isCOBOL Framework and doesn’t affect the COBOL window, but it can be handled by the COBOL program. <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.key.@f4.system (boolean) | **True** = The keystroke ALT+F4 is not intercepted by the isCOBOL Framework and affects the COBOL window directly.<br>**False** = The keystroke ALT+F4 is intercepted by the isCOBOL Framework and doesn’t affect the COBOL window, but it can be handled by the COBOL program. <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.key.f4.system (boolean) <span id="keyf4_system"></span> | **True** = If the focus is either on a combo-box or on a paged list-box, then the F4 key produces a system action (e.g. drop the combo-box or trigger the list-box search respectively) and doesn’t generate an exception in the current ACCEPT.<br>**False** = The key F4 always generates an exception in the current ACCEPT. <br><br>The default value is True. <br><br>(iscobol.gui.f4_drops_combobox is still supported for backward compatibility) |
| iscobol.key.f10.system (boolean) <span id="keyf10_system"></span> | **True** = If the window where the current ACCEPT is performed includes a menu bar, pressing F10 activates the menu bar.<br>**False** = The key F10 always generates an exception in the current ACCEPT. <br><br>The default value is True. |
| iscobol.keystroke.firstlast_on_screen (boolean) * <span id="keystroke_firstlast_on_screen"></span> | **True** = *edit=first* and *edit=last* allow to move the cursor to the first or last field of the screen respectively.<br>**False** = *edit=first* and *edit=last* have no effect. <br><br>The default value is False. |
| iscobol.keystroke.updown_like_prevnext (boolean) * | **True** = *edit=up* has the same effect of *edit=previous* and *edit=down* has the same effect of edit=next in key settings.<br>**False** = key settings preserve their behavior. <br><br>The default value is False. <br><br>This property affects the ACCEPT of user input on both character based and GUI screens. When set to true, the [No-Group-Tab](../../../User-Interface/Controls-Reference/RADIO-BUTTON/Styles/No-Group-Tab) style is assumed for every [RADIO-BUTTON](../../../User-Interface/Controls-Reference/RADIO-BUTTON/RADIO-BUTTON). |

The characters "^", "\*" and "@" represent the \[Shift\], \[Ctrl\] and \[Alt\] key, respectively. Put one or more of them after the key name to define key combinations.

For example, \[F1\] is iscobol.key.f1, \[Shift+F1\] is iscobol.key.^f1, \[Ctrl+F1\] is iscobol.key.\*f1 and \[Alt+F1\] is iscobol.key.@f1. Any combination of "^", "\*" and "@" is valid, \[Shift+Ctrl+Alt+F1\] is iscobol.key.^\*@f1.

Letter and number keys can be configured using properties in the form iscobol.key.\# (where # is a letter from 'a' to 'z' or a number from '0' to '9'), and they can be configured in conjunction with Alt, Ctrl or Shift. For example:

- setting iscobol.key.a allows you to configure the keystroke \[A\]
- setting iscobol.key.\*a allows you to configure the keystroke \[Ctrl+A\]
- setting iscobol.key.@a allows you to configure the keystroke \[Alt+A\]
- setting iscobol.key.^a allows you to configure the keystroke \[Sift+A\]
- setting iscobol.key.^\*@a allows you to configure the keystroke \[Shift+Ctrl+Alt+A\]

**Note** - iscobol.key.@ followed by a number from '0' to '9' assigns an exception value that terminates the accept only when the number was entered from the standard keyboard, not from the numpad. This separates these key combinations from the Alt+ combinations used to enter special characters.

**Note** - Keystrokes starting with "iscobol.key.@" have effect only if they don’t conflict with GUI controls’ mnemonics. When the user presses Alt+, the runtime activates the first applicable option in this order:

1. a button (Check-Box, Push-Button or Radio-Button) whose title includes "&<letter\>",
2. a Label whose title includes "&<letter\>",
3. a menu item whose title includes "&<letter\>";

if none of the above is found, then the runtime executes the action associated to the iscobol.key.@<letter\> setting.

Pressing either the "Ctrl" key or the "Shift+Ctrl" key combination in conjunction with a key letter (A-Z) causes an exception whose number varies from 1 (if the letter is A) to 26 (if the letter is Z) by default. The behavior of these key combinations can be redefined using the properties iscobol.key.\*a through iscobol.key.\*z and iscobol.key.^\*a through iscobol.key.^\*z. However, this kind of keystroke might be intercepted by the current graphical control and won't cause an exception. For example, when the focus is on an [ENTRY-FIELD](../../../User-Interface/Controls-Reference/ENTRY-FIELD/ENTRY-FIELD), pressing Ctrl+B causes an exception whose number is 2, while pressing Ctrl+C doesn’t cause an exception whose number is 3, it copies the selected text to the clipboard instead. To make all Ctrl+letter combinations cause an exception and lose copy, cut, paste, undo, redo functions on GUI controls, set [iscobol.key.default_shortcuts_enabled (boolean) \*](#key_default_shortcuts_enabled) to false in the configuration.

iscobol.key.*KeyName* can be set to one or more of the following values:

| | |
| --- | --- |
| data=*Value* | *Value* represents the character sent to the program. |
| exception=*Value* | *Value* is a numeric value from 1 to 65536 representing the exception value generated for the program. |
| termination=*Value* | *Value* is a numeric value from 1 to 65536 representing the termination value generated for the program. |
| hotkey=*Value* | *Value* is the program to be called. |
| edit=*Value* | *Value* represents how the cursor is moved within controls belonging to the same Screen Section. Possible values are: <br><br>**next** = the cursor goes to the next control<br>**previous** = the cursor goes to the previous control<br>**first** = the cursor goes to the first control [A]<br>**last** = the cursor goes to the last control [A]<br>**up** = the cursor goes to the nearest control above the current one<br>**down** = the cursor goes to the nearest control below the current one<br>**pageup** = the cursor moves as sif Page-Up was pressed<br>**pagedown** = the cursor moves as if Page-Down was pressed<br>**backspace** = the cursor moves back by one digit deleting the character<br>**delete** = the digit next to the cursor is deleted<br>**insert** = the insert mode is changed<br>**clear** = the field content is erased and the cursor is placed at the beginning of the field.<br>**cl2end** = the content from the current cursor position to the end of the field is erased<br>**erase-all** = all fields controlled by the ACCEPT statement are erased and the cursor is moved to the home position of the first field. This value should not be used in conjunction with exception or *termination*. |
| search=*Context* | *Context* represents where the keystroke will trigger a search. Possible values are: <br><br>**grid** = the Grid control<br>**print-preview** = the Print Preview dialog<br>**tree-view** = the Tree-View control<br>**web-browser** = the Web-Browser control <br><br>You can specify one or more contexts, separated by comma. <br><br>By default, the following setting is active: *iscobol.key.*f=search=grid,list-box,print-preview,tree-view,web-browser*, so the search is always triggered by Ctrl-F.<br>Let’s assume for example that you don't want the search with Ctrl-F on Grid and you want the search with Alt-F on the print preview; then you would set<br>*iscobol.key.*f=search=list-box,tree-view,web-browser*<br>*iscobol.key.@f=search=print-preview* |
| | |

\[A\] Supported only if [iscobol.keystroke.firstlast_on_screen (boolean)\*](#keystroke_firstlast_on_screen) is set to true.

**Note** - Function and special keys cannot be configured to send characters to the program, the data= setting has no effect for them.

**Note** - On graphical screens, if the active graphical control intercepts some keys for its own functionality, these keys are not returned to the COBOL program and therefore they don’t raise any exception. This is the reason why for example F5 and ESC are not intercepted by the COBOL program while the focus is on a web-browser.

\[A\]With the default configuration *key.up* and *key.down* behaves as follows: if there are fields below the current cursor location, the cursor moves to the one on the closest lower line. If there is more than one field on that line, the cursor moves to the one closest to its current horizontal location. The cursor will try to stay in the same column. If there are no fields below the current line, then no action is taken unless an EXCEPTION or TERMINATION value has been assigned, in this case *key.up* and *key.down* act as termination keys.

#### Acucobol-GT key codes

The Acucobol-GT KEYSTROKE configuration property is supported for compatibility. You can set this property in the program using a Format 6 [SET](../../../Language-Reference/Procedure-Division-Statements/SET) statement or using the [C$KEYSTROKE](../../../Appendices/Library-Routines/C$KEYSTROKE) routine. Setting the property in a configuration file has no effect.

The following key codes are supported:

| Key Code | Description |
| --- | --- |
| ZB | Backspace |
| 8 | Backspace |
| 9 | Tab |
| 13 | Enter |
| 27 | Escape |
| 127 | Delete |
| ^M | Enter |
| ^H | Backspace |
| ^I | Tab |
| ^[ | Escape |
| ^A - ^Z | Ctrl+A - Ctrl+Z |
| A0 - A9 | Ctrl+0 - Ctrl+9 |
| k1 - k10 | F1 - F10 |
| kd | Down |
| kh | Home |
| kl | Left |
| kr | Right |
| ku | Up |
| kA | Insert |
| kB | Shift+Tab |
| kE | Ctrl+End |
| kL | Ctrl+Delete |
| kN | Pag Down |
| kP | Pag Up |
| K1 - K0 | Shift+F1 - Shift+F10 |
| KB | Ctrl+Pag Down |
| KC | Ctrl + Home |
| KE | End |
| KI | Insert |
| KT | Ctrl+Pag Up |
| KX | Delete |
| Kd | Ctrl+Down |
| Kl | Ctrl+Left |
| Kr | Ctrl+Right |
| Ku | Ctrl+Up |
| K? | Help |
| S1 - S0 | Shift+Ctrl+F1 - Shift+Ctrl+F10 |
| U1 - U2 | F11 - F12 |
| U3 - U4 | Shift+F11 - Shift+F12 |
| U5 - U6 | Ctrl+F11 - Ctrl+F12 |
| U7 - U8 | Alt+F11 - Alt+F12 |
| U9 - U0 | Shift+Ctrl+F11 - Shift+Ctrl+F12 |
| Mv | mouse moved |
| Ml | left mouse button down |
| ML | left mouse button up |
| M1 | left mouse button double clicked |
| Mm | middle mouse button down |
| MM | middle mouse button up |
| M2 | middle mouse button double clicked |
| Mr | right mouse button down |
| MR | right mouse button up |
| M3 | right mouse button double clicked |

For example, in isCOBOL you can assign an exception value of 100 the the Backspace key in two ways:

- Standard isCOBOL way

```cobol
set environment "key.backspace" to "exception=100"
```

- Acucobol compatible way

```cobol
set environment "keystroke" to "exception=100 ZB"
```
